# EFTCAMB audit findings

Code under audit: branch `eftcamb-audit` at 5bef49a (EFTCAMB on CAMB v2). Generated by `fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/checks/compile_findings.py` from the audit workflow results; every S1/S2 finding has a reproducer under `fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/`.

**Counts (excluding refuted):** S1: 21, S2: 63, S3: 58, not-a-bug: 0

## Details

### F-001 [S1, confirmed] Re-using CAMBdata parameters (results.Params, its copy, or results.get_BAO(z)) aborts the process: allocate of already allocated eft_par_cache arrays

- **Location:** `fortran/results.f90:580` (also: camb/results.py:306 (get_BAO default params=None -> self.Params.copy()), fortran/eftcamb/03_abstract_EFTCAMB_cache.f90:1036 (initialize does not reset grhormass/nu_masses))
- **Track:** t0-layout; reported severity S1, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/eft-par-cache-realloc-crash.py`
- **Observed:** Designer f(R) (4_Des_F_R_LCDM_1), A0: results.get_BAO([0.5,1.0]), camb.get_background(results.Params) and camb.get_background(results.Params.copy()) all end the Python process with 'At line 580 of file .../fortran/results.f90 Fortran runtime error: Attempting to allocate already allocated variable this' (rc=2). The same calls with EFTflag=0 return normally (H=[90.075,119.886]).
- **Expected:** rc=0 and the same background as a fresh run: H(z=0.5,1) = [91.30015365594554, 122.58638055999107].
- **Root cause:** CAMBdata_SetParams copies the input into this%CP (this%CP = P, line 390). A P that came from a CAMBdata carries an eft_par_cache whose grhormass and nu_masses are already allocated. TEFTCAMB_parameter_cache%initialize (03_abstract_EFTCAMB_cache.f90:1036) does not deallocate them, and line 580 allocates them unconditionally. CAMB v2 changed get_BAO(redshifts, params=None) to use self.Params.copy() (camb/results.py:306), so the standard API call now reaches this latent bug for every EFT model. In B1, params was a required argument.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** In fortran/results.f90, before line 580 add:
+            if (allocated(this%CP%eft_par_cache%grhormass)) deallocate(this%CP%eft_par_cache%grhormass)
+            if (allocated(this%CP%eft_par_cache%nu_masses)) deallocate(this%CP%eft_par_cache%nu_masses)
             allocate(this%CP%eft_par_cache%grhormass(max_nu), this%CP%eft_par_cache%nu_masses(max_nu))
Alternatively, deallocate both arrays in EFTCAMBParameterCacheInit. Tested in work/t0-layout/fix (diff: work/t0-layout/fix_results.diff): all three scenarios return H=[91.30015365594554, 122.58638055999107]. Cls/P(k)/EFT outputs are bit-identical to A0 for 4_Des_F_R_LCDM_1, 5_Kmouflage_1 and 2_PEFT_Omega_lin_1.
- **Verifier (physics):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; I reproduced the crash and could not explain it away. The expected behaviour is the right one. CAMB v2 documents get_BAO(redshifts, params=None) as using the current Params by default (camb/results.py:306 does `(params or self.Params).copy()`). Plain upstream CAMB (B0) and A0 with EFTflag=0 both return normally on this call. For every EFT model, A0 instead stops with a gfortran runtime error. This error is always on, even in the -O3 release build, so it is not a debug-only check. Python cannot catch it with try/except, so the interpreter exits (an MCMC chain or notebook dies with it).

The root cause matches the code:
- CAMBdata_SetParams does `this%CP = P` (results.f90:390). This deep-copies P%eft_par_cache. CAMBparams.copy() goes through Fortran _new_instance(source=...), so it is a deep copy, not a memmove.
- EFTCAMBParameterCacheInit (03_abstract_EFTCAMB_cache.f90:1036-1061) never deallocates grhormass or nu_masses.
- results.f90:580 allocates them unconditionally inside the `.not. calling_again` block.

So only a P that already went through an EFT SetParams triggers it: results.Params, a copy of it, or the get_BAO default. A user's own CAMBparams has these arrays unallocated, which is why user_cp_twice and get_BAO(z, user_params) work. The internal call_again path skips the EFT block, and the tensor-then-scalar path re-assigns this%CP from an unallocated P (EFTCAMB MOD at camb.f90:133), so normal get_results is safe.

The bug was already latent in B1 (pre-rebase, results.f90:535). It crashes there for get_background(res.Params), res.Params.copy(), calc_background(res.Params) and get_BAO(z, res.Params). B1's get_BAO had a required params argument and raised TypeError when it was omitted. The CAMB v2 API change turned this latent bug into a crash on the default call.

No physics convention, units or accuracy question applies. get_BAO column 1 is H in km/s/Mpc, and with the fix it is bit-identical to the fresh-run hubble_parameter.

Severity S1 is correct under the rubric: it is a crash reachable with valid input through a documented default API call, and it hits all 72 EFT test models.

On the proposed fix: the 2-line deallocate fix alone is enough. Note that the reporter's work/t0-layout/fix_results.diff also bundles an unrelated EFTCAMB-model-not-initialized guard. The alternative fix (deallocate inside EFTCAMBParameterCacheInit) also works. Classifying the fix as safe-local is justified.

Side observation, not part of this finding: res.calc_power_spectra(res.Params) gives tensor-only total TT both with EFT plus the fix and in GR on A0 and B1. This comes from upstream CAMB Params/State%CP aliasing in CAMB_GetResults, not from EFT.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; The bug is real, it is in EFTCAMB code, and the stated root cause is correct. I ran the reporter's repro unchanged on A0. All three EFT scenarios (get_BAO([0.5,1]), get_background(results.Params), get_background(results.Params.copy())) abort with rc=2 at results.f90:580, 'Attempting to allocate already allocated variable'. The GR controls return normally.

I also wrote my own check (checks/verify/eft_par_cache_realloc_verify.py and verify_par_cache_realloc.py). It tests different API paths: calc_background(res.Params) on the same CAMBdata, camb.get_results(res.Params.copy()) through the full pipeline, and get_BAO(z, res.Params). Its controls run the same cosmology with EFTflag=0 and reuse the user's own CAMBparams. It covers three model families, a sweep of all 73 test inis, the AD (-fcheck, -g) tree, B1, and two fix variants. Every path that hands a CAMBdata-owned CAMBparams back to calc_background crashes for every EFT model. Every path that uses the user's own params works.

The cause, read in the source:
- this%CP = P (results.f90:390) deep-copies eft_par_cache, including grhormass and nu_masses if they are already allocated.
- EFTCAMBParameterCacheInit (03_abstract_EFTCAMB_cache.f90:1036-1063) zeroes only the scalar fields.
- Line 580 then allocates both arrays unconditionally. It sits inside the 'EFTCAMB MOD START' block that begins at results.f90:549, and upstream camb 1d1d3d6 has no eft_par_cache at all, so the defect belongs to EFTCAMB, not upstream CAMB.

The bug was already present before the rebase: on B1, results.Params reuse crashes the same way. But get_BAO(z, params) required params in B1 (git show 1db4141), whereas upstream CAMB v2 made it optional and defaults to self.Params.copy() (camb/results.py:298,306). So a documented standard API call now crashes for every EFT model. That is a crash on valid input in supported models, which is S1.

The one AD failure that looked different has a known cause. GR get_BAO_default on AD stops at line 2168 ('Allocatable argument this is not allocated'), which is F-002, not this bug. The proposed fix, and the alternative of deallocating inside EFTCAMBParameterCacheInit, both remove the crash on all models and leave fresh-run outputs bit-identical. fix_class safe-local is correct.

### F-002 [S1, confirmed] pickle / copy.deepcopy / repr+eval of CAMBparams with EFTCAMB drop the Fortran EFTCAMB model; get_results then segfaults

- **Location:** `camb/eftcamb.py:61` (also: camb/model.py:444 (CAMBparams has no EFT-aware __getstate__/__setstate__), fortran/results.f90:584 (unguarded model%initialize_background), fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_main.f90:139-147 (unmapped components))
- **Track:** t0-layout; reported severity S1, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/eft-pickle-repr-loses-model.py`
- **Observed:** Designer f(R), A0. Reference and cp.copy() give H=[91.30015365594554,122.58638055999107]. pickle.loads(pickle.dumps(cp)), copy.deepcopy(cp) and eval(repr(cp)) restore EFTflag=3 with model_name '' (model unallocated), and camb.get_background crashes with SIGSEGV (signal 11). Setting pars.EFTCAMB.EFTflag=3 from python without initialize_parameters also gives SIGSEGV. B1 behaves the same (rc=139).
- **Expected:** The round trip reproduces the reference H(z). The CAMBparams docstring says it can be saved and restored with repr/eval or pickle, and multiprocessing pickles parameters. A TEFTCAMB without a model should raise a CAMBError, not crash.
- **Root cause:** The python EFTCAMB class maps only the 33 flag components of TEFTCAMB (168 of 328 bytes). outroot, model and read_param_keys/values are unmapped, so CAMB_Structure.__getstate__ saves only the flags. On restore the TEFTCAMB has EFTflag/=0 but no model, and CAMBdata_SetParams dereferences this%CP%EFTCAMB%model (results.f90:584, initialize_background) without checking allocated().
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** (1) Python, camb/model.py CAMBparams: in __getstate__, pop 'EFTCAMB_parameter_cache' (SetParams refills it) and, when EFTflag/=0, store EFTCAMB.read_parameters() with bytes decoded. In __setstate__, after super().__setstate__, call self.EFTCAMB.initialize_parameters(self, saved, print_header=False), then re-apply the saved python EFTCAMB fields (diff: work/t0-layout/fix_model_py.diff). (2) Fortran, results.f90 before the EFTCAMB block at line 550: if (EFTflag /= 0 .and. .not. allocated(this%CP%EFTCAMB%model)) call GlobalError('EFTCAMB: EFTflag /= 0 but no EFTCAMB model is initialized ...', error_unsupported_params); set error; return. Tested in work/t0-layout/fix: pickle, deepcopy and repr_eval give H=[91.30015365594554,122.58638055999107], and the flag-only case raises CAMBError. Outputs are bit-identical to A0 for 3 models.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; I reproduced the finding and could not explain it away. The defect is in the code under audit: the python EFTCAMB class (camb/eftcamb.py:61 onward) maps only the leading flag components of TEFTCAMB. The Fortran type even says "Keep these components last: the python class maps the components above", so the model, outroot and read_param_keys/values never reach CAMB_Structure.__getstate__. CAMBparams (camb/model.py) adds no EFT-aware __getstate__/__setstate__. A restored object therefore has EFTflag/=0 with no model, and CAMBdata_SetParams dereferences this%CP%EFTCAMB%model at fortran/results.f90:584 without checking allocated(). The -fcheck tree (AD) names that exact line. Upstream CAMB is not involved: with EFTflag=0 the pickle round trip is bit-identical. The bug was already present before the rebase (B1 also segfaults). It is not a test artifact. The crash happens without calling model_name() (no F-001 getter) and through paths different from the repro. Re-initialising the model after unpickling restores bit-identical results, which shows the Fortran model is the only state lost. Severity stays S1 under the brief's rubric: the CAMBparams docstring (model.py:301) documents pickle and repr/eval as supported, and multiprocessing pools pickle their arguments. Any ProcessPoolExecutor use with an EFT model kills the worker with SIGSEGV. One could argue S2, since this is an API path rather than a parameter region. Corrections: (1) The given repro does not exercise B1. B1's read_ini ignores EFT keys, so every B1 mode runs with EFTflag=0 and returns rc=0, and python_flag_only gives rc=1, not 139. B1 does crash once EFTCAMB is set up with initialize_parameters (my check). (2) The python_flag_only case (setting EFTflag without initialize_parameters) is misuse, S3 on its own; the pickle, deepcopy and repr paths carry the S1. (3) Other unguarded model dereferences exist (results.f90:801, 875, 1043, 1136 and equations.f90), but SetParams runs first, so the single guard proposed at line ~550 covers them. Note on the fix: the python half depends on EFTCAMB.read_parameters(), one of the len=200 getters affected by F-001. It works on release builds but would trip -fcheck builds until F-001 is fixed. The Fortran guard alone only turns the crash into a CAMBError.
- **Verifier (physics):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; The expected behaviour is correct. Upstream CAMB says in the CAMBparams docstring (camb/model.py:301) that a CAMBparams can be saved and restored with repr/eval or pickle, and camb_test.testSave checks this. multiprocessing and ProcessPoolExecutor pickle their arguments under both spawn and fork. EFTCAMB's documented Python entry points (camb.set_params with EFT keys, read_ini, EFTCAMB.initialize_parameters) produce exactly the parameters that break.

I reproduced the crash on A0 through four independent routes, for every EFT model I tried, using EFT-sensitive observables:
- pickle, deepcopy and repr/eval on params built by read_ini;
- params built with set_params plus initialize_parameters, then get_results or CAMBdata.calc_background_no_thermo;
- a ProcessPoolExecutor (spawn and fork).

None of the usual explanations holds:
- GR controls (EFTflag=0) round-trip bit-identically, so the harness and scripts are fine.
- All 33 Python-mapped flag fields are restored identically. What is lost is the unmapped tail of TEFTCAMB: 20 non-zero 8-byte words after byte 168 shrink to one, which is the default vptr.
- Re-initialising after the restore brings back the reference results exactly.
- It is not noise or a convention difference: the run is a hard SIGSEGV.
- B1 behaves the same, so this is a long-standing EFTCAMB defect, not a regression from the rebase.

The root cause is right in substance: the unmapped components are dropped, and SetParams uses an unallocated model without checking. One detail is wrong. The crash is not at results.f90:584. initialize_background has a concrete default in the abstract type (06_abstract_EFTCAMB_model.f90:357-370 only sets success=.true.), and gfortran points an unallocated class allocatable at the declared type's vtable, so that call returns normally. lldb shows the SIGSEGV is a jump to address 0 at the first deferred-binding call, in __eftcamb_returntogr_MOD_eftcambreturntogr_functions+808, called from cambdata_setparams.

The proposed Fortran guard sits before both call sites, so it still covers the crash. I tested the full proposed fix in the t0-layout fix tree: it restores all observables bit-identically, and the flag-only case raises CAMBError.

S1 matches the rubric: a crash, with memory corruption, reached through documented-valid API use (pickle, deepcopy, repr, multiprocessing) for every EFTflag/=0 model. It could be argued down to S2, since core single-process runs are unaffected and a workaround exists (call initialize_parameters again after restoring). Setting EFTflag from Python without initialize_parameters is a misuse; on its own it would be S3, but it crashes at the same place.

### F-003 [S1, confirmed] K-mimic models: an isolated wrong P(k) mode (factor ~3) at k = 0.001/tau(turn-on) ~ 0.22/Mpc at every accuracy, and Cls not converged (30-84%); a consequence of the t5 type-1 pi' IC

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:301` (also: fortran/equations.f90:521 (EFT switch; k-dependent IC time: at mode start for k < k*, after GR evolution at tau_on for k > k*), fortran/cmbmain.f90:777 (GetTauStart = 0.001/k))
- **Track:** t10b-convergence; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/kmimic-pk-single-mode-glitch.py`
- **Observed:** Repro (A0, windows off): single-point deviation of log P from its neighbours for 5_Kmimic_1 is +1.136 at k=0.2198 (default) and -1.133 at k=0.2161 (boost2); GR gives -9.2e-3. TT default/boost2 differs by -7.9e-2 at ell 546. Sweep, default vs boost3: Kmimic_1 TT 31%@197, EE 84%@198, P(k) 155%, sigma8 5.9e-3; Kmimic_2 TT 12%@572, P(k) 101%; Kmimic_3 TT 2.6%, P(k) 22%. The same glitch is in B1 (pre-rebase). With t5's IC tree (EFT_T5_PIDOT0=1) the glitch metric is -8.9e-3 and default vs boost3 is at the GR level (unl_TT 9.5e-4); sigma8 changes 0.775 -> 0.616, so A0 is on a different branch overall.
- **Expected:** Smooth P(k) (deviation ~1e-2 as in GR) and convergence at the GR level.
- **Root cause:** The type-1 initial pi' (finite differences of coefficients that vary by orders of magnitude at a~1e-8, filed by t5-robust as ic1-pidot-transient-noisy-coefficients) is ill-conditioned for K-mimic. The time at which the IC is imposed depends on k: for k < k* = 0.001/tau_on it is imposed at the mode start, for k > k* after a short GR phase at tau_on. Modes next to k* land on a different pi branch. Which transfer-grid point sits near k* changes with accuracy_boost, so P(k) and the Cls cannot converge. get_scale_evolution shows the branch also depends on the output grid and on model_background_num_points.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** Fix the type-1 IC as proposed by t5: pi' consistent with the attractor or set to 0, plus the EFTpiD2 typo fix. This was validated here: the glitch disappears and Kmimic_1/2/3 converge to the GR level. Additionally, impose EFT ICs at a k-independent time (always at tau_on) once the IC is well conditioned. Forcing that with the current IC (AUDIT_T10B_ICTIME) makes default blow up, which shows the IC must be fixed first.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; I reproduced the repro output exactly. The finding misdescribes the effect, though: it is not one isolated wrong mode but a step. Every P(k) mode above k* = 0.001/tau_on (about 0.217/Mpc) sits on a different branch, 2 to 9 times the GR power, while modes below k* sit at about 0.95 of GR. The +1.13 and -1.13 "glitch" values are just the second-difference metric's response to a step. With dense k sampling the step stays sharp, at the same two points.

Three things confirm the attribution:
- **It follows the turn-on time.** Moving EFTCAMB_turn_on_time from 1e-8 to 1e-7 moves the step from k = 0.22 to k = 0.022, as k* ∝ 1/tau_on predicts.
- **The code path matches.** In equations.f90:391-404 and 521-528, when taustart(k) = 0.001/k is later than EFTturnOnTime (so k < k*), the switch fires at once and the EFT IC is imposed at mode start. For k > k*, GR evolves until tau_on and the type-1 IC (09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:301-307) is imposed there. The finding states this correctly.
- **Fixing the IC removes it.** With t5's fixed-IC tree (pi'_ini = 0) P(k) is continuous across k* and close to GR.

Because the whole k > k* range is wrong, not one transfer point, this is wrong physics in shipped test models: all three of Kmimic_1/2/3, in A0 and identically in B1. That is S1, not S2. The non-convergence in accuracy_boost happens because the discontinuity is sampled differently at each accuracy. The root cause is right, but the "isolated mode" headline and the S2 rating are not. Origin: EFTCAMB code (also present before the rebase), not upstream CAMB and not the test.

### F-004 [S1, confirmed] Reusing results.Params (e.g. results.get_BAO, camb.get_background(results.Params)) aborts Python for every EFT model

- **Location:** `fortran/results.f90:580` (also: same defect also filed by another track as repro/eft-par-cache-realloc-crash.py)
- **Track:** t2-state; reported severity S1, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/eft-par-cache-realloc-abort.py A0`
- **Observed:** On A0, EFTflag=1 (pure EFT, EFTOmega0=0.1, mnu=0.06): the first get_background gives H(z=1)=119.885653414662. Then results.get_BAO([0.5,1]) or camb.get_background(results.Params) exits with code 2: 'At line 580 of results.f90: Fortran runtime error: Attempting to allocate already allocated variable'. The same calls with EFTflag=0 return H(z=1)=119.885653414662. In t2_fixcheck all 11 models tested abort.
- **Expected:** Second calculation returns the same H(z=1) (exit code 0), as in GR.
- **Root cause:** CAMBdata_SetParams copies P into this%CP. Once State%CP has been through an EFT calculation, its eft_par_cache%grhormass and nu_masses are allocated, so a copy of results.Params brings them along. eft_par_cache%initialize() does not deallocate them, and the unconditional allocate at results.f90:580 then stops the program. CAMB v2's results.get_BAO defaults to self.Params.copy(), so a standard API call kills the interpreter.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** In results.f90 before line 580 (or inside TEFTCAMB_parameter_cache%initialize):
+            if (allocated(this%CP%eft_par_cache%grhormass)) deallocate(this%CP%eft_par_cache%grhormass)
+            if (allocated(this%CP%eft_par_cache%nu_masses)) deallocate(this%CP%eft_par_cache%nu_masses)
             allocate(this%CP%eft_par_cache%grhormass(max_nu), this%CP%eft_par_cache%nu_masses(max_nu))
Tested in work/t2-state/fix: get_BAO H = [90.07516532, 119.88565341] and reuse gives 119.885653414662 (identical); spectra unchanged.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; I ran the repro on A0 and it fails exactly as reported. Both EFTflag=0 cases exit 0. Both EFTflag=1 cases (get_BAO and get_background(results.Params)) exit with code 2 at A0 fortran/results.f90:580 with "Attempting to allocate already allocated variable". I then wrote my own check, fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/checks/verify/par_cache_realloc_indep.py, which uses the test-suite inis (2_PEFT_Omega_lin_1, EFTflag=1; 4_Des_F_R_LCDM_1, EFTflag=3) loaded with camb.read_ini instead of set_params, and two API paths the repro does not use: CAMBdata().calc_background(results.Params.copy()) and get_background(results.Params.copy()). Both paths abort at the same line for both models. Calling get_background a second time with the user's original pars works, because State%CP is a copy and the user's CAMBparams never gets an allocated cache. Only a parameter object that came from results.Params carries the allocated eft_par_cache%grhormass/nu_masses, which matches the stated root cause. EFTCAMB%initialize() in fortran/eftcamb/03_abstract_EFTCAMB_cache.f90 contains no deallocate. The allocate is EFTCAMB code inside an EFTCAMB MOD block, so the defect is in the code under audit and not upstream CAMB. The pre-rebase source has the same allocate (eftcamb-pre-rebase results.f90:535). The crash becomes reachable through a default API call because CAMB v2's results.get_BAO (camb/results.py:306) does P = (params or self.Params).copy(). Any standard call like this kills the Python interpreter for every EFT model, so S1 under the brief's definition (a crash reachable with valid input) is correct. I did not test the proposed fix (deallocate if allocated before line 580, or inside the cache initialize). It is the obvious correct fix and is safe-local.
- **Verifier (physics):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; I reproduced the abort on A0. With EFTflag=1, both results.get_BAO([0.5,1]) and camb.get_background(results.Params) stop the Python process with exit code 2: "At line 580 of results.f90: Fortran runtime error: Attempting to allocate already allocated variable". With EFTflag=0 the same calls return the same H.

The root cause is as reported. In fortran/results.f90, CAMBdata_SetParams copies P into this%CP. If P is a copy of a results' State%CP that has already been through an EFT run, its eft_par_cache%grhormass and nu_masses arrive already allocated. eft_par_cache%initialize() (line 558) does not deallocate them, so the unconditional allocate at line 580 fails.

This is reachable through the documented CAMB API with valid input. In camb/results.py:306, get_BAO uses (params or self.Params).copy() by default.

Physics lens: the expected value is right. Pure EFT with EFTwDE=0 (w=-1) and EFTOmega0=0.1 has a ΛCDM background, so H(z=1) should equal the GR value of 119.885653414662. That is exactly what the fixed tree returns. This is not a convention difference, accuracy noise or intended behaviour: it is a hard runtime abort.

Context: the pre-rebase branch has the same allocate pattern (eftcamb-pre-rebase results.f90:535), so the defect is old, not caused by the rebase. The B1 tree could not run the repro script at all (exit code 1 even for GR, an API incompatibility), so I could not use it for comparison.

Severity: S1 fits, because it is a crash reachable with valid input through a standard API call.

The proposed fix is safe-local. I checked it in work/t2-state/fix: all four cases exit 0 with identical H.

### F-005 [S1, confirmed] copy.deepcopy / pickle of a CAMBparams carrying EFTCAMB drops the model and segfaults

- **Location:** `camb/baseconfig.py:717` (also: fortran/results.f90:584, fortran/eftcamb/11_EFTCAMB_python.f90 (num_params/param_values getters), also filed by another track as repro/eft-pickle-repr-loses-model.py)
- **Track:** t2-state; reported severity S1, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/pickle-deepcopy-eftcamb-segfault.py A0`
- **Observed:** On A0, original and cp.copy() both give H(z=1)=119.885653414662. After copy.deepcopy(cp) or pickle.loads(pickle.dumps(cp)): EFTflag=1, model_name='', and get_background is killed by SIGSEGV. Repro output: 'observed: 2 crashed'.
- **Expected:** deepcopy/pickle reproduce the model (same H(z=1)) or raise a Python error. CAMBparams documents pickling as supported, and multiprocessing pickles params.
- **Root cause:** CAMB_Structure.__getstate__/__setstate__ (baseconfig.py:717/730) serialise only ctypes _fields_. The python EFTCAMB class maps the flags but not the allocatable Fortran `model`, so the restored object has EFTflag /= 0 and an unallocated model. CAMBdata_SetParams (results.f90:584, model%initialize_background) and the getters dereference it without checking allocated(model).
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** (1) camb/model.py CAMBparams: override __getstate__ to store EFTCAMB.read_parameters() when EFTflag != 0. Override __setstate__ to call self.EFTCAMB.initialize_parameters(self, params, print_header=False) and then restore the flags. The diff was tested in work/t2-state/fix/camb/model.py lines 851-872: deepcopy, pickle and repr/eval are all exact for 11 models. (2) Defensive Fortran guard in CAMBdata_SetParams: if (EFTflag /= 0 .and. .not. allocated(this%CP%EFTCAMB%model)) call GlobalError('EFTCAMB model not initialised', error_unsupported_params) instead of dereferencing.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; I ran the repro exactly as given on A0 and got the same result. The original params and cp.copy() both work. After copy.deepcopy or pickle the object has EFTflag=1 and model_name='', and get_background dies with SIGSEGV.

My own check (fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/checks/verify/pickle_deepcopy_verify.py, every case in its own subprocess holding a harness slot) points to the same cause:
- **Control:** pickling a GR params (EFTflag=0) round-trips exactly, so generic CAMB pickling is fine.
- **What gets saved:** __getstate__ keeps the EFTCAMB entry, but only its 33 ctypes flag fields. The allocatable Fortran `model` is not carried over.
- **Same restored object, model rebuilt:** if I call c2.EFTCAMB.initialize_parameters(c2, cp.EFTCAMB.read_parameters()), get_background gives exactly the original H(z=1). The missing model alone causes the crash.
- **Other entry points crash too:** EFTCAMB.num_params(), camb.get_results and eval(repr(cp)) all die with SIGSEGV on the restored object. The repr route also keeps EFTflag=1 but not the model.

The defect is in EFTCAMB's integration, not upstream CAMB. EFTCAMB adds an AllocatableObject whose Fortran state is not fully held in _fields_, and nothing in the Fortran (results.f90:584 model%initialize_background, and the python getters) checks allocated(model) when EFTflag /= 0.

The stated location is right: baseconfig.py:717 is __reduce__, which calls __getstate__ (line 720). The Fortran dereference is at results.f90:584.

Severity: S1 by the rubric. It is a crash reachable through documented, supported API use (model.py:301 says pickle/repr work), with no Python error raised. It is not a physics error, so S2 could be argued.

The proposed fix makes sense: rebuild the model from read_parameters() in __setstate__, plus a Fortran guard on allocated(model). I did not test it here.

I could not run the comparison on B1: its old set_params API fails before any pickling, with a TypeError on YHe. So I have not checked whether the bug predates the rebase.
- **Verifier (physics):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; I reproduced the crash on A0. The expected behaviour is correct: camb/model.py:301 says a CAMBparams can be saved and restored "using the repr and eval functions, or pickle it", and copy.deepcopy uses the same __reduce__/__getstate__ path in baseconfig.py:714-732. That path loops only over ctypes _fields_. The EFTCAMB python class maps flags and scalars but not the allocatable Fortran `model` component. The model is not exposed as a c_void_p either, so the "Cannot save class with general pointer" guard never fires and the state is dropped without any error. CAMBdata_SetParams (results.f90:584) then calls this%CP%EFTCAMB%model%initialize_background with no allocated() check, which gives SIGSEGV. cp.copy() works because it goes through the Fortran-side copy (_new_copy), which deep-copies allocatable components. This explains why copy and deepcopy give different results.

None of the alternative explanations fit. There is no accuracy noise, units issue or convention difference: the restored process dies outright. It is not a known fact (F-001/F-002 are unrelated). The bug is EFTCAMB's own, because upstream CAMB's polymorphic components are mapped through the python fields, while EFTCAMB's model is not.

Severity S1 matches the brief's definition: a crash reachable with valid input through a documented public API, and pickling is used implicitly by multiprocessing and by samplers. One could argue for S2, since it is an API edge path that does not affect normal ini/set_params runs. The root cause and the proposed fix direction (a CAMBparams __getstate__/__setstate__ that stores and rebuilds the EFTCAMB parameters, plus a Fortran allocated(model) guard that raises a GlobalError) look sound. I did not test the fix.

### F-006 [S1, confirmed] Extended Galileon expansion rate drops massive neutrinos for a>=0.2 (reads eft_cache%grhonu_tot before callers fill it); zero bisection tolerance; no DE in CAMB background for a<0.2

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/08f_full_models/008p7_Extended_Galileon.f90:455` (also: fortran/eftcamb/08f_full_models/008p7_Extended_Galileon.f90:479 (10**(-1) == 0), fortran/eftcamb/08f_full_models/008p7_Extended_Galileon.f90:460-468 (a<0.2 branch reads grhom_t/EFTc/EFTLambda; dtauda sets only grhoa2), fortran/eftcamb/08f_full_models/008p7_Extended_Galileon.f90:525-534 (ComputeHubbleDer reads grhonu_tot/gpinu_tot/gpinudot_tot), fortran/equations.f90:2816-2844, fortran/equations.f90:38-47, fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:115-140, fortran/eftcamb/11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:422-439 (call order), fortran/eftcamb/08f_full_models/008p7_Extended_Galileon.f90:337 (background EFT functions also read a stale adotoa/Hdot/Hdotdot in derivs: see fullmap-bg-eft-stale-background))
- **Track:** t2d-static; reported severity S1, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/extgal-adotoa-stale-grhonu.py [/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-marco-IW-eftcamb-update-EFTCAMB/2b9dddd0-ab26-46fb-8810-571fb7648a45/scratchpad/audit/work/t2d-static/FIX3]`
- **Observed:** Tree A0 (numbers re-run in attempt 4, unchanged), audit/inis/008p7-ext-galileon.ini on base_params (omnuh2=0.01, Omega_nu=0.0204), stability checks off, get_eft_functions vs brentq root of the code's own equation E^(2+S)=Omega_phi0+Omega_tot E^S. code/exact-1 = -3.511e-2 at a=0.2+, -3.474e-2 at 0.3, -2.842e-2 at 0.5, -1.120e-2 at 0.8, -5.973e-3 at 1. The code matches the root WITHOUT neutrinos to <=5e-7. adotoa(a=1)/H0-1 = -5.973e-3. Jump adotoa(0.2-1e-9)/adotoa(0.2+1e-9)-1 = +3.639e-2 (exact 5e-9). CAMB's dtauda H (results.hubble_parameter) vs exact: +3.0e-3 (a=0.5), +5.8e-3 (0.8), +4.2e-3 (1), and -2.6e-4 just below a=0.2 (also -3.1e-4 in the massless control: no DE for a<0.2). Control without massive nu: code/exact <= 3.8e-7, the floor of the 50-step zero-tolerance bisection (1e9/2^51 = 4.4e-7). Candidate fix (FIX3): code/exact <= 2.4e-14 for a>=0.2, adotoa(1)/H0-1 = -4.3e-15, dtauda consistent to 1e-14.
- **Expected:** adotoa equals the tracker solution including massive neutrinos (rel err <= 1e-8), continuous at a=0.2, adotoa(a=1)=H0, CAMB background H(z) consistent with the model.
- **Root cause:** (1) EFTCAMBExtendedGalileonComputeAdotoa builds Omega_tot from eft_cache%grhonu_tot (l.455-457). Every caller fills grhonu_tot only AFTER compute_adotoa: derivs equations.f90:2816-2844, dtauda equations.f90:38-47 via BackgroundEFTFunctions l.337-338, 09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:115-140, 11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:422-439. A fresh cache (dtauda, IC, stability, get_eft_functions, every derivs call when DebugEFTCAMB) therefore has grhonu_tot=0. Release derivs uses the previous call's value, so the result is history dependent. It is the only compute_adotoa that reads grhonu_tot (scan of all 12 implementations). (2) l.479 `sqrt(fsoluction**2) > 10**(-1)`: the integer 10**(-1) is 0 (gfortran -Winteger-division), so the loop always does 50 halvings. (3) The a<0.2 branch (l.460-468) uses grhom_t, EFTc and EFTLambda, which are 0 in dtauda's fresh cache, so adotoa=0. BackgroundEFTFunctions then returns (l.341) without setting any EFT function, and dtauda=sqrt(3/grhoa2) has no dark energy. ComputeHubbleDer (l.525-534) also reads grhonu_tot/gpinu_tot/gpinudot_tot, which are 0 in the dtauda/background path. Pre-existing in B1 (same line 444).
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** In ComputeAdotoa (and ComputeHubbleDer), compute the massive-neutrino background locally instead of reading the cache. Tested as FIX3 (work/t2d-static/fix3.diff): add `use MassiveNu`; grhonu_loc = sum_i grhormass(i)/a2*rho_i with `call ThermalNuBackground%rho_P(a*eft_par_cache%nu_masses(nu_i), rhonu_i, pnu_i)`; Omega_tot = (omegac+omegab)*a**(-3) + (omegag+omegar)*a**(-4) + grhonu_loc/(3*h0_Mpc**2*a2); replace the loop condition with `do while (abs(fsoluction) > 1.e-13_dl .and. counter < 100)`. Also drop the a<0.2 shortcut (use the tracker root for all a), or build grhom_t from eft_par_cache, so the dtauda path does not see grhom_t=EFTc=EFTLambda=0. The a<0.2 part was not built or tested.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; The defect is real and sits in the code under audit; it is not upstream CAMB and not a test artefact. EFTCAMBExtendedGalileonComputeAdotoa (008p7_Extended_Galileon.f90:455-457) builds Omega_tot from eft_cache%grhonu_tot. I checked every caller: derivs (equations.f90:2816-2844), IC (09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:115-140) and the python bridge (11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:422-439) all call compute_adotoa first and only then zero and fill grhonu_tot. dtauda (equations.f90:38-47) passes a cache fresh from initialize(), so grhonu_tot is 0 there too. A scan of all 07f/08f compute_adotoa routines shows this is the only one that reads grhonu_tot. The integer 10**(-1) at l.479 is indeed 0, so the bisection always runs its full 50 halvings; the control run shows the resulting ~4e-7 floor. For a<0.2, CAMB's dtauda H falls short of the analytic tracker by about Omega_DE/2 (-3.1e-4 just below 0.2 even with massless neutrinos), which matches the no-DE-in-dtauda claim. B1 has the identical code (l.423, l.444), so the bug was already there before the rebase. Caveat on the repro: run exactly as given it fails, because audit/inis/008p7-ext-galileon.ini still has DEFAULT(/Users/marco/Desktop/IW/...) and the repo has moved to /Users/marco/IW. I re-ran it with only that path fixed. Also, a full CAMB run of this model with the stability checks off fails in calc_transfer (RungeKuttaDP45 error -3), so both checks are background-only. Severity S1 stands: wrong background physics in a supported model with valid input (any massive neutrinos). The size scales with Omega_nu: 3.5% in H for omnuh2=0.01, roughly 0.2% for minimal-mass neutrinos.
- **Verifier (physics):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; Physics lens: the expected behaviour is correct. For a>=0.2 the code solves the GCCG tracker equation E^(2+S) = Omega_phi0 + Omega_tot E^S (in x = aH/H0). In the S -> 0 limit this reduces to the LCDM Friedmann equation, and for S=2 it gives the cubic-Galileon tracker E^4 = Omega_m E^2 + Omega_DE. The code itself puts massive neutrinos into Omega_tot (l.455-457), and omegav is set as the closure density including Omega_nu. So the intended solution has adotoa(a=1)=H0 exactly: the exact root with neutrinos gives 2.3349486664e-4 = h0_Mpc. The a<0.2 branch also includes neutrinos through grhom_t, so this is not a convention difference.

Accuracy noise and script error are ruled out. The massless control agrees with the exact root to <=3.8e-7, which is the 50-halving floor of [0,1e9] (1e9/2^51). The massive case is off by 3.5e-2 to 6e-3, with a 3.6% jump at a=0.2. That equals the no-neutrino root to <=5e-7, which is exactly the signature of grhonu_tot=0 when the root is computed.

Root cause checked in the source:
- l.455-457 reads eft_cache%grhonu_tot.
- dtauda (equations.f90:38-47) calls eft_cache%initialize() and then compute_background_EFT_functions, which calls compute_adotoa while grhonu_tot=0.
- derivs (equations.f90:2816-2844) calls compute_adotoa before filling grhonu_tot, so in release it reuses the previous call's value.
- l.479 `10**(-1)` is the integer 0, so the tolerance never stops the loop and only the 50-iteration counter does.
- ComputeHubbleDer (l.525-534) reads the same unfilled neutrino fields.
- The same expression is in pre-rebase B1 (eftcamb-pre-rebase, line 423 and line 488), so the bug predates the rebase.

Severity S1 is right. The default base_params (omnuh2=0.01) trigger it. The model's background expansion is wrong by 0.6% at z=0 and 3.5% at a=0.2. CAMB's own dtauda H(z) is inconsistent with it by 0.3-0.6% at low z, which affects distances and the thermal history. The perturbations use a different, history-dependent adotoa. I did not measure the size of the Cl/P(k) effect: the FIX3 tree was lost in the scratchpad wipe and was not needed to confirm the defect. The a<0.2 dtauda claim (no DE because grhom_t/EFTc/EFTLambda are 0 in a fresh cache) matches the numbers: -2.6e-4 just below a=0.2 in both the massive and massless runs, against about +2e-9 to 2e-7 just above 0.2 in the massless control.

### F-007 [S1, confirmed] EFTCAMB.get_scale_evolution/get_evolution segfaults after a matter-power-only run (WantScalars=F)

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:325`
- **Track:** t3-gr; reported severity S1, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/t3-get-evolution-transfer-only-segfault.py /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-marco-IW-eftcamb-update-EFTCAMB/2b9dddd0-ab26-46fb-8810-571fb7648a45/scratchpad/audit/work/t3-gr/R8`
- **Observed:** 3_RPH_MK_const_1, get_scalar_cls=F, transfer only: get_results ok, then get_scale_evolution kills the process (rc=-11 SIGSEGV; SIGBUS in the harness). With C_l, rc=0 and pi(k=0.05, a=0.1, 0.5, 1) = [0.03355, 0.06642, 0.07125]. lldb: EXC_BAD_ACCESS writing 1.0 to the stack in __gaugeinterface_MOD_initial, called from geteftoutputevolutionfork; the loop count is about 0x7ff80000 (garbage).
- **Expected:** rc=0 and the evolution returned (or a CAMBError), whichever observables were requested in the run.
- **Root cause:** EFTCAMB_GetEvolution calls GetNumEqns(Ev) on an OMP-private EvolutionVars. GetNumEqns fills lmaxg, lmaxnr, MaxlNeeded, nvar etc. only when CP%WantScalars is true. After a transfer-only run, results.CP%WantScalars=F, so initial() executes EV%Kf(1:EV%MaxlNeeded)=1 with stack garbage. Upstream CAMB_TimeEvolution has the same unguarded call but happens not to crash in B0.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** In EFTCAMB_GetEvolution: add `logical :: want_scalars`; before the OMP loop `want_scalars = this%CP%WantScalars; this%CP%WantScalars = .true.`; after `!$OMP END PARALLEL DO` `this%CP%WantScalars = want_scalars` (tested in tree work/t3-gr/R8: transfer-only rc=0, pi=[0.03343, 0.06633, 0.07131]). Alternatively raise a CAMBError in camb/eftcamb.py get_evolution when not camb_results.Params.WantScalars. Not investigated: after the fix, evolution following a transfer-only run differs from that after a C_l run by up to 4e-3.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; I ran the repro exactly as given and saw the crash. A0 with C_l returned rc=0. A0 transfer-only got as far as "get_results ok" and then died with rc=-11 (SIGSEGV). The R8 fix tree returned rc=0 in both modes.

The stated root cause is correct. In fortran/equations.f90:1049-1088, GetNumEqns sets lmaxg, lmaxnr, MaxlNeeded and SetupScalarArrayIndices only when CP%WantScalars is true; otherwise it just sets nvar=0. After a transfer-only run, fortran/camb.f90:160 resets OutData%CP%WantScalars to Params%WantScalars, which is false. EFTCAMB_GetEvolution (11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:325) then calls GetNumEqns on an uninitialised OMP-private Ev, and initial() runs EV%Kf(1:EV%MaxlNeeded) at equations.f90:2149 with a garbage MaxlNeeded.

One part of the claim is wrong: "upstream CAMB_TimeEvolution ... happens not to crash in B0". It does crash. Plain upstream CAMB (B0) running results.get_time_evolution after a transfer-only run also died with SIGSEGV, after printing a RungeKuttaDP45 error. A0's own upstream path crashed the same way.

So this is an upstream CAMB bug, and EFTCAMB's new function EFTCAMB_GetEvolution copied the same unguarded GetNumEqns pattern. It is still a defect in code under audit: the EFTCAMB entry point crashes on valid input. The fix should also go into the upstream get_time_evolution path (or be filed separately as "upstream:"). The proposed fix, forcing WantScalars=T around the k loop or raising a CAMBError in Python, worked in R8.

Severity stays S1 (a crash reachable with valid API usage), although triggering it needs a transfer-only run followed by an evolution call.
- **Verifier (physics):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; Reproduced on A0. Expecting rc=0 is correct: a segfault is never intended behaviour, and the run setup is valid. In fact camb.f90:145-148 itself forces P%WantScalars=.false. for any transfer-only run, so every matter-power-only user who then calls get_scale_evolution hits this. The root cause in the code agrees. EFTCAMB_GetEvolution (11_EFTCAMB_python.f90, about lines 309-322) calls GetNumEqns(Ev) on an OMP-private Ev. GetNumEqns (equations.f90, about line 1049) sets lmaxg/lmaxnr/MaxlNeeded/nvar only inside `if (CP%WantScalars)`, so initial() then uses uninitialised sizes. One part of the claim is wrong. The finding says upstream CAMB_TimeEvolution 'happens not to crash in B0', but it does crash: plain upstream CAMB v2 (B0), and A0 with a GR model, both SIGSEGV in results.get_time_evolution after a transfer-only run. So the pattern comes from upstream, and an upstream companion finding should be filed (S3 'upstream:' by the rubric). The EFTCAMB copy in its own python bridge is still a defect of the code under audit. Severity stays S1 under the rubric (crash reachable with valid input), though it is limited to the diagnostic evolution API. The proposed fix (force WantScalars=.true. around the k loop) works. The transfer-only result differs from the C_l result by up to about 3.5e-3 in pi. That is plausibly because GetNumEqns picks different hierarchy sizes and accuracy when Want_CMB/WantCls are false; it is not a physics error, but it was not investigated further. The fixed tree's C_l run also differs from A0's by about 1e-5 (probably build or noise). Raising a CAMBError is the conservative alternative.

### F-008 [S1, confirmed] 3+1 wDE designer always fails with 'background solver failed' (intent(out) success never set)

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/07f_designer_models/007p8_3p1_wDE_designer.f90:888`
- **Track:** t3-gr; reported severity S1, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/t3-3p1-success-unset.py /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-marco-IW-eftcamb-update-EFTCAMB/2b9dddd0-ab26-46fb-8810-571fb7648a45/scratchpad/audit/work/t3-gr/R7`
- **Observed:** A0: 007p8-3p1.ini, its GR point (sigma1=rho1=1, w=-1) and the GR point with the EFT path forced all fail with 'EFTCAMB: background solver failed'. With the fix (tree R7), the GR point with the forced EFT path and stability skipped reproduces EFTflag=0 to 1.2e-8 in C_l.
- **Expected:** The model runs; at the GR point C_l equal the EFTflag=0 result to <=1e-4.
- **Root cause:** EFTCAMBDesigner3p1SolveDesignerEquations has `logical, intent(out) :: success`, which is assigned only on the DLSODA error path (line 970). On success it is undefined on return, and the A0 release build returns .false. at every parameter point. The caller's `success = .true.` (line 880) does not survive an intent(out) dummy.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** Add `success = .true.` as the first executable statement of EFTCAMBDesigner3p1SolveDesignerEquations (before line 909, `grhom0 = ...`). Tested in R7.
- **Verifier (physics):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; The code matches the finding. In 007p8_3p1_wDE_designer.f90, EFTCAMBDesigner3p1SolveDesignerEquations declares `logical, intent(out) :: success` and assigns it only on the DLSODA failure branch (`success = .false.`). On a normal return the value is undefined. Because the dummy is intent(out), the caller's `success = .true.` at line 880 is a dead store, and gfortran 16 at -O3 is allowed to drop it (it clobbers intent(out) dummies). B1 returned .true. only by chance with an older compiler.

I reproduced the failure on frozen A0 (source identical to the repo). The R7 tree differs from A0 only by the inserted `success = .true.` (diff: one line after line 908), and with it the background solve succeeds. At the GR point with the EFT path forced, the fixed tree reproduces ΛCDM C_l to 1.17e-8, so the expected behaviour is physically correct.

Caveat on 'expected': even with the fix, the shipped ini and the unforced GR point are still rejected as 'theory unstable (beyond GLPV)', on R7 and on B1 alike. That is a separate stability-check issue, so the fix makes the solver work but does not by itself make 007p8-3p1.ini run. S1 stands because a supported model can never run on A0.

The repro script as filed can't run from the current checkout. The ini's DEFAULT() points to /Users/marco/Desktop/IW/..., which no longer exists, so every run fails with 'include file not found'. I used a copy of the ini with the path corrected.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; The defect is in EFTCAMB code, not in upstream CAMB or the test. EFTCAMBDesigner3p1SolveDesignerEquations (007p8_3p1_wDE_designer.f90:888) declares `logical, intent(out) :: success` (line 895). Its only assignment is `success = .false.` on the DLSODA error path (line 970). The caller sets `success = .true.` (line 880), which is a dead store because the dummy is intent(out). With -O3, A0 returns .false., so every 3+1 point fails with 'EFTCAMB: background solver failed', and no 'DLSODA failed' line is printed.

The repro script as given could not run in the current environment. harness.DEFAULT_ROOT and the ini's DEFAULT() still point at the old /Users/marco/Desktop/IW paths (include file not found), and the R7 tree was wiped. That is an environment problem, not a problem with the finding. I reran with AUDIT_ROOT set to the live root and a path-corrected copy of the ini, and built my own fix tree (A0 + `success = .true.` at the top of the solver).

Independent check: a standalone Fortran analogue of the caller/callee success pattern prints T at -O0 and F at -O3 with gfortran 16.1. This confirms that the optimizer drops the caller's store and that the fault is the missing assignment, not the solver.

With the fix, the test ini and the plain GR point fail later, with 'theory unstable (beyond GLPV)'. That is the same failure B1 shows, and a separate stability issue (EFTGamma3V rounding, as the finder noted). The forced-EFT GR point then matches EFTflag=0.

S1 is kept: it is a reachable failure of a supported model at every parameter point.

Related latent instance, not part of this finding: Designer f(R) (007p3:320) uses the same pattern. Its solver is intent(out) and only sets .false., but the model happens to run.

### F-009 [S1, confirmed] Any EFT model with omk != 0 silently gives O(1)-wrong spectra (EFT adotoa has no curvature)

- **Location:** `fortran/equations.f90:2811`
- **Track:** t3-gr; reported severity S1, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/t3-eft-curvature-ignored.py`
- **Observed:** Pure EFT with all EFT functions zero and w=-1 (= LCDM) vs EFTflag=0 at the same cosmology. omk=+0.05: max relative TT 2.43e2, TE 8.2, EE 2.3e-2, P(k) 2.6e-2, sigma8 1.3e-2. omk=-0.05: TT 57, P(k) 2.7e-2, sigma8 1.3e-2. CAMB's own background H(z) agrees to 2e-16. With omk=0 it agrees to 1e-5. This happens even when the EFT perturbations never switch on (RGR time 1.1).
- **Expected:** Agreement with LCDM at the same omk to <=1e-4, or a clear error that curvature is unsupported.
- **Root cause:** For EFTflag != 0, derivs takes adotoa from model%compute_adotoa (equations.f90:2811-2826; designer and full-mapping models build it from matter + radiation + DE only, without grhok), and the curvature cothxor/Kf terms are not consistently included. CAMB's background (dtauda, distances, Bessel functions) is curved. No check rejects omk != 0 when EFTflag != 0. Overlaps curvature-silently-wrong-with-eft.py from another track.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** Until curvature is implemented in the EFT background and perturbations, refuse it: in results.f90 (EFTCAMB parameter-cache block, ~line 550), add `if (this%CP%omk /= 0._dl) then; call GlobalError('EFTCAMB: non-flat models are not supported', error_unsupported_params); return; end if`. A full fix adds State%grhok to every compute_adotoa/compute_H_derivs and to the EFT Einstein equations.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; I ran the repro exactly as given and got the reported numbers. I then checked the claim a second way: a different model (2_PEFT_wDE_LCDM_1, Pure EFT with w0=-1), EFTCAMB_turn_on_time=1.1 so the EFT perturbations never switch on, fresh outputs, and three trees (A0, B1 and B0). At omk=+0.05 the EFT conformal Hubble rate at a=1 is low by exactly the factor sqrt(1-omk). That is direct evidence that compute_adotoa leaves out grhok. CAMB's own background H(z) is curved and agrees to 2e-16. The code matches the stated root cause. At equations.f90:2811-2826, adotoa for EFTflag/=0 comes only from model%compute_adotoa. No file in fortran/eftcamb uses grhok: eft_par_cache%grhok is set in results.f90:576 and never read. There is a second mechanism the finding only alludes to: equations.f90:2907 forces cothxor=1/tau (the flat value) whenever EFTflag/=0, so the Boltzmann hierarchy truncation is also flat. Neither the code nor the docs reject or document omk/=0 for EFT models. B1 (pre-rebase EFTCAMB) shows the same error, so the defect predates the rebase but is still in EFTCAMB code. It was not introduced by upstream CAMB, and it is not a test artifact. The finding duplicates repro/curvature-silently-wrong-with-eft.py from another track; merge the two. The S1 severity stands: omk is a valid input, and the spectra come out silently and badly wrong, with the TT quadrupole off by a factor of about 244.
- **Verifier (physics):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; The effect is real. I compared runs from the same tree, which rules out the known A0-vs-B0 offsets: Pure EFT with Omega=0 and w=-1 (exactly LCDM) against 1_EFT_GR.ini (EFTflag=0) at the same omk. At omk!=0 they differ far more than numerical accuracy, while CAMB's background H(z) agrees to 1e-16. The code confirms the cause. In derivs (fortran/equations.f90:2801-2826), when EFTflag/=0, adotoa comes from model%compute_adotoa, which has no grhok term; eft_par_cache%grhok is set to 0 (03 cache:1057) and never filled, and results.f90:563 copies only omegak. On top of that, cothxor is not set in the EFT branch and is then hard-coded to the flat value 1/tau at equations.f90:2907. So both the expansion rate and the curved-space truncation terms assume flatness, while CAMB's distances and Bessel functions are curved. Nothing rejects omk!=0 when EFTflag!=0. Physics lens: the EFTCAMB papers (1312.5742, 1405.3590) and the alpha-basis work (1404.3713) all assume a spatially flat FLRW background, so curvature was never a supported configuration. The expected behaviour (agree with LCDM, or raise an error) is still right, because silently accepting omk gives O(1)-wrong low-ell results. The same numbers appear in B1 (pre-rebase EFTCAMB), so the defect is long-standing and not caused by the rebase. I lowered the severity from S1 to S2. The inputs are outside the theory space EFTCAMB implements (the flat EFT background), so this is a wrong result in an unsupported parameter region, not wrong physics in a supported model. High-ell TT is off by only about 5e-4 at omk=0.05; the large errors are at low ell (ISW and quadrupole) and in P(k)/sigma8 at the percent level. The proposed guard (a GlobalError when omk!=0 and EFTflag!=0) is the right minimal fix. It should use abs(omk) > OmegaKFlat rather than /=0, to match State%flat. The root cause should also cite the flat cothxor=1/tau at equations.f90:2907, not just adotoa.

### F-010 [S1, confirmed] Extended Galileon: CAMB H(z) has no dark energy at z>4, adotoa drops massive neutrinos at a>=0.2, NaN spectra at S=0

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/08f_full_models/008p7_Extended_Galileon.f90:435`
- **Track:** t3-gr; reported severity S1, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/t3-extgal-gr-point-background.py`
- **Observed:** At S=0 (exact LCDM): (i) CAMB background H/H_LCDM-1 = -8.9e-3 (z=4.1), -5.5e-3 (z=5), -1.2e-3 (z=9), -4e-5 (z=30), and exactly 0 at z<3.9. (ii) get_eft_functions adotoa/(aH_LCDM)-1 = -3.44e-2 (a=0.21), -2.70e-2 (0.5), -1.03e-2 (1.0) with omnuh2=0.01; the control run with omnuh2=0 gives -8.6e-6, -2.7e-5, -3.6e-7. (iii) With the stability check on: 'Model has Nan in the timestep cache'; with it skipped, the C_l are NaN.
- **Expected:** H(z) and adotoa equal LCDM to ~1e-6 at S=0, and finite C_l equal to EFTflag=0.
- **Root cause:** ComputeAdotoa has two branches. For a<0.2 it uses (grhom_t+2c-Lambda) from the cache. Inside dtauda only grhoa2 is filled, so adotoa=0, the background EFT functions return early (a*adotoa==0), c=Lambda=0, and dtauda has no DE: this affects every S. For a>=0.2 the tracker equation's Omega_tot uses eft_cache%grhonu_tot, which is filled only after compute_adotoa: in derivs it lags by one call, and in EFTCAMB_GetEFTFunctions it is still 0. Massive neutrinos are dropped (Omega_nu=0.0204 for base_params). The bisection stop test `sqrt(fsoluction**2) > 10**(-1)` uses integer 10**(-1)=0, so it always does 50 halvings; harmless. At S=0, NaNs appear in the cache.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** (1) In ComputeAdotoa, drop the a<0.2 cache branch, or fall back to grhoa2/a^2 when grhom_t==0, so that dtauda includes Omega_phi. Better, solve the tracker equation at all a with Omega_tot from params_cache plus ThermalNuBackground%rho. (2) Compute the massive-neutrino density inside ComputeAdotoa rather than reading eft_cache%grhonu_tot. Alternatively move the 'fill in the massive neutrinos variables in cache' block (equations.f90:2829) before 'compute adotoa', and likewise in 11_EFTCAMB_python.f90. (3) Use a real tolerance, e.g. 1.e-12_dl*soluction. (4) Guard S=0 (division by S or B) in the EFT functions. Not tested.
- **Verifier (physics):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; The expectation holds. ComputeAdotoa solves E^(2+S) = Omega_phi0 + Omega_tot E^S, i.e. E^2 = Omega_tot + Omega_phi0 E^-S. The commented-out a<0.2 line is the S=2 closed form of this equation. At S=0 it reduces exactly to LCDM: B=0, so EFTc=0 and EFTLambda=-3a^2H0^2 Omega_phi0. So "H equals LCDM at S=0" is the right reference, and the deviations are not convention or accuracy noise.

Both background defects also show up at the paper's GCCG1 point (S=2, q=0.35), not just at S=0. That is why S1 stands. The S=0 point also passes the model's own priors: (S+0.02)q = 2 >= 0.5, and the strong-coupling term is < 0.

Mechanisms confirmed against the code:
(i) Inside dtauda (equations.f90:40-48) only grhoa2 is set. The a<0.2 branch uses grhom_t, EFTc and EFTLambda, which are all 0 there, so adotoa=0. BackgroundEFTFunctions then returns early and FullModelComputeDtauda gets c=Lambda=0: no dark energy for a<0.2.
(ii) eft_cache%grhonu_tot is filled after compute_adotoa: equations.f90 derivs (2816-2844, 4195-4219) and 11_EFTCAMB_python.f90 (422-439). On a fresh cache the massive neutrinos are dropped from the tracker equation at a>=0.2. CAMB's dtauda then mixes grhoa2 (with neutrinos) with c and Lambda built from the neutrino-free adotoa. For S!=0 this makes CAMB's H(z=0) differ from the input H0.
(iii) The NaN at S=0 comes from Hdotdot in ComputeHubbleDer: its denominator ((S+2) adotoa^2 a^2 H0^2 S Omega_tot)^3 is zero at S=0. This formula overlaps with the separate ext-galileon-hdotdot finding. "Guard division by S" in the proposed fix points at the right place.

Caveats:
- Root cause (i)+(ii) duplicates the t2d-static finding extgal-adotoa-stale-grhonu; merge the two.
- The NaN at S=0 alone is an S2 edge case. The model has no shipped test ini.
- The repro as filed fails with rc=1 only because the repo moved from ~/Desktop/IW to ~/IW: the DEFAULT path in audit/inis/008p7-ext-galileon.ini is stale. This is an environment issue, not a code issue. I ran a path-fixed copy that differs only in that path.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; The defect is reproduced, and an independent route attributes it to the code under audit (008p7_Extended_Galileon.f90 ComputeAdotoa, together with the full-model dtauda hook in equations.f90:39-48). It also occurs at the default supported point (S=2, q=0.35), not only at the S=0 GR point.

Running the repro as given fails (status error:rc=1 on every run). The audit ini 008p7-ext-galileon.ini has DEFAULT(/Users/marco/Desktop/IW/...base_params.ini), and the checkout now lives at /Users/marco/IW/... This is a problem with the test file, not with EFTCAMB. With a path-fixed copy (checks/verify/extgal_verify.ini + extgal_repro_pathfix.py), the repro prints exactly the claimed numbers.

Independent check (checks/verify/extgal_tracker_independent.py): EFTCAMB is run directly from the A0 tree. The tracker equation E^(2+S) = Omega_phi0 + Omega_tot E^S is solved in numpy, with Omega_tot taken from CAMB's own get_background_densities (cdm+baryon+photon+neutrino+massive nu). It is then compared with results.hubble_parameter (dtauda) and get_eft_functions adotoa.

Code reading agrees with the stated mechanism:
- In dtauda the cache is freshly initialized with only grhoa2 set, so the a<0.2 branch gives sqrt(0)=0. The background EFT functions then return early, and dtauda reduces to the no-DE value.
- Omega_tot reads eft_cache%grhonu_tot, which equations.f90:2829 and 11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:429 fill only after compute_adotoa.
- The NaN at S=0 comes from Hdotdot dividing by (...*S*Omega_tot)**3 in ComputeHubbleDer. S=0, q=100 passes the model's own additional priors.
- 10**(-1) is integer 0, so the bisection always runs 50 iterations.

Severity stays S1. For the default model with base_params massive neutrinos, CAMB's background H(z) is wrong by up to 0.6%, and H(z=0) is not equal to the input H0. That is wrong physics in a supported model. The S=0 NaN on its own would be S2 (edge case).

### F-011 [S1, confirmed] Cubic spline parametrization (flag 9): wrong at end nodes, derivatives inconsistent with value

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/04f_parametrizations_1D/04p016_spline_parametrizations_1D.f90:357` (also: fortran/eftcamb/04f_parametrizations_1D/04p016_spline_parametrizations_1D.f90:380-452, fortran/eftcamb/02_interpolation_linear_1D.f90:574-581)
- **Track:** t4a-param; reported severity S1, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/spline-cubic-endpoints-derivatives.py --model`
- **Observed:** Nodes 0, 0.2, ..., 1 with exactly linear data v=0.1+0.5x. value(0)=0.0 (null) vs 0.1; value(1)=0.0 vs 0.6. d1(0.06)=0.233, d1(0.5)=0.487, d1(0.94)=0.233, all expected 0.5. With null_value=-1, d1, d2 = -1 at nodes and outside. Curved data: d1 vs FD of value 0.58, d2 1.2, d3 29. Pure EFT 2_PEFT_Omega_lin_1 with Omega=a written as this spline vs the analytic run: worst Cl 0.146, P(k) 0.060, sigma8 0.029. With the fix: 8.1e-13.
- **Expected:** A cubic spline through linear data is the line itself. value(x_i)=v_i, d1=0.5, d2=d3=d4=0. Spectra identical to the analytic Omega=a run.
- **Root cause:** (a) Strict tests `x <= x_initial .or. x >= x_final` (lines 357, 381, 405, 427, 449, 475) return null_value at both end nodes. (b) Derivatives are not derived from the value spline cy: they are separate cubic splines (cyp, cypp, cyppp, cypppp) of nodal derivatives from linear_interpolate_function_1D InitDerivatives. That routine forces yp, ypp, yppp, ypppp to 0 at the first and last node (02_interpolation_linear_1D.f90:574-581). (c) Outside the nodes every derivative returns null_value (see spline-derivatives-outside-nodes).
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** In value and all derivatives use `if (x < x_initial .or. x > x_final) return`. Compute derivatives from the value coefficients: d1=dpoly3(x, cy(:,ip)), d2=d2poly3(x, cy(:,ip)), d3=6*cy(5,ip), d4=0. Return 0 (not null_value) for derivatives outside the nodes. Tested patch: work/t4a-param/FIX.diff (04p016 hunks); after it all FD/node checks are 0.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; I ran the repro as given (A0 build) and got the reported numbers exactly. As an independent check I read the EFT timestep cache of the full Pure EFT run, a different code path from the stand-alone Fortran driver. In that run Omega(a) is given as a cubic spline (flag 9) of the exact line Omega=a. The spline value is exact in the interior. Omega(a=1) is 0 instead of 1. OmegaP is far from 1 near both ends of the node range: 9e-6 at a=1e-6, 0.09 at a=0.01, 0.69 at a=0.1, 0.37 at a=0.955 and 0 at a=1. This matches the stated root cause. (a) The strict tests `x <= x_initial .or. x >= x_final` in 04p016:357/381/405/427/449/475 return null_value at the end nodes. (b) The derivatives come from separate splines (cyp, cypp, ...) of nodal derivatives that 02_interpolation_linear_1D.f90:574-581 forces to 0 at the first and last node, so they do not match the value spline cy. The defect is in EFTCAMB code, not upstream CAMB and not the test. The pre-rebase branch has the same logic (only formatting differs), so the defect is inherited, not introduced by the rebase. Zeroing the boundary derivatives looks deliberate ('for continuity' with the null_value outside). Even so, it makes the derivatives wrong inside the node range, and that cannot be intended. No shipped test ini uses flag 9 for these functions. It is still a selectable parametrization that gives 15% Cl errors, so S1 stands. I did not rebuild the proposed fix: the earlier work/ trees were wiped. The fix direction (derivatives taken from the cy coefficients, inclusive bounds) is right.
- **Verifier (physics):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; I reproduced every claimed number with the agent's repro on the A0 build. The expectation is correct. spline3pars is called with BC type [2,2] and value 0, which is a natural spline, so its exact interpolant of linear data is the line. Any consistent parametrization must satisfy value(x_i)=v_i and d/dx value = first_derivative, because Pure EFT feeds Omega, Omega' and Omega'' straight into the perturbation equations (1312.5742 / 1405.3590). The strict tests `x <= x_initial .or. x >= x_final` (04p016 lines 357, 381, 405, 427, 449, 475) return null_value at the end nodes. The ini reader's default node placement is x_i=(i-1)/(N-1), which is exactly [0,1] in scale factor, so the default layout puts a=1 (today) on the null-valued end node. The derivatives are separate splines of nodal derivatives that InitDerivatives forces to 0 at the first and last node (02_interpolation_linear_1D.f90:574-581, commented "for continuity"). That makes them inconsistent with the value spline everywhere, not only near the ends: d1 at the interior point 0.3 is 0.526 instead of 0.5. This might be meant as continuity with a constant extension outside the node range. That intent does not hold up: the value is not forced to null_value at the boundary, and with null_value≠0 the derivatives return null_value (not 0) outside, so value and derivatives are inconsistent either way. I tried to explain the Cl difference away by moving the end nodes outside [0,1] so a=1 becomes an interior point. The derivative problem remains on its own: with 8 nodes, Omega'(a=1) is 1.0456 instead of 1 and the worst Cl differs by 0.61%; with 16 nodes, 1.0037 and 0.03%. The large 14.6% Cl / 6% P(k) error therefore comes mostly from a=1 sitting on the null-valued, zero-derivative end node, which is the default configuration. This is wrong physics in a supported Pure EFT option at default node placement, so S1 stands. The code is identical in pre-rebase EFTCAMB apart from formatting, so this is a long-standing defect, not a rebase regression. I did not re-test the proposed fix diff.

### F-012 [S1, confirmed] Steplog parametrization (flag 8): fourth derivative wrong/NaN, integral(1) != 1

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/04f_parametrizations_1D/04p018_steplog_parametrizations_1D.f90:340` (also: fortran/eftcamb/04f_parametrizations_1D/04p018_steplog_parametrizations_1D.f90:369-376, consumers: 007p1 Pure EFT, 007p2 RPH (Omega/gamma/alpha functions), 007p3 designer f(R) and 007p7 ShiftSym (integral))
- **Track:** t4a-param; reported severity S1, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/steplog-d4-and-integral.py --model`
- **Observed:** v1=-1, v2=-0.8, aT=0.3, delta=0.5: d4(0.05)=-24537 vs exact 1.309; d4(0.2)=142.4 vs -1425.2; d4(0.3)=7457 vs 1630; NaN for a>0.385. integral(1)=1.3959 vs 1 (exact at a=0.999999). Pure EFT, Omega=steplog(0, 1.5, 0.5, 0.5): A0 run rejected ('EFTOmegaPPPP is NaN / EFTc is NaN'); with the fix it runs. Designer f(R) with steplog w(-1, -0.8, 0.3, 0.5): A0 vs fixed integral worst Cl 3.4e-4, grhov_t off by 40% at a=1.
- **Expected:** d4 of the documented 0.5(v2-v1)L/sqrt(1+L^2)+(v1+v2)/2 with L=ln(a/aT)/delta, finite everywhere. I(1)=1 (EFTCAMB convention I(a)=a^2 rho_DE(a)/rho_DE0).
- **Root cause:** d4 (lines 340-352) uses (1 - ln(x/aT)/delta^2)^(k/2) instead of (1+(ln(x/aT)/delta)^2)^(k/2), giving a negative base, hence NaN. The integral (lines 369-376) contains a ln(aT)^2/ln(x) term: algebraically equal to the right answer for x != 1, but at x=1 the exponent is Inf and 1**Inf = 1 drops one factor.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** d4 = 0.5(v2-v1)/x^4 [15 L(3-4L^2)/(1+L^2)^4.5/delta^4 - 6(12L^2-3)/(1+L^2)^3.5/delta^3 - 33 L/(1+L^2)^2.5/delta^2 - 6/(1+L^2)^1.5/delta]. Integral: term1 = x**(-1-1.5(v1+v2)), term2 = exp(-1.5(v2-v1)(sqrt(delta^2+(ln x - ln aT)^2) - sqrt(delta^2+ln(aT)^2))). Tested patch: work/t4a-param/FIX.diff (04p018 hunks); exact errors 3.3e-15 (d4) and 3.6e-15 (integral).
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; I ran the repro unchanged (with --model) and got the reported numbers. I then checked the same claim a different way. A numpy copy of the Fortran expressions was compared with mpmath at 40 digits, using numerical 4th derivatives of the documented w(a) and direct quadrature of a^2 exp(3 int_a^1 (1+w) dln a). The two methods agree with each other and with the repro.

1. The fourth derivative (04p018:340-352) uses the base (1 - ln(x/aT)/Delta^2) where it should use (1 + (ln(x/aT)/Delta)^2). It is wrong at every a. It blows up at a = aT exp(Delta^2) (0.385 for the test parameters) and is NaN above that.

2. The integral (04p018:369-376) contains a ln(aT)^2/ln(x) term. At x=1 exactly this makes the exponent Inf, and 1**Inf = 1 drops a factor, so I(1)=1.3959 instead of 1. At x=1-1e-15 the code still gives 1.000000000000, so only the endpoint is wrong.

The CPL integral in the same family gives exactly 1 at a=1, which confirms the convention I(a)=a^2 rho_DE/rho_DE0. Designer f(R) (007p3:1082,1100) takes grhov_t = grhov*integral(a), so the bad value reaches the background at a=1.

The file is identical in the pre-rebase branch (git diff eftcamb-pre-rebase HEAD on 04p018 is empty). The bug is therefore old EFTCAMB code, not something introduced by the rebase, and not upstream CAMB. It is still a defect in the code under audit.

Severity: S1. A supported parametrization (flag 8) gives NaN for typical parameters, and a valid Pure EFT model is rejected because of it. The stated root cause file:line is correct. I did not re-run the designer f(R) Cl impact (3.4e-4); it is consistent with the confirmed I(1) error.
- **Verifier (physics):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; I could not refute either part.

1. **Fourth derivative.** The value is the documented function 0.5(v2-v1)L/sqrt(1+L^2)+(v1+v2)/2 with L=ln(a/aT)/delta, and the code returns that same function. The fourth derivative uses (1 - ln(x/aT)/delta^2)^(k/2) as its base. That is not a convention difference, because no differentiation of the value function gives that base. When a > aT*exp(delta^2) the base is negative, so the result is NaN. Below that point the result is finite but wrong.

2. **Integral at a=1.** The integral convention I(a)=a^2 rho_DE(a)/rho_DE0 matches the other 1D parametrizations: CPL gives x^(-1-3(w0+wa)) exp(3wa(x-1)), and linear gives exp(-3(x-1)w)/x. Under that convention I(1)=1. At x != 1 the code's expression equals the exact result to 1e-12. At x=1, log(aT)^2/log(x) is Inf, and 1**(+-Inf)=1 drops the term that should cancel term2, so I(1) = 1.3959 instead of 1. Designer f(R) uses this value directly as grhov_t = grhov*integral(a), so the error is not accuracy noise.

The bug was already in the pre-rebase code (B1 has the same line at about line 325), so it was not introduced by the rebase. It is still a real defect in the code under audit.

**Severity: S1 stands.** Pure EFT with a steplog Omega (a supported allocator option, flag 8) is rejected for every parameter set with aT*exp(delta^2) < 1. That covers almost all physically reasonable choices; even v2-v1=0.05 is rejected. This is wrong behaviour for valid input in a supported model.

The integral error at a=1 is smaller on its own: Cl changes by 3.4e-4 and P(k) by 3.7e-6.

The proposed fix is correct. I checked it against a sympy derivative and a scipy quad integral at 9 values of a, including a=1 and a=2, and it agrees at all of them.

### F-013 [S1, confirmed] Horava compute_H_derivs: Hdot/Hdotdot inconsistent with the model's own adotoa (Hdotdotdot never set)

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/08f_full_models/008p1_Horava.f90:453` (also: fortran/eftcamb/08f_full_models/008p1_Horava.f90:454-462 (Hdotdotdot missing))
- **Track:** t4b-derivs; reported severity S1, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/horava-hdot-inconsistent.py [TREE]`
- **Observed:** repro (A0): 5_Horava_SolSyst_3 (F=0.8696): stored Hdot vs dH/dtau rel err 4.3e-2 (a=0.001), 6.4e-2 (0.01), 6.8e-2 (0.1), 0.62 (0.5), 0.21 (0.9); EFT second-Friedmann residual/H^2 6e-2..0.25; Hdotdot vs d(Hdot)/dtau 0.146. 5_Horava_eta_4 the same (0.143); 5_Horava_eta_1 (F=0.99987) 4e-5..3.5e-4. Bulk scan: Hdot_EFT residual 0.145/0.136/1.3e-4 (SolSyst_3/eta_4/eta_1); derived cache fields wrong: EFTcdot 2.9e-2, EFTLambdadot 2.1e-2, EFTGamma1P 1.6e-2, grhodotq 3.5e-2, gpresdotdotm_t 3.4e-3; Hdotdotdot = 0. At turn-on a=0.01, which enters the EFT initial conditions, the Hdot error is 3.3e-2. Spectra change when it is fixed (A0 vs patched P_horava): TT l<=30 3.9e-1 (eta_4), 4.7e-1 (SolSyst_3), 3.1e-2 (eta_3), 3.3e-3 (eta_2), 3.4e-4 (eta_1); TT l=200-2500 3.0e-2 (eta_4); EE 1.6e-3 / 6.2e-3; sigma8 0.71885->0.71477 (eta_4). Same code in B1.
- **Expected:** Hdot = d adotoa/dtau. With adotoa^2 = F (grhom_t+grhov_t)/3 that is Hdot = -(adotoa^2 + F (gpresm_t+gpiv_t))/2, so that the EFT form (1+Omega)(2Hdot+H^2) + gpresm_t + Lambda = 0 holds. Patched tree: Hdot rel err 2e-6 (FD-limited), second Friedmann 1e-16, all cache derivative relations <= 2e-7.
- **Root cause:** compute_adotoa rescales H^2 by F = (2+2xi-eta)/(3lambda+2), but compute_H_derivs evaluates the GR expression -(adotoa^2 + gpresm_t + gpiv_t)/2 with the already-rescaled adotoa and multiplies by sqrt(F) (also for Hdotdot); Hdotdotdot is not assigned.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** Replace the body of EFTCAMBHoravaComputeHubbleDer (diff in scratchpad work/t4b-derivs/fix_diffs/horava-hdot.diff):
  F = (2+2xi-eta)/(3lambda+2)
  gpiv_t = -grhov_t
  Hdot = -0.5*(adotoa**2 + F*(gpresm_t + gpiv_t))
  Hdotdot = -adotoa*Hdot - 0.5*F*(gpresdotm_t + 2*adotoa*(gpresm_t + gpiv_t))
  Hdotdotdot = -Hdot**2 - adotoa*Hdotdot - 0.5*F*(gpresdotdotm_t + 4*adotoa*gpresdotm_t + (2*Hdot + 4*adotoa**2)*(gpresm_t + gpiv_t))
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; Analytically: with adotoa^2 = F*grho_t/3 (F constant), d(grho_t)/dtau = -adotoa*(grho_t+3*gpres_t) gives Hdot = -(adotoa^2 + F*gpres)/2. The code at 008p1_Horava.f90:452-462 instead computes sqrt(F)*(-(adotoa^2 + gpres)/2), using the adotoa that is already rescaled. That is only right when F=1. Hdotdot is also off by a factor of sqrt(F): the code gives adotoa*grho*sqrt(F) ~ F, but the correct term scales as F^(3/2). Hdotdotdot is never assigned, because Horava overrides the full-mapping default, so it stays 0. I ran the given repro on A0 and it reproduces every number quoted in the finding. My own independent check uses a different method: I took CAMB's own background (results.hubble_parameter and conformal_time, which do not go through the EFT cache), splined calH(tau) and differentiated it, then compared against eft_Hdot/eft_Hdotdot from the harness reference npz on the 401-point grid. The cache adotoa matches CAMB's background to 1e-6, so the background itself is consistent and only the derivatives are wrong. That rules out a fault in the test script. The defect is in EFTCAMB code, not upstream CAMB, and the same lines exist in B1 (pre-rebase lines 414-420). The finding's root cause and file:line are correct. Accuracy noise does not explain the spectrum effect: from the earlier verify run (noise_summary.json), for eta_4 the shift from the fix is TT l=2-30 0.38 and l=200-2500 0.030, against an accuracy-boost noise of 0.0096 and 0.014, and sigma8 goes 0.71889 -> 0.71481 (noise 4e-5). For eta_2 the effect is 3.3e-3 against noise 1.4e-3. This is wrong physics in a supported model that has test inis, so S1.
- **Verifier (physics):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; Physics check: the model's own background (compute_adotoa and compute_dtauda, which CAMB integrates for conformal time) has adotoa = sqrt(F)*sqrt(X/3), with X = grhom_t+grhov_t, F = (2+2xi-eta)/(3lambda+2), and the extra grhov_t term behaving like a constant (w=-1). Matter continuity uses the true expansion rate adotoa, so dX/dtau = -adotoa(X+3P). That gives Hdot = -(F/2)(X/3+P) = -(adotoa^2 + F P)/2. The code instead computes sqrt(F)*(-(adotoa^2 + P)/2) using the already rescaled adotoa. That equals the correct value only when F=1. No convention reading makes it correct: even if the GR formula were applied to the unscaled H and then multiplied by sqrt(F), a factor of sqrt(F) would still be missing. EFTCAMB defines Hdot as d(adotoa)/dtau, and the model's EFTc, EFTLambda, EFTcdot, EFTLambdadot and Gamma1 depend on it. So the second Friedmann equation is violated by about (1-F) or more. It is not accuracy noise: a finite difference of adotoa matches the consistent analytic formula to about 1e-6. It is not a known fact either. The same code is in B1 (pre-rebase line 419), so it is long-standing, not a rebase regression. Hdotdotdot is never set for Horava (it stays at the cache default of 0), and 06_abstract_EFTCAMB_model.f90:1128-1150 uses it. This is a minor extra point. S1 stands: it is wrong physics in a supported model at test-ini parameters (F about 0.87). The error scales with (1-F), so it is small for nearly-GR parameters.

### F-014 [S1, confirmed] Scaling Cubic Galileon background ignores massive neutrinos: perturbation H(a) differs from CAMB's H(a) by 3.5-7%

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/08f_full_models/008p6_Scaling_Cubic.f90:1123` (also: fortran/eftcamb/08f_full_models/008p6_Scaling_Cubic.f90:411-650 (SolveBackground ODE), fortran/eftcamb/08f_full_models/008p6_Scaling_Cubic.f90 ComputeHubbleDer H2/h)
- **Track:** t4b-derivs; reported severity S1, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/scg-adotoa-drops-massive-nu.py [TREE]`
- **Observed:** repro on the shipped 5_scg.ini (base_params omnuh2=0.01, one massive eigenstate): adotoa(derivs) / aH_CAMB - 1 = -7.0e-2 (a<=1e-6), -5.4e-2 (1e-4), -3.3e-2 (1e-3), -3.5e-2 (0.1), -2.7e-2 (0.5), -1.0e-2 (1); grhom_t/(grhoa2 a^4) - 1 = -0.136..-0.069. With omnuh2=0.00064: -7.0e-2 early, -6.5e-4 at a=1. With massless neutrinos only: 2e-16. Bulk scan: EFT second-Friedmann residual 0.157 early; cons_grhom_t 7.3e-2. At turn-on a_on=1e-8 (enters the initial conditions): adotoa vs CAMB 7.0e-2. The one-line candidate (keep the caller's grhom_t, fix_diffs/scg-adotoa-partial.diff) makes adotoa equal CAMB's H (6e-16) and changes TT by up to 1.3e-1 (l=453), PP 0.226, sigma8 0.6287->0.6702. But it leaves Hdot inconsistent with adotoa (rel err 0.14, EFT second-Friedmann 0.27), because the model's h, H2 and ODE contain no massive neutrinos. Same code in B1.
- **Expected:** One background: the adotoa used by derivs, CAMB's dtauda and the model's Hdot all include the massive-neutrino density and pressure (as 008p2_ADE.f90 does), agreeing to ~1e-6.
- **Root cause:** The SCG background system is built with grhorad = grhornomass+grhog and grhom = grhob+grhoc only. That covers SolveBackground, the H2 and h formulas in BackgroundEFTFunctions, SecondOrderEFTFunctions and ComputeHubbleDer, and ComputeAdotoa, which also overwrites eft_cache%grhom_t. CAMB's dtauda (full-model compute_dtauda via grhoa2) and the Einstein equations include massive neutrinos.
