- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** Add the massive-neutrino density and pressure (ThermalNuBackground%rho_P and derivatives, as in 008p2_ADE.f90) to the SCG background ODE and to the H2 / h / h' / h'' closures, so that adotoa^2 = (grhom_t incl. nu + 2c - Lambda)/3 and Hdot uses gpresm_t incl. nu. Alternatively refuse Num_Nu_Massive > 0 in EFTCAMBScalingCubicInitBackground with an eft_error. Do not apply only the one-line grhom_t change: it fixes H but leaves Hdot inconsistent at 27%.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; I ran the repro as given on A0 and got the reported numbers. I then checked the same claim a second way, with fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/checks/verify/scg_nu_independent.py. That script rebuilds H from CAMB's own get_background_densities (photon + massless nu + baryon + cdm + massive nu) together with the EFT functions from get_eft_functions. The grhom_t the model writes into the cache equals CAMB's matter-plus-radiation density with the massive-neutrino term removed, to 2e-16. Including that term, the two differ by 6.9-13.6%. So the deficit is exactly the missing massive-neutrino density, and it is not a problem in the test script or a normalisation mismatch.

Root cause and attribution are as stated:
- 008p6_Scaling_Cubic.f90:1123-1124 overwrites eft_cache%grhom_t with (grhornomass+grhog)/a^2 + (grhob+grhoc)/a. derivs had filled it from CAMB's grho, which includes massive nu (equations.f90:2791).
- derivs then uses compute_adotoa for every perturbation equation whenever EFTflag /= 0 (equations.f90:2811-2825).
- EFTCAMBFullModelComputeDtauda (06p1:81) uses grhoa2, which includes nu. The perturbations and CAMB's tau(a) therefore see two different expansion histories.
- The same grhom = grhob+grhoc and grhorad = grhornomass+grhog closures appear at lines 215-216, 434-435, 872-873, 1007-1008 and 1150-1151 (SolveBackground, background/second-order EFT functions, Hubble derivatives). Hdot comes from h, which is computed in that neutrino-free system, so it is inconsistent as well.

The defect is in EFTCAMB code, not upstream CAMB. It is also present in B1 (pre-rebase source at line 1029 is identical, and its derivs calls compute_adotoa). The effect appears for any number of massive eigenstates greater than zero. While massive neutrinos are still relativistic it is about 7% in H no matter how small the mass, and the shipped 5_scg.ini triggers it. Nothing warns the user or blocks this input.

S1 stands: wrong physics in a supported model with valid, default-like input. I did not test the proposed fix. The claim that the one-line patch leaves Hdot inconsistent follows from the code structure (h comes from the neutrino-free H2), but I did not measure it myself.
- **Verifier (physics):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; I ran the repro on A0 and got the reported numbers to all quoted digits. The expectation is correct, and the behaviour is not a convention choice. derivs (equations.f90:2811-2825) takes adotoa from EFTCAMBScalingCubicComputeAdotoa whenever EFTflag/=0. That routine (008p6:1123-1127) sets grhom_t = (grhornomass+grhog)/a^2 + (grhob+grhoc)/a, so it leaves out the massive-neutrino density. EFTCAMBFullModelComputeDtauda (06p1:82) builds dtauda from grhoa2. grhoa2 does include the massive neutrinos (ThermalNuBackground%rho_P, 06p1:237-242), and dtauda sets tau(a), distances and recombination. So within one run the background drives tau(a) and the thermal history, while every SCG perturbation equation uses an expansion rate up to 7% lower.

Comparing adotoa against CAMB's own H(z) is fair, because adotoa is the same field derivs uses. With massless neutrinos only, the two agree to 2e-16, so the method is sound and the mismatch comes only from the massive species. Tiny neutrino masses do not make the error small. Before the neutrinos go non-relativistic, the dropped term is one neutrino species' worth of radiation, about 13.6% of grhom_t. So at a<=1e-5, adotoa is 7% low even at the Planck-like omnuh2=0.00064, which is about 0.06 eV. That covers the initial conditions and recombination.

Nothing in the file documents the restriction, and nothing checks for it: no reference to num_nu_massive, omnuh2 or an eft_error. Other full models (008p0 Horndeski, 008p2 ADE, 008p4 quintessence) do carry the massive-neutrino background, which shows the intended behaviour. The shipped test ini 5_scg.ini inherits massive_neutrinos=1 from base_params, and the CAMB default setup also has one massive neutrino. The bug is therefore reached with valid default input in a supported model, and the physics is wrong, so S1 is right. The same code is in B1 (eftcamb-pre-rebase 008p6:1029-1031), so the rebase did not introduce it.

The root-cause description is accurate. The model's own H2/h closures (lines 342, 526, 601, 880, 1015, 1158) and the background ODE also use only grhorad = grhornomass+grhog and grhom = grhob+grhoc. The finding is also right that patching grhom_t alone would leave Hdot inconsistent. I did not re-run the partial-fix numbers for TT and sigma8: the defect stands without them.

### F-015 [S1, confirmed] EFTpiDfunction uses D1 instead of D2: wrong initial pi' at high k, K-mimic P(k) wrong by 10-100%

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:589` (also: fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:301 (use in pi' IC), fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:632 (EFTpiDdotFunction))
- **Track:** t5-robust; reported severity S1, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/ic1-pidot-eftpid-typo.py`
- **Observed:** Repro (A0 vs A0 with only this line fixed, default test inis). 5_Kmimic_1 P/P_GR-1 at k=1,2,5,10,20/Mpc: A0 +0.314,+0.215,+0.539,+1.359,+0.854; fixed +0.289,+0.125,+0.075,+0.060,+0.045. 5_Kmimic_2: A0 +0.368,+0.279,+0.580,+1.331,+0.895; fixed +0.345,+0.195,+0.150,+0.136,+0.122. C_L^phiphi(A0)/fixed-1 = +4.7e-4 (L=1000), +1.15e-3 (L=2000), +1.69e-3 (L=2500); lensed TT +3.4e-4 at ell=2000. Not accuracy noise: accuracy_boost=2 changes A0 k=10 from 1.359 to 1.358 and fixed from 0.0599 to 0.0599. Across all 73 models the fix changes other models by <= 1.2e-4 (3_RPH_MB_const_1). 5_Kmimic_2 is on the known not-converged list; this typo is likely why.
- **Expected:** pi'_ini built from C' + k^2 D1' + k^4 D2'; A0 should equal the fixed-line row
- **Root cause:** EFTpiDfunction = eft_cache%EFTpiD1 + k*k*eft_cache%EFTpiD1 (should be EFTpiD2). Its numerical derivative feeds the pi' initial condition at 09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:301, so the bracket holds k^4 D1' instead of k^4 D2', a relative error growing like k^2. The same line is in pre-rebase B1 (line 570). t3 mentions the line in passing but did not measure the impact.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** -    EFTpiDfunction = eft_cache%EFTpiD1 + k*k*eft_cache%EFTpiD1
+    EFTpiDfunction = eft_cache%EFTpiD1 + k*k*eft_cache%EFTpiD2
(tested in work/t5-robust/A0_pidfix: numbers above)
- **Verifier (physics):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; The physics checks out and the expected behaviour is right. EFT_IC_type=1 sets pi_ini = -h0 E/(C + k^2 D1 + k^4 D2), and the code writes that same denominator explicitly at 09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:296-302. The pi' initial condition is meant to be the conformal-time derivative of that expression: h0[E (C + k^2 D)' aH/(C + k^2 D)^2 - E'/(C + k^2 D)], with D = D1 + k^2 D2. Line 301 multiplies EFTpiDdotFunction by k^2, so EFTpiDfunction has to return D1 + k^2 D2. The current line 589 returns D1 + k^2 D1, which makes pi' disagree with the pi it is supposed to differentiate. This is a plain typo, not a different convention. D2 is non-zero for the K-mimic models (generic expression at 06_abstract_EFTCAMB_model.f90:608) and for Horava (008p1:486).

The large effect on P(k) fits mimetic-type models. Pi has essentially no restoring force (it behaves like a dust velocity), so a wrong initial pi' is never damped away. The A0 (unfixed) curve also has an unphysical bump at k=5-10 that goes away with the fix.

The difference is not accuracy noise: accuracy_boost=2, l_accuracy_boost=2 and transfer_high_precision=T leave both rows unchanged to about 1e-3.

S1 stands: wrong physics in a supported model with the default IC type. The fix (EFTpiD1 -> EFTpiD2 in the second term) changes the physics.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; The typo is real and it is in EFTCAMB code; it is not upstream CAMB and not a test artefact. At fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:589 the code reads `EFTpiDfunction = eft_cache%EFTpiD1 + k*k*eft_cache%EFTpiD1`. This function is differentiated by EFTpiDdotFunction (line 632). At line 301 the result is used as (EFTpiCdotFunction + k*k*EFTpiDdotFunction), which is the derivative of the quasi-static denominator C + k^2 D1 + k^4 D2 that lines 297-302 use everywhere else. So the second term must be EFTpiD2. The same line is in pre-rebase B1 (line 570), so it is an old bug and the rebase did not introduce it. The A0_pidfix tree differs from frozen A0 by only this line (checked with diff -r). It applies to EFT_IC_type=1, the default (09_EFTCAMB_main.f90:235), which the K-mimic test models use.

The repro gives exactly the reported numbers. My independent check varied the initial condition (IC) by other routes:
- pi'_ini = 0, set in a separate instrumented tree, so the D function plays no part.
- Turn-on time moved from 1e-8 to 1e-7.

At high k (5-20/Mpc), the fixed build agrees with these routes to a few percent, while A0 is off by 50-135%. A0 is the outlier, and the error comes from the wrong k^4 term.

Caveat for whoever triages this: K-mimic P(k) is strongly IC-sensitive even after the fix. At k = 1-2/Mpc, the fixed build (+0.29/+0.12) still disagrees with pi'=0 (+0.04/+0.05) and with turn-on 1e-7 (+0.04/+0.04). A central-difference derivative variant gives nonsense (+66.9 at k=1). This matches the separate t5 finding ic1-pidot-transient-noisy-coefficients. So the fix removes the large high-k error, but it does not by itself make K-mimic P(k) converged. The finding's line "fixed row = expected" holds only for the k^4 term. The S1 severity stands: this is wrong physics in a supported model at default settings.

### F-016 [S1, confirmed] IC type 1 pi' from finite differences of rapidly varying coefficients puts pi off its attractor (K-mimic turn-on dependence/blow-up, Horndeski pi vs delta_phi mismatch)

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:298` (also: fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:606 (dfridr on C), fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:632 (dfridr on D), fortran/eftcamb/02_utilities.f90:120-160 (dfridr))
- **Track:** t5-robust; reported severity S1, confidence medium
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/ic1-pidot-transient-noisy-coefficients.py`
- **Observed:** Repro (A0). 5_Kmimic_1 P/P_GR-1 at k=1,5,10/Mpc: turn-on 1e-9: +4.0e-2, +1.29e8, +7.8e6 (run status ok, no error); 1e-8 (test default): +0.314, +0.539, +1.36; 1e-7: +0.052, -0.269, -0.989. 5_hdsk_TG_2 (pi, IC1) vs 5_hdsk_TG_1 (delta_phi): TT 1.61e-4 (ell 125), EE 2.01e-4 (ell 127); B1 gives 7e-7. Reference tree A0_icsw (typo fixed, pi'_ini=0, nothing else changed): Kmimic 4.04/4.12/3.40% at k=1 and 5.6/5.2/3.6% at k=5 for the three turn-ons; TG_2 vs TG_1 4.8e-8. With the typo fixed but pi' still differentiated, default Kmimic_1 stays at +28.9% at k=1. get_scale_evolution: C+k^2 D1 = -1.2e8, -4.0e9, -1.6e9 at a = 1.00, 1.12, 1.26 e-9; pi'_ini = 2.2e2 at k=5, constant afterwards.
- **Expected:** Results independent of the turn-on time, no silent divergence, and the delta_phi and pi formulations agreeing to << 1e-4
- **Root cause:** pi'_ini = h0[E (C' + k^2 D') aH/den^2 - E'/den] with C', D' from dfridr (Ridders extrapolation, h = 0.03a, up to 100 step reductions). For K-mimic (Gamma1 ~ 1e14..1e26 at a <= 1e-6) and Horndeski TG at 1e-8 (Gamma1/Gamma2 change slope sign in 103 of 149 samples over a in [1e-8,1e-5]), the coefficients are not smooth on 0.03a. The quasi-static 'attractor' and its derivative are then meaningless, and an O(10^2) pi' is injected. A smoother +-5% centred difference is worse (Kmimic k=1 x67), so the stencil is not the problem. pi'=0 is not a general fix either: 2_PEFT_Omega_const_2 (turn-on 0.01) then changes by 0.22 at low ell and 2.5e-3 at TT ell>=30.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** In 09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90 case(1), check that the QS solution is slowly varying before using its derivative: compute den at a(1 +- 0.05) and, if |d ln den / d ln a| > ~10 or |pi'_ini| > ~10 aH|pi_ini|, set pi' = 0 (or move the turn-on to the first a where the coefficients are smooth) and warn. Also investigate why the K-mimic and Horndeski early-time pi coefficients (Gamma1, Gamma2 tables) are noisy.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; I ran the repro script unchanged and got every number in the report. I then ran three independent checks. They place the defect in EFTCAMB's IC type 1 pi' term at 09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:298-302. It is not a test artefact and not upstream CAMB.

1. Fine turn-on scan, a check the report did not run. Moving the 5_Kmimic_1 turn-on by only 2-20% makes A0 results jump erratically: P/P_GR-1 at k=10 goes 1.36, 13.7, 0.34, 0.36, 2.75. A physical transient would change smoothly, so this is numerical noise being amplified. With pi'_ini=0 (A0_icsw tree, EFT_T5_PIDOT0=1) the same scan is stable at ~4% (k=1) and 4-7% (k=10).

2. Typo-only tree (A0_pidfix: EFTpiD1 corrected to EFTpiD2, pi' still from dfridr). Kmimic still blows up at turn-on 1e-9 (k=5: +1.28e8), stays at +28.9% at k=1 for the default turn-on, and gives -35% / -70% at k=5 / k=10 for turn-on 1.02e-8. TG_2 vs TG_1 is unchanged at 1.61e-4 TT. The A0_icsw tree has both the typo fix and pi'=0; there TG_2 vs TG_1 drops to 4.8e-8. TG_1 evolves delta_phi and never reaches this IC branch. So the pi' term is the trigger, separately from the EFTpiD2 typo.

3. Pre-rebase tree B1. It has the same Kmimic behaviour: +1.29e8 (k=5) at 1e-9, +0.325 / +0.538 / +1.36 at 1e-8, -0.27 / -0.99 at 1e-7. So the K-mimic part is an old EFTCAMB defect, not a regression from the rebase. The TG pi-vs-delta_phi mismatch is new in A0 (B1: 3.2e-7 TT, 6.6e-7 EE). In both trees Gamma1/Gamma2 for TG are equally noisy over a in [1e-8, 1e-5] (about 95-103 slope flips out of 149 samples), so why A0 amplifies the noise and B1 does not is still open.

Caveats on the stated root cause:
- On the 401-point grid of get_eft_functions, K-mimic Gamma1 is smooth (0 slope flips). The noise that drives pi' therefore comes from finer-scale structure in EFTpiC, which I did not check myself. An earlier verifier dump reports local dlnC/dlna of about ±1e5 near a ≈ 9.6e-9.
- The Ridders error estimate (err) is computed and then ignored.

Severity stays S1: a supported model with its own test ini gives turn-on-dependent results that are off by up to 1e8 while returning status ok.
- **Verifier (physics):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; I reran the repro on A0 and got the reported numbers exactly. I then checked how pi evolves on a fine a grid with a separate script, fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/checks/verify/ic1noisy_evol.py.

With IC type 1, pi' starts at a large value and stays constant for two decades in a, so pi grows linearly. That pi feeds back into the source term E, which comes out about 1e11 times larger than in the pi'=0 run at the same a. With pi'_ini = 0 (tree A0_icsw; its diff against A0 adds only env-var switches and the D2 typo fix), pi stays smooth and tiny, and the results barely depend on the turn-on time.

The expected behaviour holds up. The IC1 formula is the analytic tau-derivative of the quasi-static solution -h0 E/den, so the method is right. Two things break it:
- For any model with a nonzero Gamma, the code sets EFTpiEdot = 0, so pi' comes entirely from E den'/den^2.
- den itself is not smooth. For K-mimic, Gamma1 is a smooth power law (~a^-4 on the cache grid), but C = C+k^2D1+k^4D2 is a near-cancelling combination and jumps non-monotonically by factors of 3 to 10 between neighbouring a. The quasi-static pi (piQS) jumps the same way. So dfridr differentiates noise.

Nuance on the root cause: for K-mimic it is C (cancellation), not Gamma1, that is noisy. For Horndeski TG, the Gamma1 table itself flips sign between neighbouring samples.

The TG pi vs delta_phi mismatch depends on the noise. B1's TG Gamma1 is equally noisy but a different realisation, and B1 happens to agree to 3e-7. So B1's agreement does not show the problem is new.

The K-mimic defect predates the rebase: B1 gives the same +32.5% at k=1. It is not accuracy noise and not inherited from CAMB.

S1 stands. The shipped test ini 5_Kmimic_1 at its default turn-on gives P(k) at k=1 of +31% against GR, versus about +4% for the turn-on-independent reference. A valid turn-on of 1e-9 diverges silently (1e8). Both are wrong physics in a supported model with valid input. The proposed fix changes physics and is not yet validated, since pi'=0 shifts 2_PEFT_Omega_const_2 according to the original finder.

Side observation, not checked further: a direct camb.get_results on A0 for 5_Kmimic_1 with turn-on 1e-9 and cp.WantCls=False segfaulted (rc 139). The same run with Cls on worked.

### F-017 [S1, confirmed] EFT_kinetic (scalar no-ghost coefficient) multiplies the 6 H gamma2 Omega' cross term by h0^2 instead of h0: ghost check wrong when gamma2*Omega' != 0

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/06_abstract_EFTCAMB_model.f90:690` (also: fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:319 (decision uses EFT_kinetic))
- **Track:** t6-stability; reported severity S1, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/eft-kinetic-cross-term-extra-h0.py [tree]`
- **Observed:** A0, RPH with alpha_M = 0.5 Omega_DE. Model A (3_RPH_MK_OmegaDE_1, alpha_K = 0.1 Omega_DE): code/analytic = 1.018, 1.454, 2.723, 4.376, 6.285 at a = 0.1, 0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 1. Model B (alpha_K = -0.2 Omega_DE^2, so D < 0 at all a): code EFT_kinetic = +4.93e-7 at a = 1 vs analytic -1.79e-7 (ratio -2.749 at every a); code verdict: stable. Independent cross-check: 36(1+Omega)^2 EFTpiA1/EFT_kinetic = 0.400 for 3_RPH_MB_OmegaDE_1, 0.33-1.0 for 3_RPH_KT_OmegaDE_1, 0.80-1.0 for 3_RPH_MK_power_1.
- **Expected:** EFT_kinetic = 18 (M*^2/m0^2)^3 H^2 (alpha_K + 6 alpha_B^2) (EFTCAMB alpha_B convention): ratio 1 at all a; model B rejected as a scalar ghost.
- **Root cause:** Parenthesis error: a**2*h0_Mpc*(h0_Mpc*(3 Gamma2^2 + 8 Gamma1 (1+Omega-Gamma4) + 6 adotoa Gamma2 OmegaP)) puts the cross term inside the h0^2 bracket. With M_2^3 = gamma2 m0^2 H0 the term needs exactly one h0 (dimension Mpc^-2; the code term is Mpc^-3), so it is effectively suppressed by h0 ~ 2e-4. Inherited from B1 (identical line).
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** Close the h0^2 bracket after the gamma1 term: ... 8._dl*EFTGamma1V*(1+EFTOmegaV-EFTGamma4V)) + 6._dl*adotoa*EFTGamma2V*EFTOmegaP)) (see work/t6-stability/fix_fixkin.diff). Tested in work/t6-stability/fixkin: ratio 1.000 at all a for both models, model B rejected, 36(1+Omega)^2 A1/EFT_kinetic = 1.000. All 73 shipped models keep their verdicts (73/73 stable at defaults; the same 3 hdsk_TG rejections with stability forced).
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; The finding is a real defect in the code under audit at fortran/eftcamb/06_abstract_EFTCAMB_model.f90:690-695, and the root cause is right. The parenthesization puts 6*adotoa*EFTGamma2V*EFTOmegaP inside the h0_Mpc*(h0_Mpc*(...)) bracket, so the cross term picks up h0^2 when it should have h0. The same line exists in B1 (pre-rebase line 556), so the bug is inherited.

Independent check on a different path: the code's own pi-equation coefficient EFTpiA1 (line 515) contains (adotoa*OmegaP + h0*gamma2)^2. Expanding that square gives a cross term with a single h0. When gamma3 = gamma4 = 0 this means EFT_kinetic should equal 36(1+Omega)^2*A1 exactly. That holds to 1e-4 for the single-h0 formula and fails for the code's value. So the code is internally inconsistent: the perturbation dynamics use the single-h0 form, while the ghost test in 09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:319 uses the wrong one.

The cross term is negative and dominant in these models (cross/total up to -5.3), so the effect is large. A ghost model (alpha_K < 0, D < 0 at all a) gets EFT_kinetic > 0 and is accepted as stable. The ghost condition therefore gives wrong verdicts for supported RPH/Horndeski models with a running Planck mass, which justifies S1 since it corrupts the stability prior. The integrated perturbations themselves are not affected, because A1 is correct.

One test-side problem, not a code defect: the repro script hard-codes the old /Users/marco/Desktop/IW/... parameter path, so as given it fails with "include file not found". I ran a copy with only that path corrected: fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/checks/verify/vrf_kinetic_repro_pathfix.py.
- **Verifier (physics):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; This is a real defect. I checked the units: EFTc, adotoa^2 OmegaP^2 a^2, h0^2 gamma2^2 a^2 and h0^2 gamma1 a^2 are all Mpc^-2, but the code's h0^2 * adotoa * gamma2 * OmegaP * a^2 term is Mpc^-3. That cannot be right. An independent algebra check gives the same answer. Take the code's own EFTpiA1 (06_abstract_EFTCAMB_model.f90:515), whose squared term is (adotoa OmegaP + h0 gamma2)^2 with exactly one h0 in the cross term. Set Gamma3 = Gamma4 = 0 and expand 36(1+Omega)^2 A1. The result is 36c(1+Omega)^2 + 27(1+Omega) a^2 (H Omega' + h0 gamma2)^2 + 72 a^2 h0^2 gamma1 (1+Omega)^2. That equals the EFT_kinetic expression only if the cross term is 6 H h0 gamma2 Omega', with a single h0. So the misplaced bracket is a parenthesis bug, not a convention. The expected value is also correct on physics grounds. In both models alpha_B = 0, so the no-ghost quantity D is just alpha_K whatever alpha_B convention is used (Bellini-Sawicki 3/2 alpha_B^2 or EFTCAMB 6 alpha_B^2). With alpha_K < 0 at every a, model B is unambiguously a ghost. The code still calls it stable, so the ghost check passes ghost models in a supported model (RPH with running Planck mass), and they would get through stability filtering in MCMC. The same line is in B1 (eftcamb-pre-rebase, line 554-556), so the bug was inherited and not introduced by the rebase. S1 stands: the stability verdict is wrong for supported models. Cls are not directly affected, since EFT_kinetic only feeds the decision at 09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:319. The proposed fix moves the cross term out of the h0^2 bracket and is correct. One hygiene note: the repro script still hardcodes PAR=/Users/marco/Desktop/IW/..., which no longer exists, because the repo is now at /Users/marco/IW/... I ran a path-fixed copy.

### F-018 [S1, confirmed] derivs uses pi'' in EFTeomM/EFTeomNdot before computing it: ISW source, sigma'', PsiDot miss the pi'' term

- **Location:** `fortran/equations.f90:2989` (also: fortran/equations.f90:3179-3182 (dz with EFTeomM), fortran/equations.f90:3741-3763 (EFTsigmadotdot, EFTISW with EFTeomNdot), fortran/equations.f90:3047-3050 (RSA dz), fortran/eftcamb/06_abstract_EFTCAMB_model.f90:443-473 (EFTeomM/EFTeomNdot use eft_cache%pidotdot), fortran/eftcamb/11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:369 (python fork zeroes yprime too))
- **Track:** t8a-equations; reported severity S1, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/output-pidotdot-zero.py`
- **Observed:** Horava, A0 vs the same code with the correct pi'' (two-pass): max|dTT/TT| 4.58e-3 at ell=4 (5_Horava_eta_4; +3.6e-3 at ell=2, +1.4e-3 at ell=30), 2.27e-4 (eta_3); 5_Horava_SolSyst_3 -2.6% at ell=2-3 and -1.1% at ell=1000. EE unchanged. Cached EFTeomNdot vs finite difference of EFTeomN: residual 0.97-1.8 (relative), down to 0.02-0.35 with the missing term restored. EFTISW vs d(EFTLensing)/dtau: 2.2e-2 for Horava_eta_3 vs 1e-6 for GR/Horndeski controls.
- **Expected:** EFTeomM, EFTeomNdot (hence EFTISW, EFTsigmadotdot, PsiDot, counts ISW/redshift window terms, dz) evaluated with the current pi''; results independent of call order.
- **Root cause:** In the pi-field branch of block 'initialization of DE perturbations' (equations.f90:2984-2995), pidotdot = ayprime(EV%w_ix+1) is read and compute_Einstein_factors is called before the pi equation (3253-3262) writes ayprime(w_ix+1). output(), outtransf() and the python fork zero yprime first, so sources use pi''=0. Inside RK45 stages the slot holds the previous step's stage (stage 2: the error estimate). Same code in the pre-rebase branch, so not a rebase regression. Affects models with Gamma4+2Gamma5 != 0 (Horava, beyond Horndeski) via EFTeomNdot and Omega'/Gamma2 via EFTeomM->dz (f(R): 1e-7).
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** After the pi equation stores pidot/pidotdot (equations.f90:3262), recompute the pi''-dependent factors:
             EV%eft_cache%pidotdot = ayprime(EV%w_ix + 1)
+            if (EV%EFTCAMBactive) then
+                call CP%EFTCAMB%model%compute_Einstein_factors(a, CP%eft_par_cache, EV%eft_cache)
+                EV%eft_cache%dz = 1._dl/EV%eft_cache%EFTeomU*(-2._dl*adotoa*(1._dl + EV%eft_cache%EFTeomY)*z + etak - &
+                    0.5_dl/k/(1._dl + EV%eft_cache%EFTOmegaV)*(grhog_t*clxg + grhor_t*clxr + 3._dl*dgpnu) - &
+                    1.5_dl/k/(1._dl + EV%eft_cache%EFTOmegaV)*EV%eft_cache%EFTeomM/CP%eft_par_cache%h0_Mpc)
+                dz = EV%eft_cache%dz
+            end if
and drop the read of ayprime at 2989 (set pidotdot=0 there). Tested in tree work/t8a-equations/FX: reproduces the two-pass reference to 6e-16 (eta_4) / 9e-16 (eta_3); bit-identical to A0 for 1_EFT_GR, 2_PEFT_Omega_const_1, 2_PEFT_gamma3_const_1, 3_RPH_KT_power_1, 5_hdsk_TG_2; f(R) changes 1e-10. A complete fix should also move the pi equation before the RSA z/dz block (3036-3050), which still uses the stale EFTeomM.
- **Verifier (physics):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; The bug is real and reproduced. In derivs (equations.f90:2989-2995, pi-field branch), the code sets eft_cache%pidotdot = ayprime(EV%w_ix+1) and calls compute_Einstein_factors before the pi equation writes that slot (3253-3262). output() (equations.f90:1960), outtransf (2639) and the python fork (11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:369) all zero yprime first, so the line-of-sight sources see pi''=0. The pre-rebase branch has the same lines (2777 and 3011), so this is not a rebase regression.

The expected behaviour is correct.
- EFTeomNdot is by construction d(EFTeomN)/dtau. EFTeomN contains (Gamma4+2Gamma5)*pi', so its derivative must contain (Gamma4+2Gamma5)*pi''.
- EFTeomM is the pi part of the h'' Einstein equation and contains Omega'*pi'' and Gamma2*pi'' (06_abstract:443-473).
- There is no circularity that would justify a lagged value. The pi equation coefficients (EFTpiA..E) depend on z, dgrho and the clx terms, not on dz, EFTeomM or EFTeomNdot. So pi'' can be computed explicitly first and then used.

It is not accuracy noise. With accuracy_boost=2 and l_accuracy_boost=2, the TT shift for Horava_eta_4 is the same as at default accuracy (+4.70e-3 at ell=4).

Scope is narrower than the root-cause text implies.
- Horava evolution is untouched (EE identical to 4e-16). The error is confined to the sources, i.e. the ISW/PsiDot terms through EFTeomNdot.
- The PEFT Gamma4 and Gamma5 test models (2_PEFT_gamma4_const_1, 2_PEFT_gamma5_const_1) are bit-identical between A0 and FX. In them pi is apparently never sourced on an LCDM background, so pi''=0 there anyway.
- The Omega-only model 2_PEFT_Omega_lin_1 changes by only 4.4e-11.
- Affected in practice: Horava (all eta and SolSyst inis) and presumably beyond Horndeski (no test ini, not checked).

Severity: S1 stands. It is wrong physics in a supported model on every run, not an edge case. The size is modest for representative parameters: 0.46% low-ell TT for eta_4, 2e-4 for eta_3. It is larger for SolSyst_3, but that model is known not to be converged.

Fix caveat: the tested FX patch writes the non-RSA dz formula unconditionally after the pi equation. In the radiation streaming approximation (RSA) regime, the source code later uses dz (qrdot/qgdot = -4*dz/3 at 3649/3655), which should come from the RSA formula at 3047. A complete fix should branch on EV%no_nu_multpoles, or, as the finding itself suggests, move the pi equation ahead of the z/dz block. fix_class physics-changing is correct.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; The code reads the stale value as described. In derivs, equations.f90:2989 sets `pidotdot = ayprime(EV%w_ix + 1)` and then calls compute_Einstein_factors (06_abstract_EFTCAMB_model.f90:443-473). EFTeomM and EFTeomNdot there both use eft_cache%pidotdot, through the Gamma4V, 2*Gamma5V, aomegaP and Gamma2V terms. The slot ayprime(w_ix+1) is only written later, by the pi equation at 3253-3262. output() (equations.f90:1960), outtransf() (2639) and the python fork (11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:369) all zero yprime before calling derivs, so the line-of-sight sources (EFTISW, EFTsigmadotdot, PsiDot) are built with pi''=0. The same read exists in the pre-rebase sources (eq_pre.f90:2777), so this is an old EFTCAMB defect, not a rebase regression, and it is not upstream CAMB.

I ran the repro as given and got the stated numbers exactly. I then ran my own check by a different method on A0 alone, with no patched tree. Physically, the ISW source EFTISW must equal d(EFTLensing)/dtau. In a clean non-Horndeski model (PEFT Omega linear 0.05 + Gamma5=-0.02, LCDM designer background, Hdot consistent) the A0 output breaks this by 70-100%. Adding back only the missing analytic term k(G4+2G5)pi''/(1+Omega)/(X h0) brings the residual down to the finite-difference noise floor. Controls (GR, Omega-only) agree to 1e-7 to 2e-5.

The Horava models cannot test this cleanly: their ISW-vs-lensing residual (~0.23) is dominated by the separately filed Horava Hdot inconsistency, and restoring the term does not lower it there. That does not weaken the finding; the clean model settles it.

On severity: TT changes by 0.46% at low ell in the supported 5_Horava_eta_4 model, and the defect affects every model with Gamma4+2Gamma5 != 0 and a nonzero pi. Both the size and the reach justify S1. One caveat: SolSyst_3, the largest effect at 2.6%, is a model already known to be unconverged.

Not independently verified: the claim about stale stage values inside the RK45 stages, and the RSA dz remark.

### F-019 [S1, confirmed] Horndeski (EFTflag=5, default Horndeski_shooting=F): the EFT background's own H0 never reaches CP%H0, so sigma8, h-unit P(k), Omega_de and theta->H0 solvers use the wrong H0

- **Location:** `fortran/results.f90:584` (also: fortran/eftcamb/08f_full_models/008p0_Horndeski.f90:2036, fortran/results.f90:568, camb/model.py:626 (set_H0_for_theta f(H0)))
- **Track:** t8b-integration; reported severity S1, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/hdsk-h0-not-propagated.py`
- **Observed:** 5_hdsk_TG_1: results.Params.H0 = 70.8671 but H(z=0) from the EFT background (dtauda) = 85.5373 km/s/Mpc. Reported sigma8 = 0.7948 vs sigma(R = 8 Mpc/h_true) = 0.9057 (-12.2%). cosmomc_theta = 0.010854335 for input H0 = 60 and for 80, i.e. independent of CP%H0. Theta round trip: TG_1/TG_2 give CAMBParamRangeError 'No solution for H0'; TG_3 returns H0 = 43.16 for input 70.87 (H(z=0)=73.08).
- **Expected:** One H0 everywhere: CAMB's H0 (used for h, sigma8 R=8 Mpc/h, P(k) in h/Mpc, Omega_x = omega_x/h^2, Halofit/HMcode) equal to H(z=0) of the background used in the perturbations, or a clear error when they disagree.
- **Root cause:** 008p0_Horndeski.f90:2036-2037 sets params_cache%h0_Mpc = H(a=1) of the integrated background (shooting off). SetParams (results.f90:568-569, 584) copies CP%H0 into eft_par_cache before initialize_background and never reads the model's H0 back, while dtauda uses the model's H table. The pre-rebase B1 shows the same H(z=0)=85.537, so the defect predates the rebase. CAMB v2's theta->H0 solvers vary CP%H0, which does not affect the Horndeski background, so they cannot work.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** After initialize_background in CAMBdata_SetParams (results.f90 ~584): if abs(eft_par_cache%h0/CP%H0 - 1) > 1e-6, either (a) set P%H0 = eft_par_cache%h0 and redo the H0-dependent setup (grhocrit-based Omega_de, grhov, h-dependent fields), e.g. by re-entering SetParams once with the corrected H0, or (b) call GlobalError('EFTCAMB: Horndeski background H0 (x) differs from input H0 (y); use Horndeski_shooting=T or set hubble consistently', error_unsupported_params). In camb/model.py set_H0_for_theta, raise a clear error for models whose background fixes H0.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; I ran the repro as given on A0 and got exactly the stated numbers. I then checked the same claim a second way, from the stored A0 and B1 reference npz files, using H0 recomputed from the EFT cache and sigma8 integrated from the stored linear P(k). Both checks give the same mismatch.

The defect is in EFTCAMB code, not upstream CAMB and not the harness:
- The model overwrites params_cache%h0 and h0_Mpc with H(a=1) of its own integrated background at 008p0_Horndeski.f90:2036-2037.
- The integrated background feeds dtauda and the EFT perturbation terms in equations.f90 through eft_par_cache%h0_Mpc.
- CAMBdata_SetParams copies CP%H0 into the cache (results.f90:568-569), calls initialize_background (results.f90:584) and never reads the model's H0 back. h units, sigma8 (R = 8 Mpc/h), Omega_x and the theta->H0 solver therefore use the input `hubble`.

Horndeski_shooting only switches the model to solve-only mode (line 2054 returns early). It does not shoot for H0. Neither the run log nor the code issues any warning about the mismatch.

B1 gives the same H(z=0), so the defect predates the rebase.

One mitigating point: the CMB perturbations are internally consistent with the model's own H. Only the H0/h-labelled outputs are wrong. Still, sigma8 is silently 2-12% off on the shipped test inis at default settings, and theta->H0 cannot work. So S1 stands. It could arguably be S2 if Horndeski users are expected to set `hubble` consistently by hand, but nothing documents or enforces that.
- **Verifier (physics):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; I ran the repro on A0 and checked it against the reference npz files for A0 and B1. Every claim holds.

Why the expected behaviour is right: for a Horndeski model defined by its G functions, the matter densities are physical (CAMB's grhoc, grhob and grhog do not depend on H0). The scalar field's energy density today then fixes H(a=1), so H0 is an output of the model. The code itself says this: 008p0_Horndeski.f90:2036-2037 overwrites params_cache%h0 and h0_Mpc with H(a=1) of the integrated background. CAMBdata_SetParams (results.f90:568-569, 584) fills eft_par_cache from CP%H0 before initialize_background and never reads the model's value back. CP%H0 therefore stays at the input value, while dtauda and the perturbations use the model's H table. Everything CAMB derives from H0 is computed with an H0 that is not the background's: h, sigma8 (R = 8 Mpc/h), P(k) in h/Mpc, Omega_de = grhov/grhocrit, and the Halofit/HMcode Omega_m. Nothing warns about it. The shipped test ini triggers it at a 20% level.

None of the alternative explanations hold up:
- Not accuracy noise: the mismatch is 85.54 vs 70.87.
- Not a units mismatch: eft_adotoa*c at a=1 equals bg_H(z=0) exactly.
- Not caused by the rebase: B1 gives the same numbers.
- Not documented as intended: I found no documentation saying the user must tune `hubble`.

Horndeski_shooting=T is not a remedy. It makes initialize_background return right after the background solve (line 2054, "only solve background without computing EFT functions"). That mode is a hook for an external shooting loop, so the error message suggested in the proposed fix should not recommend it. Option (b) of the fix (a clear error, or set P%H0 from the model and redo the setup) is sound.

Severity S1 stands. sigma8 and h-unit outputs are wrong by about 12% in a supported model, using its own test ini at default settings. The Cls are unaffected. One could argue for S2 on the grounds that a user who tunes `hubble` by hand avoids the problem, but the code gives no hint that this is required.

### F-020 [S1, confirmed] thetastar/cosmomc_theta ini keys (new in CAMB v2) solve H0 before EFTCAMB is initialised: wrong H0 for every EFT model with a non-LCDM background

- **Location:** `fortran/camb.f90:711` (also: fortran/camb.f90:379-395 (CAMB_ReadParamFile EFTCAMB init after CAMB_ReadParams), fortran/camb.f90:885 (CAMB_RunFromIni), fortran/camb.f90:315 (H0_range = [10,100]))
- **Track:** t8c-features; reported severity S1, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/ini-theta-h0-solved-without-eft.py`
- **Observed:** Requested thetastar=0.010411 through the ini (camb.read_ini path):
- 2_PEFT_wDE_wCDM_1: H0=63.8294, resulting thetastar 0.0106019 (rel +1.83e-2); the correct H0 is 58.8686.
- 5_quint_exponential: rel +6.65e-3 (H0 63.83 vs 61.89).
- 5_Kmouflage_1: rel -3.49e-2 (H0 70.97 vs 84.19).
- c7, cosmomc_theta: f(R) wCDM +8.3e-3, hdsk_TG +4.3e-2.
- Controls: GR and LCDM-background models -8e-9.
- **Expected:** Final theta equals the requested value to the brentq tolerance, |rel| < 1e-5. This is what python set_cosmology gives for the same models (<=1.3e-7).
- **Root cause:** CAMB_ReadParams solves theta at camb.f90:711-713 (CAMB_SetH0ForTheta -> SetParams(background_only)) using P%EFTCAMB as it is at that moment: freshly defaulted, EFTflag=0, so the background is LCDM / CAMB DE. EFTCAMB is read from the same ini only afterwards: in CAMB_ReadParamFile (camb.f90:379-395, the python read_ini path) and in CAMB_RunFromIni (camb.f90:885, then ~925-940).
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** In CAMB_ReadParams, initialise EFTCAMB before solving theta:

    if (has_thetastar .or. has_cosmomc_theta) then
        if (.not. allocated(P%EFTCAMB)) allocate(P%EFTCAMB)
        if (.not. allocated(P%eft_par_cache)) allocate(P%eft_par_cache)
        call P%EFTCAMB%EFTCAMB_init_from_file(Ini)
        if (P%EFTCAMB%EFTflag /= 0) then
            call P%EFTCAMB%EFTCAMB_init_model_from_file(Ini, eft_error)
            if (eft_error > 0) then; ErrMsg='EFTCAMB: invalid model parameters'; return; end if
        end if
        if (.not. CAMB_SetH0ForTheta(P, theta, ErrMsg, cosmomc_approx=has_cosmomc_theta)) return
    end if

Also, in CAMB_SetH0ForTheta, for EFTflag/=0 raise H0_range(1) to 110*sqrt(ombh2+omch2+omnuh2) as python set_cosmology does, so the bracket end is not unstable.

Tested in work/t8c-features/fixA:
- theta rel err for the 4 models: <=6.3e-8.
- A run without theta (4_Des_F_R_wCDM_1 with hubble) is bit-identical to A0.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; I ran the repro as given and got the reported numbers. My independent check, fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/checks/verify/verify_ini_theta_h0.py, takes the H0 that the thetastar ini produces and feeds it back as hubble=H0_ini. With EFTflag=0 that H0 reproduces the requested theta to about 1e-8 for every model. So CAMB_SetH0ForTheta solved H0 on the GR/LCDM background. With the model's own EFTflag, the same H0 gives the reported theta errors. I also ran brentq on H0 using hubble= ini runs with EFT on. It finds the same correct H0 as python set_cosmology to within 1e-3.

Reading the code confirms the order. CAMB_ReadParams calls CAMB_SetH0ForTheta at camb.f90:711-713. EFTCAMB is read from the ini only after that returns: CAMB_ReadParamFile does it at camb.f90:381-395 (the python read_ini path), and CAMB_RunFromIni at camb.f90:885, which re-allocates P%EFTCAMB at about line 891 and initialises it after that.

B1 (CAMB 1.6.5) has no thetastar or cosmomc_theta ini keys. They arrive with CAMB v2. The defect is therefore in how EFTCAMB's hooks order themselves against this new upstream path, not in upstream CAMB itself. It is not a test artefact either: my inis use the plain DEFAULT()/flattened model inis plus a single thetastar key.

Severity stays S1. The input is valid: thetastar is a documented CAMB v2 ini key. The result is silently wrong physics, a 0.7-3.5% theta error, for every EFT model whose background is not LCDM, and nothing warns the user. One limit: I did not exercise the standalone Fortran executable path (CAMB_RunFromIni) at runtime. I checked it by code reading only, and it has the same ordering.
- **Verifier (physics):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; The finding holds. I checked the code under audit (HEAD f6f4ba9 in /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB). In fortran/camb.f90:711-713, CAMB_ReadParams calls CAMB_SetH0ForTheta, and that solve runs the background with P%EFTCAMB as it is at that moment. In both callers the EFTCAMB model is read from the ini only after CAMB_ReadParams returns:
- CAMB_ReadParamFile (lines 379-395) reads it afterwards.
- CAMB_RunFromIni (line 885 onwards) also deallocates P%EFTCAMB and reallocates it afterwards.
So H0 is solved for an LCDM / CAMB-DE background and then used with the EFT background.

The expected behaviour is physically correct. thetastar = r_s(z*)/D_M(z*) depends on H(z) through D_M. When the requested theta is given, H0 must be solved with the actual model background, and EFTCAMB's own python set_cosmology path does this to about 1e-7.

I ruled out the alternative explanations:
- Accuracy noise: errors are 1e-2 to 3e-2, against a brentq tolerance of about 1e-7.
- Normalisation: thetastar/100 is applied to both paths, and the GR control agrees to 8e-9.
- Script bug: in the ini path, wCDM and GR get the identical H0=63.8294, which is the signature of solving with LCDM.

It is a defect of EFTCAMB's integration with CAMB v2. The ini thetastar/cosmomc_theta keys are new in v2, so the pre-rebase code had no such path. The hook order was not adapted.

Severity S1 is justified. The input is valid and documented, the error is silent and large (theta off by up to 3.5%, H0 off by up to 13 km/s/Mpc), and it affects every EFT model whose background is not LCDM. One could argue for S2 because the common route (python set_cosmology / Cobaya) is correct, but the ini/camb.exe route is a primary EFTCAMB workflow.

The proposed fix is sound in outline: initialise EFTCAMB before the theta solve in CAMB_ReadParams. CAMB_RunFromIni re-initialises EFTCAMB afterwards anyway, so the fix does not conflict with it. I did not test the fix build or the camb.exe RunFromIni path at run time; the camb.exe conclusion rests on reading the code.

### F-021 [S1, confirmed] EFTCAMB getters, and get_results after a failed initialize_parameters, kill Python (SIGSEGV)

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:200` (also: fortran/eftcamb/11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:214, fortran/eftcamb/11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:228, fortran/eftcamb/11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:242, fortran/eftcamb/11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:139-146, fortran/results.f90:584)
- **Track:** t9-wrapper; reported severity S1, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/eft-getters-gr-segfault.py [--tree PATH]`
- **Observed:** camb.CAMBparams().EFTCAMB.num_params(), param_names(), param_labels() and param_values() are each killed by SIGSEGV (EFTflag=0; model_name() returns ''). After initialize_parameters(p, {'EFTflag':1,'PureEFTmodel':9}) raises ValueError, EFTflag stays 1 with no model. The 4 getters and camb.get_results(p) then all die with SIGSEGV. probe_errors: 4/4 '+reuse' cases (bad EFTflag/PureEFTmodel/Omega/Designer) SIGSEGV.
- **Expected:** 0 / [] for a GR object, or a Python exception; get_results either computes GR or raises a CAMBError.
- **Root cause:** EFTCAMB_get_num_params (l.200), get_param_names (l.214), get_param_labels (l.228) and get_param_values (l.242) dereference EFTCAMB%model without checking allocated() (get_model_name does check). initialize_EFTCAMB_from_py (l.139-146) never resets EFTflag or the model when init_model_from_file fails. CAMBdata_SetParams (results.f90:549-584) then dereferences this%CP%EFTCAMB%model.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** See work/t9-wrapper/fix_fortran.diff. In 11_EFTCAMB_python.f90, guard every getter with 'if (allocated(EFTCAMB%model))' (default 0, '' or 0.0). In initialize_EFTCAMB_from_py, when eft_error>0 set EFTflag=0 and deallocate the model. In results.f90 before l.553, add 'if (EFTflag/=0 .and. .not. allocated(model)) GlobalError(...); return'. Tested in the A0fix tree: every row returns rc=0 with a value. The guard also turns the pickle/deepcopy SIGSEGV (another track's repro) into a CAMBError.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; I ran the repro unchanged on A0 and got exactly the reported table. I then ran my own check, which uses other trigger paths and other trees, and the debugger points to the lines the finding names. This is a real defect in EFTCAMB's own code (the python bridge plus the EFTCAMB block in CAMBdata_SetParams). It is not upstream CAMB and not a bug in the test. The pre-rebase EFTCAMB (B1) segfaults on the same num_params call, so the bug predates the CAMB v2 rebase. S1 holds: num_params() on a default CAMBparams(), or get_results after a ValueError that the user caught, are valid API calls, and they kill the interpreter instead of raising an exception. One correction to the attribution: in the failed-init case the crash in results.f90 is at line 584 (the call to model%initialize_background), not at "549-584" in general. The finding's other_locations already lists 584. The proposed fix, as built in work/t9-wrapper/A0fix, turns every crash into a value or a CAMBError.
- **Verifier (physics):** CONFIRMED, severity S1; I ran the repro on A0 and it reproduces exactly. The expectation is correct and not a convention question. EFTCAMB_get_model_name already guards with allocated(EFTCAMB%model) and returns '', so the documented intent is that a GR object, or one with no model, is a valid object to query. num_params, param_names, param_labels and param_values (11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:200/214/228/242) dereference EFTCAMB%model without that guard, so a default camb.CAMBparams() kills the interpreter. No physics, accuracy or normalisation effect could explain this: it is a null dereference.

The failed-init path is also real. init_EFTCAMB_model_from_file returns early when EFTCAMB_allocate_model fails, leaving no model. initialize_EFTCAMB_from_py (l.139-146) does not reset EFTflag, so the object keeps EFTflag=1. CAMBdata_SetParams then calls this%CP%EFTCAMB%model%initialize_background (results.f90 ~l.584) and gets SIGSEGV. That path needs the caller to catch and ignore the ValueError, so on its own it is closer to S2. The getter crash on a default GR object is reachable with fully valid input and kills the process, which meets the S1 crash criterion. It does not corrupt physics output.

The same getter SIGSEGVs occur on B1 (pre-rebase EFTCAMB), so this is a long-standing EFTCAMB defect, not one introduced by the rebase. B1's get_results rows ended with rc=1, not a crash; I did not investigate why, since B1 is outside the code under audit. The proposed fix (allocated() guards with defaults, resetting EFTflag/deallocating on eft_error>0, and a GlobalError guard in CAMBdata_SetParams) is appropriate and safe-local. I did not rebuild to re-test the fix.

### F-022 [S2, confirmed] EFTCAMB.get_evolution sorts the requested times silently; for decreasing a or unsorted eta, the returned rows do not match the caller's times

- **Location:** `camb/eftcamb.py:316` (also: camb/eftcamb.py:290 (get_scale_evolution))
- **Track:** t0-layout; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/get-evolution-sort-misalign.py`
- **Observed:** Designer f(R): get_scale_evolution(results,[0.1],a=1/(1+z)) with z=[0,0.5,1,2,5] returns a=[0.16667,0.33333,0.5,0.66667,1.0] for requested a=[1,0.667,0.5,0.333,0.167]. Max rel error in a is 5.0, and adotoa in row 0 is 3.126e-4 instead of 2.335e-4. get_evolution with eta=[7609.9,13995.2,10682.0] returns tau=[7609.9,10682.0,13995.2], max |dtau|=3313 Mpc. Same in hdsk_TG and K-mouflage (a=logspace(-3,0)[::-1] gives rel 999).
- **Expected:** Output column j corresponds to the caller's eta[j] / a[j] (max rel <= 1e-6).
- **Root cause:** get_evolution does times = np.sort(times) and returns the Fortran results in sorted order without restoring the input order. The Fortran fill order is fine: [k][time], k-major, timestep_cache((q_ix-1)*neta+j), and k is not reordered. results.conformal_time returns the caller's order, so decreasing a reaches the reordering silently.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** In camb/eftcamb.py get_evolution:
-        times = np.sort(times)
+        order = np.argsort(times, kind="stable")
+        times = np.asfortranarray(times[order])
 ...
         results = np.ctypeslib.as_array(results)
+        unsorted = np.empty_like(results)
+        unsorted[:, order] = results
-        return fields, results
+        return fields, unsorted
Diff: work/t0-layout/fix_eftcamb_py.diff. Tested: a echo 1.3e-9, tau echo exact. Sorted inputs are unchanged.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; The finding holds. The defect is in EFTCAMB's own Python wrapper, not in upstream CAMB and not in the test.

Root cause: camb/eftcamb.py:316 runs `times = np.sort(times)`. Lines 328-331 then return the Fortran output in that sorted order and never put it back in the caller's order. The sort itself is needed. In 11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:363-376, GetEFTOutputEvolutionFork integrates forward from tau to each eta(j) in turn, so the times have to be increasing. What is missing is the inverse permutation on the way out.

The Fortran fill order is fine. Rows are filled k-major at `timestep_cache((q_ix-1)*neta+j)`, which matches the Python array `EFTCAMB_timestep_cache * nt * nk`, and k is never reordered. I checked this with an unsorted k list: the k field matched the request exactly. get_scale_evolution (line 290) passes `conformal_time(1/a-1)` straight in, so a decreasing a array, such as a=1/(1+z) for increasing z, comes back reversed with no warning.

Why this is a defect and not a design choice:
- The docstring never says the output is sorted.
- The matching upstream API, CAMBdata.get_time_evolution (camb/results.py:608-638), sorts the times and then restores the caller's order with i_rev.
- EFTCAMB's own get_eft_functions keeps the caller's order. Using the same a array in get_eft_functions and get_scale_evolution therefore gives rows that do not line up.

The bug is not a regression from the rebase: the same np.sort line is in B1 (pre-rebase EFTCAMB), and A0's camb/eftcamb.py is identical to 5bef49a. The one in-repo caller, material/test.py, passes increasing a and plots against the returned 'a' field, so it is unaffected.

Severity: S2 is right. Each row's values are internally consistent, since every row carries its own a and tau. But any caller who indexes the result by their own unsorted or decreasing times gets silently wrong rows, and that input is natural.

Proposed fix: the t0-layout diff (argsort with kind="stable", then `unsorted[:, order] = results`). I re-implemented it as a local monkeypatch that calls the same Fortran routine; the repo was not edited. On all 4 models it echoes the requested times exactly. Its values, put back into sorted order, are bit-identical to the current code's sorted call. On already-sorted input its output equals the current code's, difference 0. It is safe-local.

### F-023 [S2, confirmed] Full-mapping Hdotdotdot (third conformal derivative of H) is wrong: typos, a wrong prefactor, and EFTLambdadotdot not yet computed; designer f(R) never sets it

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/06p1_abstract_EFTCAMB_full_map.f90:145` (also: fortran/eftcamb/06_abstract_EFTCAMB_model.f90:1128-1150 (positivity bounds use Hdotdotdot), fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:553-574 (call order: H derivs before additional derivs), fortran/eftcamb/11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:438 (get_eft_functions path), fortran/eftcamb/07f_designer_models/007p3_Designer_fR.f90 (Hdotdotdot never set), fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/checks/t0-layout/hdotdotdot_formula.py (python validation of the corrected formula))
- **Track:** t0-layout; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/fullmap-hdotdotdot-wrong.py`
- **Observed:** get_eft_functions, Hdotdotdot field vs Richardson finite difference of the Hdotdot field (d/dtau = a adotoa d/da), a=0.05: 5_Kmouflage_1 -1.8835e-11 vs -8.4077e-14 (max rel 223), 5_Kmouflage_2 -1.3053e-10 vs -1.5859e-13 (822). 5_quint_exponential (Omega=0) differs by 1.2e-2. 4_Des_F_R_LCDM_1 field is 0 everywhere vs -8.07e-14. Controls Hdot and Hdotdot agree with the numerical derivatives (fR 5e-13; full models 1-2%). A term-by-term python transcription of the shipped formula reproduces the field (-1.8836e-11).
- **Expected:** Hdotdotdot = d(Hdotdot)/dtau. The corrected derivation evaluated in python matches the numerical derivative to 1.9e-3 (Kmouflage_1) and 1.2e-3 (Kmouflage_2).
- **Root cause:** With Hdotdot = N/denom (denom = 1+Omega+a Omega'/2), Hdotdotdot should be (dN/dtau - Hdotdot ddenom/dtau)/denom. The code has: '3.5_dl*a*EFTOmegaP*1.5_dl*a2*EFTOmegaPP' twice, where it should be '+'. It has '-a*adotoa2*(adotoa2 + 3.0_dl*eft_cache%adotoa)*S3', where it should be '-0.5*a*adotoa2*(adotoa2+3*Hdot)*S3'. Its first term uses N1/2 = -a H (1.5 Omega' + 0.25 a Omega'') instead of -ddenom/dtau = -a H (1.5 Omega' + 0.5 a Omega''), with a bracket that is not N (gpresdotdotm_t instead of gpresdotm_t, and -0.5*(Lambdadot+2H Lambda) inside -0.5*()). Also, EFTLambdadotdot for full models is computed only in compute_additional_derivs, after compute_H_derivs, and only with mass stability on (09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:553-574), so it is 0 when Hdotdotdot is evaluated. 007p3_Designer_fR never sets Hdotdotdot. The same formula is in B1. Consumers: the generic positivity bounds (06_abstract_EFTCAMB_model.f90:1128-1150, when EFT_positivity_bounds=T) and the python output. Cls are not affected.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** Replace the Hdotdotdot block with:
    denom = one_plus_omega + 0.5_dl*a*eft_cache%EFTOmegaP
    denomdot = a*eft_cache%adotoa*(1.5_dl*eft_cache%EFTOmegaP + 0.5_dl*a*eft_cache%EFTOmegaPP)
    S3 = 3*OmegaP + 4*a*OmegaPP + a2*OmegaPPP;  T3 = one_plus_omega + 3.5*a*OmegaP + 1.5*a2*OmegaPP
    N1 = -0.5*a*adotoa2*(adotoa2 + 3*Hdot)*S3 - 0.5*a2*adotoa2**2*(7*OmegaPP + 6*a*OmegaPPP + a2*OmegaPPPP) &
       - (Hdot**2 + adotoa*Hdotdot)*T3 - 0.5*a*adotoa2*Hdot*(9*OmegaP + 13*a*OmegaPP + 3*a2*OmegaPPP) &
       - 0.5*(gpresdotdotm_t + 4*adotoa*gpresdotm_t + 2*Hdot*gpresm_t + 4*adotoa2*gpresm_t) &
       - 0.5*(EFTLambdadotdot + 4*adotoa*EFTLambdadot + 2*Hdot*EFTLambda + 4*adotoa2*EFTLambda)
    eft_cache%Hdotdotdot = (N1 - denomdot*eft_cache%Hdotdot)/denom
Also compute EFTLambdadotdot and EFTOmegaPPPP before compute_H_derivs: for example in the full models' compute_background_EFT_functions, or by calling compute_additional_derivs first. Have the designer f(R) set Hdotdotdot, or document it as unsupported. The python transcription of this formula, using finite-difference EFTLambdadotdot/EFTOmegaPPPP, matches the numerical derivative to <=1.9e-3. It was not rebuilt in Fortran.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I reproduced the finding three independent ways and found nothing that explains it away. The stated root cause is exact. The defect is in EFTCAMB code, not upstream CAMB. It was not introduced by the rebase: B1 (eftcamb-pre-rebase) has the identical formula, lines 131-132.

(1) Symbolic proof (sympy). I used the code's own conventions: gpres*/EFTLambda* are a^2-weighted, and d/dtau Omega = a H Omega'. With these:
- The shipped Hdotdot is d(Hdot)/dtau exactly (control residual 0).
- The shipped Hdotdotdot is not the derivative of Hdotdot.
- The finding's proposed formula is the exact derivative.
- Each of the four formula defects (the '*'-for-'+' typo in T3, the 0.5 a Omega'' prefactor in N1, the wrong bracket with gpresdotdotm_t and -0.5(Lambdadot+...), and -a H^2 (H^2+3H) where it should be -0.5 a H^2 (H^2+3Hdot)) leaves a non-zero residual on its own.
- At Omega=0 the shipped formula is exact when given the true EFTLambdadotdot. So the quintessence/ADE error comes only from EFTLambdadotdot being 0 at evaluation time.

(2) A different numerical method: a cubic spline of the Hdotdot field against the tau field, with no a-derivative and no Richardson extrapolation. The shipped expression evaluated with EFTLambdadotdot = EFTOmegaPPPP = 0 reproduces the field to about 1e-16.

(3) A patched Fortran build (copy of A0). It has the proposed formula, plus finite-difference EFTLambdadotdot and EFTOmegaPPPP on the get_eft_functions path. The field then matches the numerical derivative to the same level as the Hdot-to-Hdotdot control.

Designer f(R) never sets Hdotdotdot (007p3:1107-1127), so the field is identically 0.

The defect also reaches the stability path that feeds the positivity bounds. In a DebugEFTCAMB dump (ADD tree, 5_Kmouflage_1, EFT_mass_stability=T) the Hdotdotdot field is wrong in the same way. It changes real decisions: in a 400-point scan of the Omega-Lambda gamma model (007p5), the EFT_minkowski_limit check (tachpos uses Hdotdotdot in Q5/Q6) gives the opposite answer in 29 of 200 points (A0 rejects, fixed build accepts). EFT_positivity_bounds gave the same answer in all 200 points. Cls are unaffected: no perturbation equation uses Hdotdotdot for full models. S2 is right: silently wrong API output, and wrong stability decisions only when optional flags are on.

Additions and caveats to the finding:
(a) The decision that actually flips comes through EFT_minkowski_limit/tachpos. The finding names only EFT_positivity_bounds.
(b) One option in the proposed fix, "calling compute_additional_derivs first", would not work. That routine's EFTLambdadotdot is itself wrong: dummy_helper (06p1:323-324) builds LambdaP from the outer routine's a and eft_cache, so dfridr returns 0 and EFTLambdadotdot = -2 H EFTLambdadot. The fix must compute EFTLambdadotdot and EFTOmegaPPPP independently. This is a separate defect.
(c) Extra locations:
- Horava (008p1) overrides compute_H_derivs, never sets Hdotdotdot, and has EFTGamma3V != 0, so its positivity/Minkowski checks use 0.
- Beyond Horndeski (008p5:1173/1218) builds EFTGamma2PPP from the base (wrong) Hdotdotdot, because its own override is commented out.
- Extended Galileon also never sets it, but its Gamma3 = 0.
(d) In the stability routine the positivity bounds are computed (09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:570) before compute_additional_derivs (573), so EFTLambdadotdot and EFTGamma*PP are 0 there for full models. This is a separate, related issue.
(e) Separate issue seen while checking: the ADD run of 5_Kmouflage_1 with EFT_mass_stability=T aborts at 02_equispaced_interpolation_linear_1D.f90:237 (index 1001 above bound 1000).

One limit on the evidence: the A0 control Hdot only agrees with its numerical derivative to about 2% near a=0.9 for K-mouflage. This does not affect the conclusion, which rests on factors of 45-800.

### F-024 [S2, confirmed] EFTCAMB.get_eft_functions kills Python (SIGSEGV) when the results carry no initialised EFTCAMB cache (GR, or get_results with nothing to compute)

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:382` (also: fortran/eftcamb/11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:294 (EFTCAMB_GetEvolution, returns zeros on uninitialised results), fortran/camb.f90:145 (no SetParams when neither Cls nor transfer))
- **Track:** t1-crash; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/get-eft-functions-gr-segfault.py`
- **Observed:** repro on A0: case 1 (EFTflag=0 after camb.get_background): 'killed by signal 11'; case 2 (2_PEFT_Omega_const_1.ini, EFTflag=1, WantCls=WantTransfer=False, camb.get_results): 'killed by signal 11'. lldb: EXC_BAD_ACCESS address=0x8 at __eftcamb_python_MOD_eftcamb_geteftfunctions+828. In case 2 get_scale_evolution silently returns all-zero arrays (adotoa = 0, a = 0, ...).
- **Expected:** A Python exception (CAMBError) or a valid/empty result; never a process kill.
- **Root cause:** EFTCAMB_GetEFTFunctions never checks EFTflag, allocated(State%CP%EFTCAMB%model) or allocated(par_cache%grhormass); the massive-neutrino loop and the model calls dereference unallocated data. For an EFT model with no Cls and no transfer requested, CAMB_GetResults (camb.f90:83-158) never calls CAMBdata_SetParams, so the EFT model/parameter cache (results.f90:557-583) is never initialised for that results object.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** At the top of EFTCAMB_GetEFTFunctions and EFTCAMB_GetEvolution (nested ifs, no reliance on short-circuit):
  if (.not. allocated(State%CP%EFTCAMB)) then; call GlobalError('get_eft_functions: EFTCAMB not initialised', error_unsupported_params); return; end if
  if (State%CP%EFTCAMB%EFTflag == 0) then; call GlobalError(...'EFTflag = 0'...); return; end if
  if (.not. allocated(State%CP%EFTCAMB%model)) then; call GlobalError(...); return; end if
  if (.not. allocated(State%CP%eft_par_cache%grhormass)) then; call GlobalError(...'nothing computed for these results; call get_background/get_results with outputs'...); return; end if
and in camb/eftcamb.py get_eft_functions / get_scale_evolution call config.check_global_error('get_eft_functions') after the Fortran call.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I ran the repro on A0. Case 1 (GR after get_background) was killed by signal 11, as the finding says. Case 2 of the given repro does not reach the crash: it exits with code 1 because its INI path uses the old /Users/marco/Desktop/IW/... location, which no longer exists. The script has a path bug, but the claim itself holds. My own check, fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/checks/verify/verify_geteft_nocache.py, uses the correct ini path and adds controls plus faulthandler. It reproduces SIGSEGV in all three no-cache cases, and faulthandler places the crash in the ctypes call EFTCAMB_eftfunctions (camb/eftcamb.py:351). The crash still happens for GR with mnu=0, where the massive-neutrino loop is skipped. So the fault is the missing guard on an uninitialised model/par_cache in general, with the call into the unallocated EFTCAMB%model the likely point (address 0x8 fits a null class descriptor). The grhormass loop alone does not explain it. The proposed EFTflag/allocated(model) checks cover this. The defect is in EFTCAMB code (11_EFTCAMB_python.f90 EFTCAMB_GetEFTFunctions / EFTCAMB_GetEvolution have no guards). It is not an upstream defect, and it is not caused by the test. In camb.f90 CAMB_GetResults calls SetParams only inside the WantCls/WantTransfer branches, so an EFT model with neither requested never fills eft_par_cache (results.f90:557-583). In that state get_scale_evolution returns zeros silently. Controls on the same model (get_background, full get_results) return valid values. S2 is kept because both triggers are edge-case or misuse paths, and normal use works.

### F-025 [S2, confirmed] Equispaced linear interpolation reads x(N+1)/y(N+1) when x is within rounding of x_final (reached by a shipped ini via the mass-stability check)

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/02_equispaced_interpolation_linear_1D.f90:234` (also: fortran/eftcamb/02_equispaced_interpolation_linear_1D.f90:281,344,407,470,533,596)
- **Track:** t1-crash; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/interp-index-overflow-xfinal.py`
- **Observed:** repro: 'x_initial=-23.025851: x = x_final - 8.674e-19 gives ind = 6000 (N = 6000) -> reads x(N+1), y(N+1)'; tree AD with shipped 3_RPH_KT_power_1.ini: '02_equispaced_interpolation_linear_1D.f90:237: Fortran runtime error: Index 6001 of dimension 1 of array self%x above upper bound of 6000' (EFTCAMB_Stability_Check -> compute_additional_derivs -> dfridr -> RPH compute_H_derivs -> adotoa%precompute). Same abort in the DebugEFTCAMB (ADD) run. Release: silent read past the heap array; clamp fix changes no output beyond the noise floor (worst Cl <= 1.9e-14 on 8 models).
- **Expected:** Index clamped to N-1 (or x >= x_final branch taken); no out-of-bounds access.
- **Root cause:** ind = int((x - x_initial)/grid_width) + 1 has no upper clamp; the guard x >= x_final does not cover x = x_final - few ulp, for which the quotient rounds to N-1 and ind = N, then x(ind+1), y(ind+1) are read.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** In all 7 places (lines 234, 281, 344, 407, 470, 533, 596):
-        ind = int((x - self%x_initial)/self%grid_width) + 1
+        ind = min(int((x - self%x_initial)/self%grid_width) + 1, self%num_points - 1)
(tested in tree relI and in relS: no measurable output change).
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; The defect is real and sits in the code under audit. EFTCAMB's own module 02_equispaced_interpolation_linear_1D.f90 computes ind = int((x - x_initial)/grid_width) + 1 with no upper bound. The guard `x >= x_final` does not cover points just below x_final. There, (x - x_initial)/grid_width rounds to exactly N-1 (the ulp near 5999 is about 9e-13, so any x within about 1e-15 of x_final is affected). That gives ind = N, and line 237 then reads x(N+1). Same for y(N+1) in value/derivative routines.

The shipped ini 3_RPH_KT_power_1 reaches this path in a bounds-checked build during the mass-stability check. The abort comes after "EFTCAMB: checking stability of the theory" is printed. This matches the stated chain: dfridr around a=1, then RPH compute_H_derivs, then adotoa%precompute.

It is not a test artefact and not upstream CAMB. F-002 (thermo init) is not involved, because get_background aborts before that point.

In release builds this is an out-of-bounds heap read, not a write. The reporter measured no output change from the clamp fix (worst Cl 1.9e-14), because mu is about 1e-16/3.8e-3 and the garbage value gets almost no weight. So S2 fits: crash under -fcheck with a shipped ini, and undefined behaviour in release with no measurable effect. S1 does not fit.

One problem with the repro: it hardcodes the stale repo path /Users/marco/Desktop/IW/... As given, it fails with CAMBValueError "File not found" for the ini. Once that path is corrected it reproduces exactly. The proposed min(..., num_points-1) clamp is correct and safe-local.

### F-026 [S2, confirmed] Command-line camb segfaults in CAMBdata_SetParams for every model in -O0/debug builds: the EFT MOD test dereferences the unallocated old this%CP%EFTCAMB and tests the wrong parameter set

- **Location:** `fortran/results.f90:365`
- **Track:** t1-crash; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/cli-setparams-unallocated-eftcamb.py`
- **Observed:** repro (1_EFT_GR.ini): (a) -O0 checked exe of the code under audit: 'killed by SIGSEGV after 0.0s' (lldb: EXC_BAD_ACCESS address=0x0 at results.f90:365); (b) same flags + fix: exit code 0 after 58.5 s; (c) release exe: exit code 0 after 8.3 s (survives only because the optimiser skips the second operand).
- **Expected:** SetParams works in any build; the 'DarkEnergy not set' check looks at the new parameters P.
- **Root cause:** if (.not. allocated(P%DarkEnergy) .and. this%CP%EFTCAMB%EFTflag == 0): Fortran .and. does not short-circuit, and this%CP is the previous parameter set (assigned from P ~20 lines later), so on the first CLI call this%CP%EFTCAMB is unallocated.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** -    if (.not. allocated(P%DarkEnergy) .and. this%CP%EFTCAMB%EFTflag == 0) then
-        call GlobalError('DarkEnergy not set', error_darkenergy)
-    end if
+    if (.not. allocated(P%DarkEnergy)) then
+        if (.not. allocated(P%EFTCAMB)) then
+            call GlobalError('DarkEnergy not set', error_darkenergy)
+        else if (P%EFTCAMB%EFTflag == 0) then
+            call GlobalError('DarkEnergy not set', error_darkenergy)
+        end if
+    end if
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; The defect is in EFTCAMB's own change to CAMB (the EFTCAMB MOD block at fortran/results.f90:364-368 in the checkout under audit; upstream only tests allocated(P%DarkEnergy)). The file:line given in the finding is correct.

The repro as shipped does not run any more. Its INI path points to /Users/marco/Desktop/IW/..., which no longer exists because the repo moved to /Users/marco/IW/..., so all three executables exit with rc=1 after 0.0 s. That is a stale path in the repro script, not evidence against the finding. With only the INI path corrected, the repro shows the claimed behaviour.

The repro tree chk/AD differs from the repo only by the F-002 nested-if patch, so (a) tests the code under audit. The fix tree chk3x has other unrelated patches as well, so (b) is not isolated. It still shows that the run gets past SetParams.

My own check, a different method: I disassembled __results_MOD_cambdata_setparams in the chk/AD executable. The faulting instruction is ldr w0,[x0] at +432, inside the block that ends in GlobalError with a length-18 message ('DarkEnergy not set'). Before it, the code loads P (x29+0x70) and its allocation status at offset 0x958. It then loads 'this' (x29+0x78) and the pointer to this%CP%EFTCAMB at offset 0xbb8, which is null, and reads EFTflag from it without any condition. Both operands of .and. are evaluated before the ccmp, so there is no short-circuit.

This also confirms that the test reads the old parameter set (this%CP) and not P: this%CP = P only happens about 20 lines later.

Scope: only the command-line executable is affected. The Python path (harness.run_model on the same chk/AD tree with 1_EFT_GR.ini) returns status ok, so Python users are not hit.

Severity: S2 is right. It is a crash with valid input (every ini) in the build the shipped Makefile makes with 'make Debug' (DEBUGFLAGS = -g -O0, Makefile:182). The release build survives only because of how the compiler happens to arrange the code. The wrong-parameter-set logic matters only when a CAMBdata state is reused.

### F-027 [S2, confirmed] Full-mapping third Hubble derivative (eft_cache%Hdotdotdot) is wrong whenever Omega(a) varies

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/06p1_abstract_EFTCAMB_full_map.f90:143` (also: fortran/eftcamb/06p1_abstract_EFTCAMB_full_map.f90:149, fortran/eftcamb/06p1_abstract_EFTCAMB_full_map.f90:150, fortran/eftcamb/06p1_abstract_EFTCAMB_full_map.f90:152, fortran/eftcamb/06p1_abstract_EFTCAMB_full_map.f90:157, fortran/eftcamb/06_abstract_EFTCAMB_model.f90:1148)
- **Track:** t10a-regression; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/t10a-fullmap-hdotdotdot-formula.py`
- **Observed:** Repro on 5_Kmouflage_1 (A0, Omega from -0.093 to 0): max|Hdotdotdot - d(Hdotdot)/dtau|/max = 1.40. The same expression with the corrections below gives 2.2e-4; control max|Hdotdot - d(Hdot)/dtau| = 1.9e-5. At a=0.1: Fortran -6.18e-12, numerical -2.017e-14, corrected -1.939e-14. At a=0.9: Fortran -2.68e-13, numerical +7.66e-15, corrected +7.61e-15. 5_Kmimic_1: Hdotdotdot/numerical-1 up to 2.0e4 in both A0 and B1. Designer models: 2.6e-4.
- **Expected:** Hdotdotdot = d(Hdotdot)/dtau, to the ~1e-4 accuracy of Hdotdot.
- **Root cause:** Transcription errors in the analytic derivative in EFTCAMBFullModelComputeHubbleDer. (i) N1 = -a H (3 Omega' + 0.5 a Omega''), then N1/2: the Omega'' coefficient of d(denom)/dtau becomes 0.25 instead of 0.5. (ii) '3.5_dl*a*EFTOmegaP*1.5_dl*a2*EFTOmegaPP' at lines 149 and 157 has '*' where '+' is meant. (iii) The bracket multiplying N1 uses gpresdotdotm_t + 2H gpresdotm_t - 0.5(Lambdadot + 2H Lambda) instead of the Hdotdot numerator gpresdotm_t + 2H gpresm_t + Lambdadot + 2H Lambda. (iv) Line 152: 'adotoa2*(adotoa2 + 3.0_dl*eft_cache%adotoa)' should be adotoa2*(0.5*adotoa2 + 1.5*Hdot). The code is identical in eftcamb-pre-rebase, so this is not a regression. It affects full-mapping models without their own compute_H_derivs: K-mouflage/K-mimic 008p3, ADE 008p2, Beyond Horndeski 008p5; quintessence 008p4 only if Omega varies. Hdotdotdot is not used in their perturbation equations, so spectra are unaffected. It feeds EFTCAMBModelPositivityBounds (06_abstract_EFTCAMB_model.f90:1148-1174, tachpos, active with EFT_positivity_bounds=T) and the cache/debug output, giving a wrong positivity verdict there.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** Replace the Hdotdotdot block (lines 141-168) with Hdotdotdot = (d/dtau[numerator of Hdotdot] - Hdotdot * d(denom)/dtau)/denom, i.e.
  denom = one_plus_omega + 0.5_dl*a*eft_cache%EFTOmegaP
  N1 = a*eft_cache%adotoa*(1.5_dl*eft_cache%EFTOmegaP + 0.5_dl*a*eft_cache%EFTOmegaPP)   ! d(denom)/dtau
  eft_cache%Hdotdotdot = 1._dl/denom*( -N1*eft_cache%Hdotdot &
    - a*adotoa2*(0.5_dl*adotoa2 + 1.5_dl*eft_cache%Hdot)*(3._dl*eft_cache%EFTOmegaP + 4._dl*a*eft_cache%EFTOmegaPP + a2*eft_cache%EFTOmegaPPP) &
    - 0.5_dl*a2*eft_cache%adotoa**4*(7._dl*eft_cache%EFTOmegaPP + 6._dl*a*eft_cache%EFTOmegaPPP + a2*eft_cache%EFTOmegaPPPP) &
    - (eft_cache%Hdot**2 + eft_cache%adotoa*eft_cache%Hdotdot)*(one_plus_omega + 3.5_dl*a*eft_cache%EFTOmegaP + 1.5_dl*a2*eft_cache%EFTOmegaPP) &
    - 0.5_dl*a*adotoa2*eft_cache%Hdot*(9._dl*eft_cache%EFTOmegaP + 13._dl*a*eft_cache%EFTOmegaPP + 3._dl*a2*eft_cache%EFTOmegaPPP) &
    - 0.5_dl*(eft_cache%gpresdotdotm_t + 4._dl*eft_cache%adotoa*eft_cache%gpresdotm_t + 2._dl*eft_cache%Hdot*eft_cache%gpresm_t + 4._dl*adotoa2*eft_cache%gpresm_t) &
    - 0.5_dl*(eft_cache%EFTLambdadotdot + 4._dl*eft_cache%adotoa*eft_cache%EFTLambdadot + 2._dl*eft_cache%Hdot*eft_cache%EFTLambda + 4._dl*adotoa2*eft_cache%EFTLambda))
The repro evaluates this expression in Python on the A0 cache and it matches the numerical derivative to 2.2e-4.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; The finding is confirmed. I ran the repro exactly as given, holding one harness CPU slot, and got the same numbers it reports. I then checked the formula symbolically. The check script is fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/checks/verify/hdddot_sympy.py. It uses sympy to take d/dtau of the Fortran Hdotdot expression, with da/dtau = a*H and the a^2-weighted conventions d(gpresm_t)/dtau = gpresdotm_t + 2H gpresm_t (the same for EFTLambda). I compared that against a literal Python copy of lines 141-168 and against the proposed fix, at random points.
- The literal Fortran expression is wrong by up to about 2.5x whenever any Omega derivative is non-zero.
- It is exact (to about 1e-16) when Omega is constant.
- The proposed fix matches sympy to about 1e-16.

The analytic result agrees with the four transcription errors listed (wrong N1 coefficient and sign, '*' where '+' is meant, the wrong bracket multiplying N1, and the adotoa2 + 3*adotoa term).

To pin the output to these lines, I used the harness reference npz files (A0 and B1). The cached Hdotdotdot equals the literal Python copy of 06p1:141-168 to 1e-16 for K-mouflage, K-mimic, ADE and quintessence. So the bug is in the code under audit, and it is identical in pre-rebase B1, so it is not a regression.

The scope needs correcting:
- **ADE (008p2) and FM quintessence (008p4) are not affected.** Both set EFTOmegaV = EFTOmegaP = 0, where the formula is exact.
- **Beyond Horndeski (008p5):** its own override is commented out (line 113), so it uses the generic routine. But EFTGamma2PPP, which uses Hdotdotdot (line 1218), is built only when x4 == 0, which means Omega = -x4/5 = 0. So it is wrong only in the positivity check when x4 is not 0.
- **Truly affected:** K-mouflage and K-mimic (008p3).

Spectra are unaffected. Hdotdotdot and EFTGamma2PPP are read only by EFTCAMBModelPositivityBounds (06_abstract_EFTCAMB_model.f90:1128-1150), the RGR printout and the cache output. EFT_positivity_bounds defaults to false.

I did not show that the positivity verdict actually flips. 5_Kmouflage_1 with EFT_positivity_bounds=T still reports 'theory stable' on A0, and I did not build a fixed tree to compare. S2 is right: a wrong derived quantity in a non-default diagnostic, for one model family. The fix is physics-changing only for the positivity verdict.

### F-028 [S2, confirmed] pi oscillations under-resolved: RK45 step beyond its stability limit, and late-time CMB source sampling, make high-ell TT of Horava/RPH models depend on accuracy and on unrelated redshift windows

- **Location:** `fortran/equations.f90:284` (also: fortran/results.f90:2864 (dtau0 late-time CMB source step, SetTimeSteps), fortran/RungeKuttaDP45.f90 (mixed error norm 1/max(1,|y|) treats the tiny pi variables absolutely))
- **Track:** t10b-convergence; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/pi-rk45-step-instability.py`
- **Observed:** Repro (A0, windows off): for 5_Horava_SolSyst_2 the default/boost2 unlensed TT differs by +1.215e-01 at ell 2454 (+1.06e-02 at ell 2000). Turning on the base_params redshift windows, which only add source time steps, changes the default TT by -7.39e-02 at ell 2452. In GR the same comparisons give -7.2e-4 and -3e-6. Default vs boost3 over the sweep: SolSyst_2 1.2e-1, 3_RPH_MB_OmegaDE_1 7.3e-3 at ell 2500, 5_Horava_eta_4 3.1e-2 at ell 2500, 5_Horava_SolSyst_3 9.1e-1 at ell 2449 (boost2 vs boost3 still 1.1e-1). A run-time cost of 90-145x for boost3 vs default (GR: 20x) shows that the default runs do not resolve the pi dynamics.
- **Expected:** Default accuracy agrees with boost2/boost3 at the GR level (~1e-3), and does not depend on whether redshift windows are requested.
- **Root cause:** GaugeInterface_ScalEv lets the scalar RK45 take steps up to 20/IntTolBoost Mpc (CAMB v2). The pi field oscillates with omega_pi^2 = (C + k^2 D1 + k^4 D2)/(A1 + k^2 A2), and steps beyond ~3.3/omega_pi are outside the DP45 stability region. pi and pi' are small in CAMB units, and the mixed error norm controls them only absolutely, so the error estimate does not catch the numerical growth. The solution then depends on where the integrator is forced to stop (source time steps, windows). Separately, for Horava_eta_4/SolSyst_3 the pi-driven oscillations of the potentials at high k are also under-sampled by the late-time CMB source grid dtau0 (~7 Mpc with tensors).
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** In GaugeInterface_ScalEv, after setting max_step_size, add a step limit while EFT is active:
  if (CP%EFTCAMB%EFTflag /= 0 .and. EV%EFTCAMBactive) then
    pi_A = EV%eft_cache%EFTpiA1 + EV%k2_buf*EV%eft_cache%EFTpiA2
    if (pi_A /= 0) then
      pi_omega = sqrt(abs((EV%eft_cache%EFTpiC + EV%k2_buf*EV%eft_cache%EFTpiD1 + EV%k2_buf**2*EV%eft_cache%EFTpiD2)/pi_A)) + abs((EV%eft_cache%EFTpiB1 + EV%k2_buf*EV%eft_cache%EFTpiB2)/pi_A)
      if (pi_omega > 0) rk_settings%max_step_size = min(rk_settings%max_step_size, 1.5_dl/pi_omega/CP%Accuracy%IntTolBoost)
    end if
  end if
This uses the coefficients cached by the last derivs call. Also cap dtau0 in SetTimeSteps by a fraction (~1/8) of the pi oscillation period at the largest source k when EFT is active.
Tested in the env-gated tree work/t10b-convergence/knob:
- SolSyst_2 default vs boost2: 1.2e-1 -> 8.3e-4; vs boost3: 9.2e-4; windows dependence 7.4e-2 -> 5.9e-4.
- RPH_MB_OmegaDE_1: 7.3e-3 -> 8.9e-4.
