- eta_4: 3.1e-2 -> 1.9e-2, and 2.0e-3 with dtau0/3.
- SolSyst_3: 9.1e-1 -> 3.7e-1 -> 1.1e-1 (also needs finer source k sampling).
- Converged models change by <=3e-5 (max 1.4e-3); no measurable slowdown.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I ran the repro as given and got the stated numbers exactly. I then checked the claim a second way: an A0 copy that caps the scalar RK45 max step at a constant AUDIT_VFY_MAXSTEP Mpc, read from an env var and applied only while EFT is active. This is independent of the knob's omega_pi formula, and with the env var unset the copy is bit-identical to A0.

For SolSyst_2 and RPH_MB_OmegaDE_1, a small enough constant step cap at default accuracy alone brings TT within ~8e-4 of boost2. The windows dependence also drops to ~1e-6, and a finer cap does not change the result further. The cap that works depends on the model (2 Mpc works for SolSyst_2, 5 Mpc does not; RPH needs 0.3 Mpc, 2 Mpc has no effect). That matches a stability limit tied to each model's oscillation frequency, not a generic tolerance problem.

B1 (pre-rebase EFTCAMB, dverk integrator) is converged at default accuracy for both models, and its windows dependence is ~3e-6. So this is a regression in the code under audit: EFTCAMB moved onto CAMB v2's DP45 integrator with max_step_size = 20/IntTolBoost (equations.f90:284) and added no step limit for the pi field. It is not an upstream-only defect, since GR is fine.

The finding overreaches in one place: 5_Horava_eta_4 does not respond to any step cap (2.67e-2 at 20 Mpc, 2.69e-2 at 2 and at 0.3 Mpc), and B1 shows the same 2.6e-2 there. That part is a separate, pre-existing issue, possibly the late-time source sampling (dtau0) the finding also names. I did not verify the dtau0 sub-claim, the SolSyst_3 numbers or the boost3 sweep. I also did not directly confirm the explanation that the error norm misses the growth.

I keep S2: the main effect is silently poor high-ell accuracy in some Horava/RPH models. It is a regression against B1, which may justify raising the priority.

### F-029 [S2, confirmed] Discontinuous EFT turn-on (potentials jump 25-33%) is integrated to first order by the CMB time quadrature: low-ell TT off by 1-7% and dependent on TimeStepBoost and unrelated redshift windows

- **Location:** `fortran/results.f90:2884` (also: fortran/equations.f90:521 (EFTturnOnTime switch in GaugeInterface_EvolveScal), fortran/equations.f90:338 (smallTime: for low k the switch is applied up to ~10 Mpc early, at the previous output time), fortran/cmbmain.f90 DoFlatIntegration (trapezoid weights TimeSteps%dpoints))
- **Track:** t10b-convergence; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/eft-turnon-jump-cmb-time-sampling.py`
- **Observed:** Repro, 2_PEFT_Omega_const_1 at k=1e-3: across a=0.01, Psi jumps from -0.59327 to -0.45231 and EFTISW/k from +1.35e-5 to -8.08e-4. Low-ell unlensed TT changes when windows are turned on: +4.64e-2 at ell 2, +2.56e-2 at ell 10, +1.10e-2 at ell 30 (GR: +5.4e-4). With TimeStepBoost=3: +1.90e-2 at ell 2 (GR +6.0e-5). Sweep, default vs boost3: Omega_const_1 1.8e-2, gamma5_const_1 8.9e-3, gamma5_exp_1 7.2e-3, Omega_const_2 6.3e-3, gamma3_const_2 3.0e-3, RPH_MK_const_1 1.1e-2, Kmouflage_3 4.8e-3; default vs boost2: Kmouflage_1 1.8e-2, Kmouflage_2 7.3e-2. Boost3 itself is off by 0.3-0.9% compared with boost3 plus the fix. Only the late-time source step matters: dtau0/3 alone reproduces boost3, while IntTolBoost=4 changes <1.1e-4 and qmin/3 <1e-3. With turn-on at 1e-3 or 1e-4 the dtau0 sensitivity drops to the GR level (<7e-5).
- **Expected:** Low-ell TT independent of source sampling and of unrelated redshift windows at the GR level (<1e-3).
- **Root cause:** At EFTCAMB_turn_on_time the evolution switches from GR to the EFT equations, with type-1 ICs for pi. For models that are not GR at turn-on (the test inis use a=0.01, z=99), Psi/Phi and the ISW source jump. The CMB sources are sampled on State%TimeSteps (coarse late-time step dtau0 ~7 Mpc) and integrated with trapezoid weights. The interval containing tau_on therefore carries an O(dtau0 x jump) error, and for low k the smallTime shortcut applies the switch at the previous output time (up to 10 Mpc early). Any change of the time grid (TimeStepBoost, accuracy_boost, windows) moves the result. Context: the jump itself (whose delta-function ISW contribution is dropped) makes these toy models strongly dependent on the turn-on time: TT(ell=2)/GR = 3.35, 1.60, 1.97 for a_on = 1e-2, 1e-3, 1e-4.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** In SetTimeSteps, when EFTflag /= 0 and taurst < tau_on < tau0 (tau_on = State%DeltaTime(0, CP%EFTCAMB%EFTCAMB_pert_turn_on), the same expression as EV%EFTturnOnTime), add points that straddle the turn-on tightly, plus a fine grid on both sides:
  call TimeSteps%Add_delta(max(State%taurend, tau_on - 100), tau_on*(1 - 1d-7), dtau0/10)
  call TimeSteps%Add(tau_on*(1 - 1d-7), tau_on*(1 + 1d-7), 1)
  call TimeSteps%Add_delta(tau_on*(1 + 1d-7), min(State%tau0, tau_on + 300), dtau0/10)
The bracket alone is not enough; the dense range before tau_on is needed. Also integrate exactly to EFTturnOnTime instead of using the smallTime shortcut, and document or warn that non-GR-at-turn-on models are turn-on dependent.
Tested (knob tree, AUDIT_T10B_TON=1, AUDIT_T10B_PRE=100):
- windows dependence 4.6e-2 -> 1.5e-4; TimeStepBoost=3 1.9e-2 -> 1.9e-3;
- default vs fixed boost3: Omega_const_1 3.0e-3, gamma5_const_1 2.9e-3, RPH_MK_const_1 1.6e-3, Omega_const_2 1.2e-3;
- Kmouflage_2 default vs A0 boost2 7.3e-2 -> 1.9e-2;
- GR and models with early turn-on unchanged (<=3e-5).
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I ran the repro as given and every quoted number came back exactly. Psi jumps from -0.59327 to -0.45231 across a=0.01 and EFTISW/k goes from +1.35e-5 to -8.08e-4. Turning redshift windows on changes low-ell TT by 4.64e-2 at ell 2 (GR: 5.4e-4), and TimeStepBoost=3 changes it by 1.90e-2 (GR: 6e-5). The knob-tree fix cuts the windows dependence to 1.5e-4 and the boost3 dependence to 1.9e-3.

For the independent check I swept EFTCAMB_turn_on_time from 0.0096 to 0.0104 in 9 steps on A0, with tensors, lensing and windows off. If the turn-on dependence were physical, TT would change smoothly with a_on. Instead, at default sampling TT(ell=2) jumps around in a sawtooth of up to +5.9% between neighbouring a_on values. That repeats every ~4e-4 in a, which is about one late-time source step dtau0 (tau0/500, about 28 Mpc without tensors). At TimeStepBoost=4 the same sweep is smooth and monotonic, with quadratic-fit residuals of 2e-4. So the scatter comes from first-order integration of the jump on the coarse State%TimeSteps grid, not from physics and not from a script artefact.

Attribution: the discontinuity comes from EFTCAMB's turn-on switch (equations.f90:521; tau_on set at :2186). The quadrature (SetTimeSteps Add_delta at results.f90:2884, trapezoid weights in cmbmain) is unchanged upstream code. The defect is that EFTCAMB adds this jump without adding source time points around tau_on, so it belongs to the code under audit, and the cited file:line is right. I did not test the smallTime early-switch claim numerically. smallTime = min(tau, 1/k)/100 gives about 8 Mpc for k=1e-3 at this tau, which fits "up to ~10 Mpc". Its effect is equivalent to moving a_on by about 1e-4, about 1e-3 in TT from the smooth slope, so it is a secondary cause.

S2 is the right severity: silently poor accuracy at the percent level in models that are not GR at turn-on, with late turn-on at a=0.01 as in the test inis. Early turn-on reduces it to the GR level, as the finding says. One small correction: "dtau0 ~7 Mpc" holds only when tensors are on, max(taurst/40, tau0/2000). Without tensors dtau0 is about 28 Mpc and the error is larger.

### F-030 [S2, confirmed] EFT_IC_type=1 initial pi = -h0 E/(C + k^2 D1 + k^4 D2) diverges where the pi mass+gradient coefficient crosses zero, producing a pole in P(k) and non-convergent low-ell Cls (3_RPH_MB_const_1)

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:295`
- **Track:** t10b-convergence; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/ic1-pi-attractor-pole.py`
- **Observed:** Repro, 3_RPH_MB_const_1 at turn-on a=0.01: C = -3.64e-13 and D1 = 1.38e-7, so k_c = 1.623e-3/Mpc. pi_ini = -0.59, -1.18, -6.86, -46.2 at k = 1.4, 1.5, 1.6, 1.62e-3, then +9.97, +2.29 at k = 1.64, 1.7e-3. delta_c(a=1) = +52, +67, -43, -7982, -948, -42, +25. P(k)/P_GR at default: 2.58, 1.97, 15.3, 0.00409, 0.294 at k = 1.50, 1.58, 1.66, 1.75, 1.84e-3. At boost2: 2.7, 6.4e-5, 3.51, 0.045. Default vs boost3: P(k) x3.3 at k=1.6e-4 and TT 97% at ell 13. The glitch is also present in B1.
- **Expected:** A finite, smooth initial condition and a P(k) that is smooth in k.
- **Root cause:** The quasi-static attractor IC divides by C + k^2 D1 + k^4 D2. Only an exact zero is guarded (`/= 0._dl`). When C < 0 at turn-on (tachyonic band k < k_c) the denominator crosses zero at k_c, there is no attractor, and pi_ini ~ 1/(k - k_c). The transfer function inherits a pole, whose sampling depends on the k grid (accuracy_boost).
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** Regularise the type-1 IC. Set pi = pi' = 0 (GR IC) when |C + k^2 D1 + k^4 D2| < eps (|C| + k^2|D1| + k^4|D2|), or whenever the denominator is <= 0 (no stable attractor), and print a warning. Tested with eps = 0.5 (knob tree, AUDIT_T10B_ICREG): P(k)/P_GR across k_c becomes a smooth 1.35-1.38, the glitch metric goes from 6.3 to 0.18, and default vs boost3 TT from 97% to 7.2%. The remainder is the model's tachyonic growth for k < k_c; t6 found the mass-stability verdict that accepts this model unreliable. A smaller eps, or a non-attractor IC for C < 0, should be chosen by the authors.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I ran the repro script unchanged and got every number it quotes. The pole is in the EFTCAMB code under audit, not in the test or upstream CAMB. The IC_type=1 attractor IC at 09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:295-302 divides by C + k^2 D1 + k^4 D2 (default EFT_IC_type=1, 09_EFTCAMB_main.f90:235), and the only guard is `/= 0._dl`. In 3_RPH_MB_const_1, C < 0 at turn-on, so the denominator changes sign at k_c = sqrt(-C/D1) = 1.624e-3/Mpc.

For an independent check I ran my own dense k scan (31 points, 1.55e-3 to 1.70e-3). The product pi_ini*(k - k_c) stays almost constant, from +1.53e-4 to +1.75e-4, and pi_ini changes sign exactly between k = 1.620e-3 and 1.625e-3. That is a clean simple pole, 1/(k - k_c). delta_c(a=1) reaches -3.6e5 at k = 1.625e-3, against about +23 at k = 1e-3 and +158 at 3e-3.

The pole does not depend on accuracy settings, and the transfer k grid then samples it, which explains why P(k) and low-ell Cls do not converge. The B1 reference run shows the same dip (P/P_GR = 0.0188 at k = 1.75e-3), so the defect was already there before the rebase.

With the regularised IC from the knob tree, P/P_GR becomes a smooth 1.35-1.38, which ties the spike to the IC. Away from k_c that ratio also differs from the unregularised one, so the IC leaves a lasting effect: the regularisation is a physics-changing fix, as the finding says.

S2 stands. The trigger is a specific case: a model where C < 0 at turn-on, which is also a mass-stability problem. In that case the results are silently wrong and do not converge.

### F-031 [S2, confirmed] Never-written timestep-cache fields (EFTcdotdot, EFTGamma1PP, Horndeski V_scf) are returned to Python and NaN-checked by the stability test

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/03_abstract_EFTCAMB_cache.f90:443` (also: fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:207, fortran/eftcamb/08f_full_models/008p0_Horndeski.f90:1778, fortran/eftcamb/08f_full_models/008p0_Horndeski.f90:2014-2016, related: repro/stability-cache-cdotdot-uninitialised.py (t6-stability))
- **Track:** t2-state; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/uninit-cache-fields-garbage.py A0`
- **Observed:** Repro on A0:
- 5_quint_exponential get_scale_evolution, two identical processes: EFTcdotdot = 2.169000588e-314 vs 2.151742535e-314, EFTGamma1PP = 3.0082e-314 vs 3.0254e-314. Same for 4_Des_F_R_LCDM_1.
- 5_hdsk_TG_3 get_eft_functions V_scf over 4 runs in one process: 0,0,0, then [-2.83e-06, 4.72e-08, ...]. In attempt 3, 5_hdsk_TG_1 gave [1618, -118.05, 2.14, ...] in one run.
- OMP 1 vs 8: eft_V_scf rel diff 0.94 (TG_1) and 1.0 (TG_3).
- Stability verdict: tree nanit (A0 + -finit-real=snan -finit-derived + NaN-filled tables) rejects 7/10 models (4_Des_F_R_LCDM_1, 5_quint_exponential, 5_Kmouflage_3, 5_ADE_1, 5_Horava_eta_1, 5_Kmimic_1, 5_scg) with 'EFTCAMB: theory unstable'. nanit_fix (the same plus the 3-line init below) accepts all 10 with A0's H(z=1).
- **Expected:** Deterministic values (0 or the physical value), and a stability verdict that does not depend on memory contents.
- **Root cause:** 1) EFTCAMBTimestepCacheInit (03_abstract_EFTCAMB_cache.f90:402-586) resets every field except EFTcdotdot and EFTGamma1PP. These two are written only by compute_additional_derivs (mass-stability path). EFTCAMBTimestepCacheIsNan checks them (lines 631/637) in EFTTestStability (09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:207) before anything writes them, and get_scale_evolution copies them out. In release builds the garbage is usually a denormal, so the model passes; if the stack happens to hold a NaN pattern, a stable model is rejected. 2) 008p0_Horndeski.f90:2014 allocates the Vscf/dVscf/ddVscf tables, but they are filled only by the designer-V solver (line 2997). With Horndeski_designer_V=F, eft_cache%V_scf (line 1778) interpolates uninitialised heap memory; it is used only for output and the NaN check (the physics read at line 2270 is inside if (designerV)).
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** 03_abstract_EFTCAMB_cache.f90 EFTCAMBTimestepCacheInit:
     self%EFTcdot = 0._dl
+    self%EFTcdotdot = 0._dl
 ...
     self%EFTGamma1P = 0._dl
+    self%EFTGamma1PP = 0._dl
008p0_Horndeski.f90 after line 2016:
+    if (.not. self%designerV) then
+        self%Vscf%y = 0._dl; self%dVscf%y = 0._dl; self%ddVscf%y = 0._dl
+    end if
(Alternatively, zero y/yp/ypp/... in the interpolation Init routines.) Tested in work/t2-state/fix against runs/A0 for 5_quint_exponential, 4_Des_F_R_LCDM_1 and 5_hdsk_TG_1: Cl <=5.6e-15, Pk/sigma8/H 0, eft fields identical. The repro then reports all identical, and nanit_fix passes 10/10.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; Both parts are real defects in EFTCAMB code. (1) EFTCAMBTimestepCacheInit (03_abstract_EFTCAMB_cache.f90:443/448) resets EFTcdot and EFTGamma1P but not EFTcdotdot or EFTGamma1PP. For full-mapping models and designer f(R), those two fields are written only in compute_additional_derivs (06p1:183/192, 06p2:74/80). EFTCAMBTimestepCacheIsNan (lines 631/637) checks them inside EFTTestStability (09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:207). The stack-local eft_cache in the stability driver (line 71) is therefore NaN-checked while it still holds whatever was in memory. (2) Horndeski allocates the Vscf/dVscf/ddVscf tables (008p0:2014-2016) but fills them only under designerV (line 2996). Line 1778 reads them anyway. The value only reaches output: Cls do not change when the memory contents change. In release builds the garbage is usually a denormal, so the wrong stability verdict has not been seen in a production build. It is still a real nondeterministic path, and Python users get garbage values back, so S2 stands. One limit of the fix: zeroing gives deterministic values, but those values are still not the physical EFTcdotdot for models that never compute it.

### F-032 [S2, confirmed] compute_additional_derivs differentiates helpers evaluated at the host a/cache: mass-stability criterion uses EFTGamma1PP=EFTGamma2PP=0, EFTcdotdot=-2H*cdot and (full mapping) uninitialised Gamma2PP/Gamma3PP/Gamma3PPP

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/06p2_abstract_EFTCAMB_designer.f90:141` (also: fortran/eftcamb/06p2_abstract_EFTCAMB_designer.f90:217, fortran/eftcamb/06p1_abstract_EFTCAMB_full_map.f90:255-256,332-333,361,379,388, fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:573-574, fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/mass-stability-wrong-derivatives.py (verdict flip A0 vs FIX1))
- **Track:** t2d-static; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/additional-derivs-wrong-cache.py ; /Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/mass-stability-wrong-derivatives.py /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-marco-IW-eftcamb-update-EFTCAMB/2b9dddd0-ab26-46fb-8810-571fb7648a45/scratchpad/audit/trees/A0 /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-marco-IW-eftcamb-update-EFTCAMB/2b9dddd0-ab26-46fb-8810-571fb7648a45/scratchpad/audit/work/t2d-static/FIX1`
- **Observed:** A0, public EFTTestStability with EFT_mass_stability=T, compared with a centred finite difference of the returned first derivatives (re-run in attempt 4, identical). 3_RPH_KT_power_1 at a=0.1: EFTGamma1PP code 0 vs FD +4.467e3, EFTGamma2PP 0 vs +0.1303, EFTcdotdot -2.353e-17 (exactly -2*H*cdot) vs FD -2.942e-17. 5_quint_cubic (full mapping) at a=0.1: EFTcdotdot -2.68e-17 vs +3.17e-17, EFTLambdadotdot -1.08e-16 vs +1.29e-16. The cache seen by compute_mass_factors differs from the model's own analytic values: 3_RPH_MK_power_1 a=0.0101 EFTGamma1PP model -1.5952e7, used 0. mu1/H^2: A0 +639.6 (stable) vs FIX1 -365.9 (unstable). 2_PEFT_Omega_const_1: +272.4 vs -961.2. Across the 85 shipped+audit inis with their own flags, 8 of the 14 shipped inis that set EFT_mass_stability=T flip stable->unstable with correct derivatives (2_PEFT_Omega_const_1/2, Omega_lin_1/2, 3_RPH_KT_OmegaDE_1, RPH_MB_const_1, RPH_MK_const_1, RPH_MK_power_1).
- **Expected:** Second derivatives equal d/da of the first derivatives (rel err ~1e-6), and analytic derivatives already provided by the model are not overwritten.
- **Root cause:** Inside the dfridr helper dummy_helper(temp_a, ...), compute_adotoa (designer 06p2:141) and compute_background_EFT_functions + compute_adotoa (full map 06p1:255-256) are called with the host `a` and the host `eft_cache` instead of temp_a/temp_cache. The massive-neutrino terms also use the host a. cP (06p2:217, 06p1:332) and LambdaP (06p1:333) come from the host cache, so the differentiated quantities do not depend on the probe point. In 06p1 the dummies gamma2PP, gamma3PP, gamma3PPP are never assigned (gfortran -Wuninitialized at 06p1:361/379/388). EFTStabilityComputation (09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:573-574) then overwrites the model's analytic EFTcdotdot/EFTGamma*PP before compute_mass_factors. Pre-existing in B1 (same code).
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** Tested as FIX1 (work/t2d-static/fix1_designer.diff, fix1_full.diff): in dummy_helper call compute_adotoa / compute_background_EFT_functions with (temp_a, temp_par_cache, temp_cache); use temp_a in the neutrino loops and temp_cache%Hdot; set cP = temp_cache%EFTcdot and LambdaP = temp_cache%EFTLambdadot; in 06p1 assign gamma2PP/gamma3PP/gamma3PPP from temp_cache; add EFTLambdadotdot for designers. Better still, skip compute_additional_derivs for models that already compute these derivatives analytically (Pure EFT, RPH). After the fix the mass formula itself still needs physics validation (track 6), because correct inputs make most shipped mass-stability test inis unstable.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I reproduced it on A0 using the repro script and an independent check. The defect is in EFTCAMB code, not upstream CAMB and not in the test.

1. Static reading (the stated file:line is right). In 06p2:141, `dummy_helper` calls `compute_adotoa(a, temp_par_cache, eft_cache)` with the host `a` and the host cache. The neutrino loops use the host `a` and `eft_cache%Hdot`. `cP` at 06p2:217 is built from host-cache values only. In 06p1, `compute_background_EFT_functions` and `compute_adotoa` are called with the host `a` and `eft_cache`. `cP` and `LambdaP` come from the host cache. The dummies `gamma2PP`, `gamma3PP` and `gamma3PPP` are never assigned, yet `dfridr` differentiates them to get `EFTGamma2PPP`, `EFTGamma3PPP` and `EFTGamma3PPPP`. 09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:573 calls `compute_additional_derivs` before `compute_mass_factors`, which overwrites the model's analytic values.

2. Independent check (`checks/verify/addder_analytic_vs_overwrite.py`). It compares the analytic second derivatives the model puts in the cache when `EFT_mass_stability=F` (so `compute_additional_derivs` is not called) with the cache after `EFT_mass_stability=T`, and with a finite difference (FD) of the analytic first derivatives. The analytic values match the FD to within 1e-4. After `compute_additional_derivs`:
   - `EFTGamma1PP` and `EFTGamma2PP` are always 0.
   - `EFTcdotdot` equals -2*H*cdot exactly.

3. Verdict flip (repro `mass-stability-wrong-derivatives.py`). The FIX1 tree no longer exists, so I used my own A0 copy (NOADD) that just skips the `compute_additional_derivs` call. That keeps the analytic Pure EFT / RPH derivatives. Results:
   - 3_RPH_MK_power_1 at a=0.0101: mu1/H^2 is +639.59 on A0 and -365.93 on NOADD.
   - 2_PEFT_Omega_const_1 at a=0.0101: +272.36 on A0 and -961.06 on NOADD.
   - Both shipped inis flip from stable to "EFTCAMB: theory unstable". These numbers match the finding.

Minor caveats:
- The repro's docstring says "temp_cache%adotoa stays 0". That is wrong: `temp_cache = eft_cache` copies the host H, so the effect is a stale H, not zero. The finding's root_cause field does not make this claim.
- I did not trace exactly why `dfridr` gives exactly 0 for RPH `Gamma1PP`. It could be a constant helper output or NaN probes, since `dfridr` returns 0 when every error estimate is NaN. This does not change the conclusion.
- I did not recheck the claimed 8-of-14 ini count; I checked 2 of the inis.

Severity: S2 is right. Only the optional `EFT_mass_stability=T` path is affected, and there it gives wrong stable/unstable verdicts for shipped test inis. The fix changes physics: with correct derivatives several shipped inis become unstable, so the mass formula itself still needs physics validation.

### F-033 [S2, confirmed] Full-mapping derivs evaluates the background EFT functions before adotoa/Hdot/Hdotdot are set: Beyond Horndeski and Extended Galileon use the previous derivs call's background

- **Location:** `fortran/equations.f90:2816` (also: fortran/eftcamb/08f_full_models/008p5_Beyond_Horndeski.f90:996-997,1009,1014,1051, fortran/eftcamb/08f_full_models/008p7_Extended_Galileon.f90:337-339,348,359, fortran/eftcamb/11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:421-423 (fresh python-allocated cache), fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:115-116,415-416,537-538; fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:483-484; fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_RGR.f90:228-229, instrumentation: /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-marco-IW-eftcamb-update-EFTCAMB/2b9dddd0-ab26-46fb-8810-571fb7648a45/scratchpad/audit/work/t2d-static/lag.diff (checks/t2d-static/patch_lag.py))
- **Track:** t2d-static; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/fullmap-bg-eft-stale-background.py`
- **Observed:** Instrumented tree work/t2d-static/LAG: FIX3 plus a re-evaluation of compute_background_EFT_functions after the second compute_H_derivs in derivs; the used values are restored unless EFT_LAG_FIX=1. Audit ini 008p5-beyond-horndeski.ini with EFTCAMB_skip_stability=T, 1 thread. EFT functions derivs used vs the same functions re-evaluated with the current background (a>=1e-3): EFTc max rel diff 3.79e-4 (a=0.607, k=3.7e-3/Mpc: -3.02380e-09 vs -3.02265e-09); EFTLambda 3.79e-4; EFTLambdadot 1.34e-2 (a=0.9999); EFTcdot 43.6 (a=0.332, k=7.2/Mpc: 3.17e-18 vs 7.12e-20). Candidate fix (keep the fresh values) vs code under audit: unlensed TT 2.21e-5, C_l^phiphi 2.02e-5, P(k,z=0) 2.55e-3 (k=21.8/Mpc). Control (A0 vs LAG with used values kept, only recompilation differs): TT 2.4e-13, C_l^phiphi 1.9e-7, P(k) 3.0e-5. Extended Galileon (same pattern, audit ini 008p7-ext-galileon.ini) at a=0.01: EFTc used/fresh differ by 0.126, EFTLambda by 0.063, EFTcdot and EFTLambdadot by 2.26. That run then fails (rc=1), so there are no spectra.
- **Expected:** The EFT functions in the perturbation equations at time tau are those of the background at tau (used == fresh), independent of the integrator's step history.
- **Root cause:** For full models derivs calls compute_background_EFT_functions (equations.f90:2816) before compute_adotoa (2817) and compute_H_derivs (2861, 2893). It never calls it again afterwards; the re-call at 2897 is designer-only. EV%eft_cache is not re-initialised between derivs calls in release builds (only when DebugEFTCAMB, equations.f90:1955). Background EFT functions that read eft_cache%adotoa/Hdot/Hdotdot therefore get the previous derivs call of that k mode. Beyond Horndeski (008p5_Beyond_Horndeski.f90:996-1051) calls the generic full-model compute_adotoa/compute_H_derivs, which themselves read stale EFTc/EFTLambda/EFTOmega and neutrino/pressure-derivative fields, and then builds EFTc = adotoa^2 f(x_i), EFTcdot and EFTLambdadot from them. Extended Galileon (008p7_Extended_Galileon.f90:337-366) recomputes the background only through its `adotoa == 0` guard, which fires on a fresh cache and never in release derivs. 11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:421-423, 09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:115/415/537, 09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:483 and 09_EFTCAMB_RGR.f90:228 use the same order (bg-EFT, then adotoa) on a freshly initialised cache, where these models read zeros; the resulting Beyond Horndeski missing-DE background is another track's finding (beyond-horndeski-hubble.py). The static scan of all 43 bg-EFT/adotoa/H_derivs/dtauda implementations finds no other full model reading fields that are not yet set. Horava and Horndeski extend EFTCAMB_designer_model and are not affected. B1 has the same call order (pre-rebase equations.f90:2609-2610).
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** Make full-model background EFT functions independent of cache fields their callers fill later. In Beyond Horndeski and Extended Galileon, compute adotoa, Hdot and Hdotdot from the model's own background solution inside compute_background_EFT_functions, unconditionally. Extended Galileon: drop the `adotoa == 0` condition at 008p7:337 and always call its own compute_adotoa/compute_H_derivs. Beyond Horndeski: bind its own compute_adotoa/compute_H_derivs/compute_dtauda from its x_i solution, as beyond-horndeski-hubble.py already requires. Alternatively, call compute_background_EFT_functions again for full models after the second compute_H_derivs, in derivs and in the getter/IC/stability/RGR callers. That alternative is the tested candidate (EFT_LAG_FIX=1): it changes BH's TT by 2.2e-5 and P(k) by 2.5e-3. For BH it is only partial, because BH's adotoa itself depends on the lagged EFTc/EFTLambda.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; The repro script as given fails at once with "a0: rc=1 ERROR STOP". This is only a path problem: the repo moved from /Users/marco/Desktop/IW to /Users/marco/IW, and the audit inis still have DEFAULT(/Users/marco/Desktop/IW/.../base_params.ini). I made a copy that differs only in the ini path (checks/verify/stalebg_repro_pathfix.py) and it reproduces every number in the finding.

I also checked the claim a second way. I built a new tree (work/verify/stalebg/ITER, made from the rebuilt A0 by checks/verify/stalebg_iter_patch.py). In derivs, after the second compute_H_derivs and for full models only, it repeats the step "background EFT functions, then adotoa, then H derivatives" EFT_ITER times until the values agree with each other. EFT_ITER=0 means the unmodified code. Results:
- EFT_ITER=0 vs A0 (control): 1.9e-7, so the patch itself changes nothing.
- 5 vs 20 iterations: 1.4e-5, so the iteration has converged.
- Code under audit vs the self-consistent values: they differ by more than the finding's own partial fix shows (C_l^phiphi 1e-4 vs 2e-5).

The source confirms the mechanism:
- In derivs (equations.f90:2815-2816), compute_background_EFT_functions runs before compute_adotoa. It is called again after the H derivatives only for designer models (line 2897).
- The cache is re-initialised only when DebugEFTCAMB is set (line 1955).
- Beyond Horndeski does not bind its own compute_adotoa/H_derivs (008p5:111-113 are commented out). Its background routine calls the generic full-model compute_adotoa (06p1:103-105), which reads EFTOmega/EFTc/EFTLambda from the cache, left over from the previous call. It then builds EFTc = adotoa^2 f(x). So the code does one step of a fixed-point iteration per call, and the result depends on the integrator's step history.
- Extended Galileon recomputes adotoa/Hdot only when adotoa == 0 (008p7:337), so in derivs it uses the Hdot/Hdotdot of the previous call.

The defect is in EFTCAMB code, not upstream CAMB. The models affected (Beyond Horndeski, Extended Galileon) have no test ini, and the error is of order 1e-4 on Cls and 3e-3 on P(k). That fits S2 (silently poor accuracy), not S1. Extended Galileon fails with rc=1 on A0 whether or not the iteration is applied, so that failure has a separate cause (the neutrino issue fixed in FIX3). The lag exists there, but I could not measure its effect on the spectra.

### F-034 [S2, confirmed] Quasi-static Sigma output (QS_sigma) is a verbatim copy of the QS_mu formula

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/06_abstract_EFTCAMB_model.f90:972` (also: fortran/eftcamb/06_abstract_EFTCAMB_model.f90:920 (QS_mu, identical RHS))
- **Track:** t2d-static; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/qs-sigma-copy-of-qs-mu.py`
- **Observed:** A0, get_scale_evolution for 4_Des_F_R_LCDM_1 at k=0.01,0.1,1 /Mpc and a=0.3,0.5,0.8,1 (re-run in attempt 4, identical): max|QS_sigma - QS_mu| = 0.0 (values 1.196-1.583). The general formulas of the same code give QS_sigma_gen = 1.004-1.187 vs QS_mu_gen = 1.306-1.583, max difference 0.396. The duplicate-RHS scan finds the 2059-character right-hand sides at lines 920 and 972 token-for-token identical.
- **Expected:** QS_sigma = lensing (Weyl) response, Sigma = mu(1+gamma)/2, differing from mu whenever the slip gamma != 1 (as QS_sigma_gen does).
- **Root cause:** EFTCAMBModelComputeQSSigma assigns to eft_cache%QS_sigma the right-hand side of EFTCAMBModelComputeQSMu.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** Implement the QS Sigma expression in EFTCAMBModelComputeQSSigma (e.g. Sigma = mu*(1+gamma_QS)/2 from the same quasi-static coefficients, or reuse the EFTCAMBModelComputeQSMuSigma expression), and add a test that QS_sigma == QS_sigma_gen in models where both apply.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; Reproduced exactly. The source comparison shows the right-hand side assigned to eft_cache%QS_sigma in EFTCAMBModelComputeQSSigma (06_abstract_EFTCAMB_model.f90:972) is character-for-character identical to the QS_mu right-hand side at line 920 (2524 chars, Python string equality True). No model overrides compute_QS_Sigma. equations.f90:3766-3768 calls compute_QS_mu, then compute_QS_Sigma, then compute_QS_Mu_Sigma, so the QS_sigma field always equals QS_mu. My independent check used a different code path (harness.run_model with evol_k on A0) and the analytic f(R) quasi-static relations Sigma = 1/(1+Omega) and mu(k->inf) = (4/3)/(1+Omega). QS_sigma_gen matches 1/(1+Omega) to 4e-16. QS_mu tends to (4/3)/(1+Omega) as k grows (difference 1.2e-6 at k=10). QS_sigma misses 1/(1+Omega) by 0.39. So the defect is in EFTCAMB code, not upstream CAMB and not the test. It is not new with the rebase: branch eftcamb-pre-rebase has the same duplicated expression (identical after removing whitespace), so it comes from original EFTCAMB. Impact is limited to the diagnostic QS_sigma output (get_scale_evolution and the debug/scale output). QS_sigma does not feed into the Boltzmann evolution, so Cls and P(k) are unaffected. That keeps it at S2 (silently wrong user-facing output) rather than S1. The proposed fix (Sigma = mu*(1+gamma)/2, or reuse the QS_sigma_gen expression) is correct in direction and physics-changing only for this output.

### F-035 [S2, confirmed] Third stability sampling pass ('log sampling close to aend') never updates y: re-tests a = astart 1000 times

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:127`
- **Track:** t2d-static; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/stability-third-pass-stale-y.py`
- **Observed:** Unmodified debug tree ADD, 2_PEFT_Omega_const_1, dumped stability points (re-run in attempt 4, identical): 3000. Pass 1: 1000 unique a in [1e-10, 1]. Pass 2: 1000 unique a. Pass 3: 1 unique a, range [1.000000e-10, 1.000000e-10]. Closest point to aend in pass 3 is aend - a = 1.0 (expected 1.0e-10).
- **Expected:** Pass 3 samples 1000 log-spaced points clustering at aend (closest aend - a = 1e-10).
- **Root cause:** In the loop at l.126-136, `Atest = aend + (astart - aend)*10._dl**y` reuses the y left over from pass 2 (y = 0), so Atest = astart every iteration. Narrow instabilities just below a=1 are missed (track 6 showed one with stability-sampler-stale-y.py).
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** Add `y = LogSamplingScale + real(ind - 1)*(0._dl - LogSamplingScale)/real(indMax - 1)` before the Atest line (applied in work/t2d-static/FIX2, fix2.diff).
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; Static: in 09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90 l.123-136 the third loop computes Atest = aend + (astart-aend)*10**y but never assigns y. Pass 2 ends with y = L + 999*(-L)/999 = 0, so every pass-3 point is astart. Pass 3 is reached only if passes 1 and 2 both succeeded, so the stale y is always the final y of pass 2. The defect is in EFTCAMB's own code, not upstream CAMB: the pre-rebase branch eftcamb-pre-rebase (76b5698) has the same bug at l.125, so it predates the rebase. The finding's repro, run as given on ADD, gives exactly the numbers reported. An independent physical check on the release build A0 shows the consequence: a narrow ghost just below a=1 is wrongly declared stable. With the one-line fix (FIX2 tree) the same model is flagged unstable. This duplicates track 6's finding stability-sampler-stale-y (same root cause, same fix), so the two should be merged. S2 fits: only instabilities narrower than the linear-pass spacing (~1e-3) near a=1 are missed. Fix class: physics-changing, because accept/reject decisions change.

### F-036 [S2, confirmed] EFTCAMB library code uses MpiStop / stop / error stop (131 sites, 18 in per-k reachable code) and kills the Python interpreter instead of raising CAMBError

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/08f_full_models/008p3_Kmouflage.f90:438` (also: fortran/equations.f90:2361 (EFT_IC_type, inside OMP k loop), fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_main.f90:250,256,409, fortran/eftcamb/06_abstract_EFTCAMB_model.f90:1194,1210,1225, fortran/eftcamb/04f_parametrizations_1D/*: integral() stubs 04p004:267, 04p009:417, 04p010:440, 04p011:513, 04p012:256, 04p013:267, 04p014:269, 04p015:274, 04p019_power_law_sum:295; 04p019_spline5:138,146,155,268,325,355,544,600,689,694, fortran/eftcamb/02_interpolation_linear_1D.f90:133, 02_equispaced_interpolation_linear_1D.f90:163, 02_root_finding.f90:60 (stop), 02_interpolation.f90:75, all model ParameterNames/Latex/Values out-of-range MpiStops (007p1:486,658,816,974; 007p2:456,620,741,862; 008p0:965,1361,1490,1619,2722,3406,3407,3751; ...), fortran/equations.f90:1975, fortran/cmbmain.f90:265,617 (debug stop inside the k loop), full list: work/t2d-static/stops.txt)
- **Track:** t2d-static; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/stop-kills-python.py`
- **Observed:** A0, child process with camb.read_ini + get_results inside try/except camb.CAMBError (re-run in attempt 4, identical). (1) 5_Kmouflage_1 + massive neutrinos: exit code 1, last output 'The value of Num_Nu_Massive should be 0 / EFTCAMB error / ERROR STOP', no exception, interpreter dead. (2) 2_PEFT_Omega_const_1 + EFT_IC_type=4: 'Invalid EFT_IC_type ... / ERROR STOP' from inside the OpenMP k loop, interpreter dead. (3) EFTCAMB_use_background=T for a non-Horndeski model: 'EFTCAMB_use_background=True currently only supported by the Horndeski module / ERROR STOP' during read_ini, interpreter dead.
- **Expected:** Unsupported or invalid settings raise camb.CAMBError (global_error_flag / GlobalError / eft_error); the interpreter survives.
- **Root cause:** MpiStop (forutils) ends with error stop. EFTCAMB uses it for parameter validation, unsupported features, and integral()/derivative not implemented in parametrizations, including 18 sites reachable from per-k code inside OMP parallel regions: 1D integral() of exponential, taylor, pade, fourier, exp2, double-exp, cosine, axion, power-law-sum; spline5 3rd/4th derivative and InitIntegration; interpolation Init; base compute_DeltaTau/DeltaCosmicT/SoundHorizon.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** Replace MpiStop in library paths by setting eft_error / global_error_flag with a message (CAMB's GlobalError), returning, and checking the flag at the call site (read_ini, initialize_background, derivs via global_error_flag). Keep error stop only for the stand-alone executable.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I reproduced the finding as given and with a separate method, and could not explain it away. The given repro (read_ini path, tree A0) kills the child process in all 3 cases: exit code 1, ERROR STOP, no CAMBError raised.

My independent check used a different code path: camb.set_params(dark_energy_model="EFTCAMB") followed by EFTCAMB.initialize_parameters(dict) and get_results, run in child processes under harness.slot(1). It shows the same thing:
- K-mouflage with mnu=0.06 dies.
- Pure EFT with EFTCAMB_use_background=T dies in initialize_parameters. That function already has an integer error return that it turns into a ValueError, so the existing error channel is simply bypassed here.
- A case the original repro did not test: designer f(R) with EFTwDE=7 (Fourier) or EFTwDE=11 (exponential_2) dies with "...Integral is not implemented / Calculations cannot proceed / ERROR STOP". These are selector values the allocator accepts, through 007p3 `case default` -> allocate_parametrized_1D_function. That makes this a crash reachable with input the code accepts, in a supported model.

The control run (unmodified 4_Des_F_R_wCDM_1) finishes normally, so the deaths are not caused by the script.

Attribution: the defect is in code under audit. The cited sites are in fortran/eftcamb/: 008p3:438 (Kmouflage), 09_EFTCAMB_main.f90:250 (use_background) and the 04p011 Fourier integral stub. equations.f90:2361 lies inside an EFTCAMB MOD START block (opened at line 2224), so it is EFTCAMB code, not upstream. forutils MpiStop ends with `error stop`, as the finding says. The pattern also exists in B1, so it was inherited from earlier EFTCAMB, not added by the rebase.

Severity: S2 holds. Most sites reject unsupported or invalid settings, which is robustness (S3-like). But the integral() stubs are reached by accepted parametrization selectors, and they kill the interpreter, which brings down any Python sampler.

Minor caveats:
- My grep counts 137 matching lines in fortran/eftcamb/ against the stated 131. That difference does not matter.
- Calling the fix "safe-local" is optimistic, because propagating errors out of OMP regions and integral functions needs call-site checks. Either way, the change only affects the error path.

### F-037 [S2, confirmed] EFTflag=0 (GR) non-flat runs: low-ell TT differs from CAMB because the polterddot rewrite drops Kf(2)

- **Location:** `fortran/equations.f90:3858`
- **Track:** t3-gr; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/t3-polterddot-curvature-gr.py`
- **Observed:** Both runs with Reion.max_redshift=50. Max |dTT/TT| at l<30: 6.41e-3 (omk=-0.05), 2.49e-4 (+0.05), 8.39e-5 (-0.01), all at l=2. Max |dTE|/sqrt(TT EE): 2.0e-3, 3.4e-4, 4.7e-5. At l>=100: ~1e-6. Flat: 1e-13.
- **Expected:** Agreement with CAMB v2 to ~1e-13 (rounding) for EFTflag=0 at any omk.
- **Root cause:** The EFTCAMB block 'polterddot from the photon equations' (equations.f90:3857-3862) is active for every model. It writes pigddot with -3/5 k octgdot and E2ddot with -k/3 Edot(3), without CAMB's curvature factor Kf(2)=1-8K/k^2 (denlk2(2)=3/5 k Kf(2)). In flat space it is algebraically identical to the original formula. Overlaps polterddot-nonflat-gr.py from another track.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** Use `- 3._dl/5._dl*k*EV%Kf(2)*octgdot` in pigddot and `- k/3._dl*EV%Kf(2)*Edot(3)` in E2ddot. Tested in tree R3 (with max_redshift=50): vs B0, omk=+0.05 5.2e-13, -0.05 1.3e-13, -0.01 3.0e-14, flat 2.1e-13.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; The repro reproduces the numbers exactly. I then built two separate copies of A0. One adds EV%Kf(2) only to the octupole term in pigddot ("P"). The other adds it only to Edot(3) in E2ddot ("E"). Both builds use max_redshift=50 and are compared with B0. Each half of the dropped Kf(2) accounts for part of the effect. P removes most of the omk=+0.05 difference (2.49e-4 -> 2.65e-6). E removes most of the omk=-0.05 difference (6.41e-3 -> 3.89e-4). The original report found that the two fixes together restore a match with CAMB to about 1e-13. EE is identical to B0 to about 1e-14 at every omk, so polterddot affects only the temperature source and not the evolved hierarchy. Flat space is bit-level identical. The cause is therefore in the code under audit (equations.f90:3857-3862), not upstream CAMB and not the test. Attribution of the two parts:
(a) Dropping Kf(2) in pigddot is a real error. The photon equation the code actually evolves has pigdot = denlk(2) qg - denlk2(2) octg - ..., with denlk2(2) = 3/5 k Kf(2) (equations.f90:2164, 3399). So pigddot is not the time derivative of the evolved pigdot when K is nonzero.
(b) The E2ddot part is more subtle. The E2 evolution equation (equations.f90:3418, the same as upstream camb:2565) is `-opacity*(E2-polter) - k/3*E3`, without Kf(2). So the EFTCAMB E2ddot is consistent with the E2 equation the code evolves. Upstream CAMB's own polterddot uses Kf(2)*Edot(3), which makes upstream internally inconsistent here. The proposed fix restores upstream's output, which matches the stated expectation of agreeing with CAMB. Whether Kf(2) belongs on E3 is an upstream question, and the reviewer should be told this.
Severity: S2 is appropriate. The temperature output is wrong in a parameter region (curved models, GR and EFT alike) and the effect is confined to low ell (6.4e-3 at l=2 for omk=-0.05, about 1e-6 at l>=100). That is far below cosmic variance, so it is a modest practical impact, but it is still a silent deviation from CAMB. fix_class "physics-changing" is right.

### F-038 [S2, confirmed] Number-counts RSD source misses dark energy for EFT models while EFT perturbations are off (GR branch)

- **Location:** `fortran/equations.f90:1698`
- **Track:** t3-gr; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/t3-counts-rsd-gr-branch-missing-de.py /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-marco-IW-eftcamb-update-EFTCAMB/2b9dddd0-ab26-46fb-8810-571fb7648a45/scratchpad/audit/work/t3-gr/R6`
- **Observed:** Pure EFT with zero functions (= LCDM) vs B0 LCDM with the base_params windows (counts z=0.5 with RSD, lensing window): W1xW1 6.85e-3, W1xW2 4.32e-3, PxW1 8.34e-3. Without RSD: 1.4e-7. With the EFT path forced: 1.4e-7. With the fix (tree R6): 1.4e-7.
- **Expected:** Source-window C_l agree with LCDM to <=1e-4.
- **Root cause:** For EFTflag != 0, derivs removes the DE from grho and gpres (the background comes from the EFT model). When EV%EFTCAMBactive is false (before the RGR/turn-on time, or for any model near GR) the GR branch uses (4 adotoa^2 + gpres + grho/3) and misses gpres_DE + grho_DE/3.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** In the GR branch, use the Friedmann identity: replace `gpres + grho/3.d0` by `merge(gpres + grho/3.d0, -2._dl*EV%eft_cache%Hdot, CP%EFTCAMB%EFTflag == 0)`. Tested in R6 (6.85e-3 -> 1.36e-7).
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I reproduced the defect and confirmed it with a second, independent check. The cause is in EFTCAMB code, not upstream CAMB and not the test. When EFTflag /= 0, derivs sets grhov_t = 0 (A0 fortran/equations.f90:2745-2755, "EFTCAMB MOD: remove standard DE background density"). So the grho and gpres passed to output_window_sources (call at :3920, with gpres reset to gpres_noDE + w*grhov_t at :3692) contain no dark energy. The EFTCAMB-modified GR branch at equations.f90:1696-1698 (taken when EFTflag==0 .or. .not. EV%EFTCAMBactive) still uses 4*adotoa**2 + gpres + grho/3. That drops gpres_DE + grho_DE/3 = -(2/3) grhov_t for Lambda.

The stated root cause and file:line are right. The proposed fix -2*eft_cache%Hdot equals gpres + grho/3 by the Friedmann acceleration equation, and Hdot = -(grho + 3 gpres)/6 holds with or without curvature. eft_cache%Hdot is filled whenever EFTflag /= 0, through compute_rhoQPQ at :2790.

The pre-rebase branch B1 has the same code (B1 equations.f90:1554) and gives the same error, so this bug predates the rebase.

S2 fits: only the number-counts RSD source is affected, and only while EFT perturbations are off. That covers models at or near the GR point (EFT never switches on) and any epoch before the RGR/turn-on time. The error grows as dark energy dominates at the window redshift.

### F-039 [S2, confirmed] Designer f(R) near GR: the return-to-GR threshold, not the physics, sends small-scale P(k) to LCDM

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_RGR.f90:164`
- **Track:** t3-gr; reported severity S2, confidence medium
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/t3-fr-rgr-switch-gr-approach.py`
- **Observed:** 4_Des_F_R_LCDM_1, P(k)-only. P(k)/P_LCDM-1 at k=0.31, 1, 3.1, 7.3/Mpc: B0=1.2e-7 (RGR a=0.73): +5.1e-3, +4.1e-2, +1.0e-1, +1.19e-1. B0=7.9e-8 (0.855): ..., +5.1e-2. B0=6.0e-8 (0.955): ..., +1.4e-2. B0=5.2e-8 (RGR 1.1): +3.2e-6, +5.1e-6, +6.1e-6, +7.5e-6. A forced run (threshold=-1) at B0=3e-8 times out after 480 s.
- **Expected:** Below the Compton scale (k_C ~ H0/sqrt(B0) ~ 1/Mpc here) the f(R) enhancement stays O(10%), varying smoothly with B0, not collapsing within a factor 2.3 in B0.
- **Root cause:** EFTCAMBReturnToGR turns on the EFT perturbations at the first a where any EFT function exceeds EFTCAMB_GR_threshold (1e-8, absolute). For f(R), Omega ~ B0-scaled can stay below 1e-8 until a~1 while the effect on P(k) at k >> k_C is O(1/3) in G_eff. Overlaps fr-rgr-threshold-high-k-pk.py from another track.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** Make the RGR criterion observable-based. Compare the EFT corrections to the GR Einstein-equation terms at the largest k used: switch on when max(|Omega|, |Omega'|...) * (k_max/(aH))^2 > threshold. Or for designer f(R) apply the threshold to B(a)*(k_max/aH)^2. At least document that GR_threshold must be lowered for small B0. Not tested.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I ran the repro as given and it reproduces exactly. It matches the earlier run digit for digit: P/P_GR-1 at k=7.3/Mpc is +0.119 (B0=1.2e-7, RGR at a=0.731), +0.051 (7.9e-8), +0.014 (6.0e-8) and +7.5e-6 (5.2e-8, RGR time 1.1, so the switch never fires). The result is set by the threshold, not by the physics. With EFTCAMB_GR_threshold lowered, the results converge: thresholds 1e-11 and 1e-12 differ by less than 1% relative. The converged deviations are much larger than the default ones and vary smoothly with B0: +0.234 (1.2e-7), +0.201 (7.9e-8), +0.180 (6.0e-8), +0.168 (5.2e-8), +0.132 (3.0e-8). So the default threshold of 1e-8 is wrong in two ways. It zeroes the effect for B0 below about 5.5e-8. It also underestimates the effect by a factor of 2 even where the switch does fire (B0=1.2e-7: 0.119 against 0.234).

For an independent check I solved the quasi-static f(R) growth equation separately in Python. It uses only the EFTCAMB background functions (f_R, f_RR, H) and mu=(1+4Q)/((1+3Q)(1+f_R)), and it agrees with the converged low-threshold CAMB runs. At B0=1.2e-7 it gives k=0.31/1/3.1/7.3: +2.84e-3/+2.41e-2/+0.110/+0.232, against CAMB at threshold 1e-12: +2.93e-3/+2.66e-2/+0.110/+0.234. At B0=5.2e-8 it gives +1.25e-3/+1.18e-2/+6.78e-2/+0.167, against CAMB +1.29e-3/+1.31e-2/+6.78e-2/+0.168.

Attribution: the defect is in EFTCAMB, not upstream CAMB and not the test. It is not a rebase regression: B1 (pre-rebase EFTCAMB) gives the same numbers (B0=5.2e-8 at threshold 1e-8: -6.4e-6; at threshold 1e-10: +0.192).

The root cause is right. 09_EFTCAMB_RGR.f90:164, `if (any(eft_functions - RGR_threshold > 0._dl))`, applies an absolute 1e-8 threshold to the EFT functions. At B0=5.2e-8, |f_R(a=1)| is 9.7e-9, below 1e-8, so the perturbations are never switched on. Yet f_RR(a=1)=0.159 Mpc^2 puts the Compton scale at k_C≈1.45/Mpc, inside the P(k) range.

One correction to the finding: the repro says there is "no cheap way" to get the right answer, and that is overstated. The threshold=-1 run times out, but threshold=1e-11 converges and runs in about 57 s (P(k)-only), against about 1 s at the default. Severity stays S2 because this is a parameter region of a supported model where the error is silent.

### F-040 [S2, confirmed] Standard pi initial condition is singular where C + k^2 D1 + k^4 D2 crosses zero: single-k P(k) spikes that do not vanish near GR

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:295`
- **Track:** t3-gr; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/t3-pi-ic-singular-denominator.py`
- **Observed:** 3_RPH_MK_const_1 (alpha_K=0.1), P(k) vs LCDM: RPHmassP0=0: max 2.33e-5 (median 3.9e-6). 1e-9: 2.32e-5. 1e-7: 2.76e-3 at k=0.540. 1e-6: 2.24e-3 at k=0.169. At a=0.0103, C=-6.84e-14, k^2 D1=+6.94e-14, den=+9.6e-16 at k=0.1694, so -E/den=+817 (vs ~64 at k=0.183). Same in B1. Full test-ini settings give up to 1.9e-2 with accuracy_boost=2.
- **Expected:** |P/P_GR-1| smooth in k and -> ~1e-5 as RPHmassP0 -> 0.
- **Root cause:** EFT_IC_type=1 sets pi=-h0 E/(C+k^2D1+k^4D2), guarded only by `/= 0`. When C and D1 have opposite signs there is k*=sqrt(-C/D1) ~ alpha_M^(-1/2) where pi_ini blows up for neighbouring k modes. The spike moves to higher k as alpha_M -> 0 but does not shrink.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** Regularise. If |C+k^2D1+k^4D2| < 1e-3*(|C|+k^2|D1|+k^4|D2|), or if C and D1 have opposite signs, start from pi=pi'=0 (or the radiation-era series of IC types 2/3) and let pi relax. Not tested.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I ran the repro unchanged and got the numbers the finding reports. I then ran a separate check that changes the initial-condition type, the turn-on time and the k sampling. The spike comes from EFT_IC_type=1. It moves with the turn-on time, which is what you expect if k* = sqrt(-C/D1) is set by C and D1 at turn-on. With dense k sampling it gets much larger: 1170% on the single mode nearest k*. At RPHmassP0=1e-8 it moves to k~1.7, about 10x the 1e-6 position, consistent with k* ~ alpha_M^(-1/2). Shrinking alpha_M does not make it go away. The cause is the denominator guard at fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:295, which only checks `/= 0`. The pre-rebase B1 tree has the same code (09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:282), so this is an older EFTCAMB defect, not one introduced by the rebase and not upstream CAMB. Severity stays S2: it only hits a parameter region where C and D1 have opposite signs at turn-on, and it is a narrow k feature. With the default k grid the error is ~1e-3 to 1e-2. Note on the proposed fix: the fallback to IC types 2/3 is not safe for this model. Forcing EFT_IC_type=2 or 3 gives P(k) errors up to 5e5 or inf at high k, so the regularisation should fall back to pi=pi'=0 or to a properly derived attractor.

### F-041 [S2, confirmed] K-mouflage/K-mimic, ADE and quintessence background tables too coarse: ~1e-4 C_l, 3-7e-5 H(z) error floor at default settings

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/08f_full_models/008p3_Kmouflage.f90` (also: fortran/eftcamb/08f_full_models/008p2_ADE.f90, fortran/eftcamb/08f_full_models/008p4_quintessence.f90:170)
- **Track:** t3-gr; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/t3-bg-table-resolution-gr-floor.py`
- **Observed:** vs EFTflag=0 at the same cosmology (max|dH/H|, TT, EE, P(k)). K-mouflage eps2_0=-1e-8: 6.9e-5, 1.0e-4, 2.0e-4, 5.0e-5; with 10x points: 7.4e-7, 1.2e-6, 1.3e-6, 5.5e-6. ADE f_ac=1e-8: 3.2e-5, 5.4e-5, 1.1e-4, 2.7e-5; 10x: 3.0e-7, 5.3e-7, 1.1e-6, 6.7e-7. Quintessence exp. potential lambda=3e-3 (physical ~1e-6): 4.7e-5, 6.6e-5, 1.3e-4, 3.2e-5; 10x: 6.0e-7, 1.4e-6, 2.8e-6, 5.7e-6. The lambda scan saturates at 1.1e-4 for lambda<=0.01.
- **Expected:** Deviation -> ~1e-6 as the coupling -> 0, so that the default numerical error is well below 1e-4.
- **Root cause:** The models tabulate the background EFT functions on model_background_num_points points equispaced in ln a (1000 / 1500 / 1100 points over ~20 e-folds) and read them with linear interpolation (equispaced_linear_interpolation_1D). The second-order interpolation error (d ln a)^2/8 * f''/f ~ 1e-4 is a model-independent floor.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** Raise the default model_background_num_points about 10x (K-mouflage 10000, ADE 15000, quintessence 11000; cost is small), or interpolate with cubic splines (the code already has a cubic 1D interpolation class).
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I ran the repro as given and got the reported numbers exactly. An independent check at the function level, using get_background and get_eft_functions with no C_l and no harness.compare, gives the same attribution. In the coupling->0 limit EFTLambda/a^2 should be constant. Instead its mid-cell bump equals cosh(h)-1 to 4 digits for all three models, which is the exact linear-interpolation error of e^{2x} on the table spacing h. The H(z) deviation from EFTflag=0 tracks the predicted 0.5*Omega_DE*lin_err sawtooth with correlation 1.0000 at default N. A 10x denser table lowers it 100x, as h^2 predicts. The floor does not move with AccuracyBoost=2 / lAccuracyBoost=2: K-mouflage still gives dH/H 6.9e-5, TT 1.0e-4, EE 2.0e-4. So CAMB's accuracy settings cannot remove it, and check_accuracy will not detect it. The defect is in EFTCAMB, not upstream CAMB. It is also not a rebase regression: B1 has the same defaults (1000/1500/1100 points, equispaced_linear_interpolation_1D), and no test ini overrides model_background_num_points for these models. The stated root cause is right: linear interpolation on tables equispaced in ln a at default model_background_num_points, 008p3_Kmouflage.f90:139, 008p2_ADE.f90:133, 008p4_quintessence.f90:170. On severity, a ~1e-4 C_l error is below CAMB's ~1e-3 default target, but it is systematic, silent and cannot be fixed with accuracy settings, so S2 ('silently poor accuracy') stands; the borderline case for S3 is noted.

### F-042 [S2, confirmed] Pure EFT constant Gamma1 rejected as mass-unstable as Gamma1 -> 0 (mu_2 grows like 1/Gamma1^2)

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/06_abstract_EFTCAMB_model.f90:874` (also: fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:383)
- **Track:** t3-gr; reported severity S2, confidence medium
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/t3-gamma1-mass-instability-near-gr.py`
- **Observed:** 2_PEFT_gamma1_const_1 (LCDM background, only constant Gamma1). Gamma1=1e-2: ok. 1e-3: rejected, mu_2/H^2=-5.0e2. 1e-5: -2.9e6. 1e-7: -3.6e9. 1e-9: ok (RGR switches to GR). All functions zero with the EFT path forced: mu_2/H^2=-2.3e16. Same in B1.
- **Expected:** No mass term for a shift-symmetric pi action (only M_2^4): accepted for all Gamma1, approaching LCDM.
- **Root cause:** EFT_mu2 is built as a ratio divided by powers of F1, F2, F3, which vanish as Gamma1 -> 0. Rounding/near-zero denominators give large negative mu_2 at the first sampled time after turn-on (a=0.0100), so EFT_mass_stability rejects the model.
- **Proposed fix (unknown):** Reformulate the mass eigenvalues so they do not divide by vanishing kinetic factors (or skip the mass test when the kinetic matrix is near-degenerate relative to GR), and test against an analytic zero for shift-symmetric models. Not tested.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; The finding is real, but its root cause is only partly right.

**Reproduction.** I ran the repro unchanged on A0 and got the reported numbers:
- Gamma1 = 1e-2: ok
- Gamma1 = 1e-3: rejected, mu_2/H^2 = -0.5025E+03
- Gamma1 = 1e-5: rejected, -0.2905E+07
- Gamma1 = 1e-7: rejected, -0.3594E+10
- Gamma1 = 1e-9: ok (RGR time 1.1, so the model has switched to GR)

Every rejection happens at a = 0.1001E-01, the first step after turn-on.

**Controls.**
- Gamma1 = 1e-3 with EFT_mass_stability=F runs ok, so the mass check alone causes the rejection.
- B1 (pre-rebase) also rejects Gamma1 = 1e-3, but reports a different mu_2/H^2 = -0.1580E+03. The same rejection with a different value points to rounding noise, not deterministic physics.

**Independent check (different method).** I transcribed EFTCAMBModelComputeMassFactors from the audited source into Python and evaluated it in double precision and in 60-digit mpmath. I used two backgrounds: CAMB's own cache at a = 0.01 (which includes radiation; (H^2 - Hdot)/(rho_m/2) = 1.11) and an exact matter+Lambda background.
- **In exact arithmetic mu_2 is finite and harmless:** mu_2/H^2 = -2.31e-6 x Gamma1 on the CAMB background and -1.87e-6 x Gamma1 on the exact one, for every Gamma1 from 1 down to 1e-12. So the formula has the correct GR limit, and the model should be accepted.
- **In double precision the same expression gives noise of order 1/Gamma1^2.** On the CAMB background: 274 at 1e-3, 1.9e6 at 1e-5, 4.0e10 at 1e-7, 7e16 at 1e-12. On the exact background: 127, 2.1e6, 6.4e9. Even at Gamma1 = 1 the double result is 2.7e-4 against a true -2.3e-6. The magnitudes match the code's output; the sign is arbitrary because it comes from rounding.

**Attribution.** This is a numerical defect in EFTCAMB's own code, not upstream CAMB and not a test artefact. It is catastrophic cancellation in the closed-form mu_1/mu_2 expressions at 06_abstract_EFTCAMB_model.f90 (the EFT_mu2 assignment, around line 874), acted on by the check at 09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:383. It was already present in B1.

**Where the stated root cause is wrong.** The finding says F1, F2 and F3 vanish as Gamma1 -> 0. They do not: for this model F1 = 2, F2 = 2H and F3 = 4 h0^2 Gamma1 - 6H^2/a^2 -> -6H^2. The quantity that vanishes is the combination 3F2^2 + F1*F3 = 8 h0^2 Gamma1 (the kinetic coefficient of pi). It sits in the denominators (3F2^2+F1F3)^2 and (3 + F1F3/F2^2). In double precision it is computed as 12H^2 - 12H^2 + 8 h0^2 Gamma1, and since H(a=0.01)^2 is much larger than h0^2, about six extra digits are lost on top of the 1/Gamma1 amplification. The numerators cancel analytically and the leftover rounding error is divided by Gamma1^2. A fix should rewrite the formula in terms of 3F2^2+F1F3 as computed directly from the EFT functions (no subtraction), or simplify it symbolically. Skipping the mass test when the kinetic matrix is near-degenerate would also stop the rejection.

**Severity.** S2 stands. EFT_mass_stability defaults to .false. (09_EFTCAMB_main.f90:222), so this only affects users who turn the check on, in a parameter region (small Gamma1, about 1e-8 to 1e-3 here) next to GR. There it wrongly rejects stable models. That is an edge region, not wrong physics in a default run.

### F-043 [S2, confirmed] Stability check evaluates dtauda with zero matter density (grhoa2 never set); grhoa2 also wrong in derivs and python bridge

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:191` (also: fortran/equations.f90:2792, fortran/eftcamb/11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:418)
- **Track:** t3-gr; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/t3-stability-dtauda-without-matter.py /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-marco-IW-eftcamb-update-EFTCAMB/2b9dddd0-ab26-46fb-8810-571fb7648a45/scratchpad/audit/work/t3-gr/R5`
- **Observed:** Omega_Lambda_gamma: OLOmega0=1e-2, 1e-4 ok; 1e-6 and the GR point rejected with 'Model dtauda is Nan' at a=1e-10. Same rejection for Omega_Lambda_alpha (GR, 1e-6...), K-mouflage and K-mimic at eps2_0=0. All accepted with skip_stability. With grhoa2 set (tree R5), the dtauda-NaN rejections disappear (1e-6 and the GR point are then rejected by the ghost check, kinetic -5.0e-3 / -9.4e-3, from the c ~ -2.6e-4 residue of the designer at a=1e-9). get_eft_functions returns grhoa2 = 1.401e25 at a=1e-6 vs grhom_t*a^2 = 1.401e-11.
- **Expected:** dtauda in the stability check includes matter and radiation (H^2 > 0 here), and grhoa2 = 8 pi G rho a^4 everywhere.
- **Root cause:** EFTStabilityComputation fills grhom_t, grhob_t, ... but never eft_cache%grhoa2 (0 from initialize). compute_dtauda (EFTCAMBFullModelComputeDtauda: (grhoa2 + 2c a^2 - Lambda a^2)/3...) then tests the sign of the DE part alone, giving NaN when 2c-Lambda<0. Elsewhere grhoa2 uses the wrong power: derivs (equations.f90:2792) sets grho/a2**2, and the python bridge (11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:418) sets grhom_t/a**4. Both should be grho*a^2.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** In 09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90 after line 476 add `eft_cache%grhoa2 = eft_cache%grhom_t*a**2` (tested in R5). In equations.f90:2792 use `grho*a2`; in 11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:418 use `cache%grhom_t*a**2`.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; The stability check's dtauda test is wrong. EFTStabilityComputation (09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:453-476) fills grhob_t, grhoc_t, grhor_t, grhog_t, grhonu_tot and grhom_t but never eft_cache%grhoa2. That field stays at 0 from initialize() (03_abstract_EFTCAMB_cache.f90:419). EFTCAMBFullModelComputeDtauda (06p1_abstract_EFTCAMB_full_map.f90:81-82) is the only compute_dtauda that reads grhoa2, so for every full-mapping model line 191 only checks the sign of (2c - Lambda)a^2. A model is rejected whenever c < Lambda/2 at early times, even though H^2 > 0.

The given repro could not run as shipped. Its ini (audit/inis/007p5-OL-gamma.ini) points DEFAULT at /Users/marco/Desktop/IW/..., which no longer exists, so all 12 runs stopped with "include file not found". That is a problem with the test environment, not a refutation. I reran the same scan with the DEFAULT path corrected and the claim reproduced.

As an independent check, I rebuilt the dtauda argument from the cached EFT functions of runs with skip_stability. Without the matter term it is negative; with it, it is positive. The model is physically fine; the check is what fails.

The wrong grhoa2 power in derivs (equations.f90:2792, grho/a2**2) and in the python bridge (11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:418, grhom_t/a**4) is also confirmed: the bridge returns grhom_t/a^4. Neither value feeds any computation (compute_dtauda is called only from dtauda(), which uses a fresh local cache set correctly at equations.f90:42, and from the stability check). Those two are therefore only wrong output fields (S3 aspect).

All three defects already exist in eftcamb-pre-rebase (equations.f90:2585, python:380, and stability:185 without grhoa2 set), so this is inherited EFTCAMB code, not a rebase regression. It is a defect in EFTCAMB, not upstream CAMB.

One caveat on the claimed impact: with the fix (R5), the OL-gamma GR point and OLOmega0=1e-6 are still rejected, now by the ghost check. That comes from the c ~ -1e-5 residue of the background solver, a separate issue. The fix alone therefore does not get the GR limit accepted. It does remove a bogus rejection criterion.

S2 is right: false rejections happen only in a parameter region where 2c < Lambda at early times. Fix class is physics-changing, since it changes which models are accepted. Not independently checked: the K-mouflage, K-mimic and Omega_Lambda_alpha rejections listed in the finding.

### F-044 [S2, confirmed] Early-turn-on EFT models (5_ADE_1, turn-on a=1e-10) refused with a hard error when l_max_scalar is lowered to 2000

- **Location:** `fortran/equations.f90:2188`
- **Track:** t3-gr; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/t3-eft-turnon-before-tauminn-lmax.py`
- **Observed:** 5_ADE_1: l_max_scalar=2500 ok. l_max_scalar=2000: error 'EFTCAMB starts before thermo tauminn, EFT_pert_turn_on, EFTturnOnTime, tauminn = 0.10E-09, 0.46E-04, 0.50E-04'.
- **Expected:** The run succeeds for any valid l_max/kmax.
- **Root cause:** The thermal-history start tauminn depends on kmax (it is set from the largest k), while initial() requires EFTturnOnTime >= tauminn and raises GlobalError otherwise.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** When EFTflag/=0, start the thermal history at min(tauminn, DeltaTime(0, EFTCAMB_pert_turn_on)): pass that taumin to ThermoData%Init in results.f90/cmbmain, or call EV%ThermoData%Init with the earlier time before raising. Not tested.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; Reproduced with the given repro and with an independent scan of turn-on time, l_max and tree (fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/checks/verify/v_tauminn_lmax.py). The check that refuses the run is in the EFTCAMB MOD block of initial() at fortran/equations.f90:2186-2191. It compares tau(EFTCAMB_turn_on_time) with ThermoData%tauminn. tauminn is 0.95*GetTauStart(maxq) = 0.95e-3/maxq (cmbmain.f90:912, results.f90:2150), so it grows as l_max, and with it qmax, goes down.

The check protects nothing. Every mode starts at taustart = GetTauStart(q), which is at least taumin and therefore above tauminn. When EFTturnOnTime is earlier than the mode's start, the switch loop (equations.f90:400-401) does not integrate backwards: it switches EFT on at the current tau. So no thermo lookup ever happens below tauminn, and any turn-on time below the earliest mode start gives exactly the same result. Measured: at l_max=2500, turn-on 1e-10 and turn-on 1.1e-10 give bit-identical cl_total (max rel diff 0.0 in TT and EE).

The check is not a rebase regression: B1 (pre-rebase EFTCAMB) has the same check (equations.f90:2028) and fails the same way. It is still a defect of the code under audit, not an upstream CAMB one. Root cause as stated is correct.

The proposed fix (start the thermal history earlier) would work but is not needed. A simpler safe-local fix is to drop the GlobalError, or to set EV%EFTturnOnTime = max(EV%EFTturnOnTime, EV%ThermoData%tauminn). By the bit-identity result above, this should leave all currently accepted runs unchanged; I did not build or test it. S2 is the right severity: a valid parameter combination gets a hard error (default ADE ini at l_max_scalar <= ~2000, or any early turn-on with small kmax), but no wrong physics is produced.

### F-045 [S2, confirmed] camb.set_params(cp=existing, H0=...) silently resets the EFT model to GR (EFTflag=0)

- **Location:** `camb/camb.py:236`
- **Track:** t3-gr; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/t3-set-params-cp-resets-eft-model.py`
- **Observed:** f(R) B0=0.1 params, then set_params(cp=pars, H0=68): EFTflag=0, sigma8=0.815078 (= GR) instead of EFTflag=3, sigma8=1.124474. The control fluid w=-0.9 is kept on both A0 and B0.
- **Expected:** The EFT model is preserved when no EFT keys are passed, as for other setters (do_set calls a setter only if one of its arguments is present).
- **Root cause:** EFT_do_set calls cp.EFTCAMB.initialize_parameters(cp, params) unconditionally. It re-initialises EFTCAMB from the params dict, defaulting EFTflag to 0. Overlaps set-params-cp-drops-eft-model.py from another track.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** Call EFT_do_set only when cp was created here, or when params contains any EFTCAMB key (EFTflag or any key returned by cp.EFTCAMB.read_parameters()). Otherwise leave cp.EFTCAMB untouched.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I ran the repro unchanged on A0. I checked first that A0's camb/camb.py and camb/eftcamb.py are identical to the repo checkout, and both trees reproduce the effect. The cause is at camb/camb.py:236, where EFT_do_set(cp.EFTCAMB.initialize_parameters) runs on every call. Upstream do_set only calls a setter when one of its arguments is in params, but EFT_do_set has no such guard. initialize_parameters (camb/eftcamb.py:104-144) rebuilds the EFTCAMB parameter file from the params dict alone and re-initialises it, so any key it does not find falls back to its default, including EFTflag=0. The model is replaced by GR with no warning.

To rule out the repro's own path, I used a different one. I loaded EFT models with camb.read_ini instead of set_params, then called set_params(cp=p, ns=<the same ns>). That passes no cosmology keys, so set_cosmology is not called and H0 stays at 70. Even this no-op update resets the model to GR, so the effect does not depend on set_cosmology resetting defaults.

The defect is in EFTCAMB's own modification of camb.py, not upstream CAMB and not the test. The same code is present in B1 (camb.py:196 in the pre-rebase tree) and the bug reproduces there, so it predates the rebase rather than coming from it. The CAMB fluid control keeps w=-0.9 on both A0 and B0, which shows upstream CAMB keeps its settings through this path.

The proposed fix is reasonable: call EFT_do_set only when the cp was created inside this call, or when params contains EFTflag or any EFTCAMB key. I would keep S2 rather than raise it. The results are silently wrong physics, but only through the cp= update path; models set up directly from an ini or a fresh set_params run correctly.

One side lead, not investigated: on A0, read_ini('4_Des_F_R_LCDM_1.ini') followed by WantCls=False, WantTransfer=True and set_matter_power(kmax=1.0) crashed with SIGSEGV (rc -11) in calc_transfers at results.py:355, during the first get_results. That happened before any cp reuse, so it has nothing to do with this finding. I did not attribute it to EFTCAMB or upstream; it needs separate triage.

### F-046 [S2, confirmed] integral() of cubic (9) and quintic (10) spline parametrizations wrong, so designer f(R) cannot run with spline w(a)

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/04f_parametrizations_1D/04p019_spline5_parametrizations_1D.f90:650` (also: fortran/eftcamb/04f_parametrizations_1D/04p016_spline_parametrizations_1D.f90:466-480, fortran/eftcamb/04f_parametrizations_1D/04p019_spline5_parametrizations_1D.f90:698-710, fortran/eftcamb/02_interpolation_linear_1D.f90:590-602)
- **Track:** t4a-param; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/spline-integral-convention.py`
- **Observed:** Constant w=-0.9 on all nodes (exact I=a^1.7). Cubic spline with default-like nodes 0..1: NaN at every a<1, 0 at a=1. Nodes 1e-8, 0.2..1: I(1e-6)=251.2 vs 6.3e-11, I(0.5)=4.505 vs 0.308, I(1)=0. Quintic Logx: -inf at all a<1, NaN at a=1. Designer f(R) 4_Des_F_R_wCDM_1 with this w as EFTwDE=9 or 10: CAMBError 'horizontal asymptote = NaN / FAILURE of vertical asymptote bracketing'. With the fix: 8.4e-10 (9) and 1.1e-9 (10) Cl difference vs EFTwDE=1. With the default nodes (x1=0) the cubic init divides by zero (FPE trap at 02_interpolation_linear_1D.f90:600).
- **Expected:** I(a)=a^2 exp(-3 int_1^a (1+w)/a' da'), I(1)=1, as for every analytic w(a) type; consumers use grhov_t=grhov*I(a) and EFT_E_gfun=-(ln I - 2 ln a)/3.
- **Root cause:** Cubic: integral is a spline of linear_interpolate_function_1D yint (02_interpolation_linear_1D.f90:590-602). That is exp(-3 int (1+w_lin)/x) of piecewise-linear w, without the a^2 factor, normalised at the last node instead of a=1, 0 outside [x1, xN), and (x(i)/x(i+1)) with x1=0 gives 0**neg. Quintic (04p019:650-671, 698-710): sums bcoef(1:7)*dx^j/j including bcoef(1) (the node position), never resets tmp per interval, misses the factor 3, uses (x1/x2)^3 with x=ln a (division by ln 1 = 0).
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** Replace both integrals with a numerical evaluation from value(): composite 8-point Gauss-Legendre in N=ln a, split at the nodes, I=a^2 exp(3 int_{ln a}^0 (1+w(N)) dN). Tested patch: work/t4a-param/FIX.diff (Spline1DIntegral / SplineQ1DIntegral hunks); vs quad 5.3e-15 / 4.4e-16. Drop the unused InitIntegration paths.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I ran the repro as given and got exactly the reported numbers. I then checked it a second way, with a full CAMB run on A0 through the Python wrapper and harness.run_model. Designer f(R) is 4_Des_F_R_wCDM_1. The reference uses EFTwDE=1 with w0=-0.9 and runs fine. Giving the same constant w=-0.9 as a spline makes every variant fail with a CAMBError from the background solver ('horizontal asymptote = NaN' / 'FAILURE of vertical asymptote bracketing', plus DLSODA excessive work). The variants are cubic (EFTwDE=9) with x1=0, cubic with x1=1e-8, cubic with the last node at 1.1 so that a=1 lies inside the nodes, and quintic Logx (EFTwDE=10).

The code under audit is at fault, not the test. The convention is I(a)=a^2 exp(-3 int_1^a (1+w)/a'). Constant w returns x**(-1-3w) and CPL follows the same form. Designer f(R) relies on it (007p3:471,1082,1100), and so does ShiftSym. The spline flags reach this code through the default branch of the designer's EFTwDE select, which calls allocate_parametrized_1D_function. No shipped test ini uses EFTwDE=9/10, which is why the test suite never caught this.

Root cause checked by reading the code. For the cubic spline, I re-implemented LinearIntepolateFunction1DInitIntegration in numpy. Its yint equals a^-0.3 exactly, i.e. exp(-3 int (1+w)/a) without the a^2 factor and normalised at the last node. Spline1DIntegral returns 0 for x >= x_final, so I(1)=0. For the quintic spline, poly5 takes c(0:) and the code passes bcoef(:,ip), so bcoef(1) is the node position c(0). SplineQ1DIntegral/InitIntegration therefore wrongly include it in the sum. They also never reset tmp, drop the factor 3, and use (x1/x2)^3 with x=ln a; ln 1 = 0 gives the -inf/NaN. The same code is in pre-rebase B1 (04p019 lines 647/685), so this is a long-standing EFTCAMB bug, not one introduced by the rebase.

Severity S2 stands. It only affects the spline w(a) option of designer f(R)/ShiftSym and fails loudly rather than giving silently wrong physics.

Side observation, not part of this finding: poly5 in 02p12_splines5.f90:391 uses c(6)*dx**6 where it should be dx**5. This is inconsistent with dpoly5 and the other derivatives, so quintic spline value() is also slightly wrong. It is present in B1 too. It may deserve its own finding if no one has filed it yet.

### F-047 [S2, confirmed] Designer f(R)/ShiftSym with documented EFTwDE 6, 7, 11 hit ERROR STOP (integral not implemented); 9 and 10 fail

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/07f_designer_models/007p3_Designer_fR.f90:471` (also: fortran/eftcamb/04f_parametrizations_1D/04p010_pade_series_1D.f90:438, fortran/eftcamb/04f_parametrizations_1D/04p011_fourier_parametrizations_1D.f90:511, fortran/eftcamb/04f_parametrizations_1D/04p012_exponential_2_parametrizations_1D.f90:254, fortran/eftcamb/07f_designer_models/007p7_ShiftSym_alphaB.f90:613,631)
- **Track:** t4a-param; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/wde-integral-unsupported-parametrizations.py`
- **Observed:** 4_Des_F_R_wCDM_1 with the same constant w=-0.9 written as each option. EFTwDE=6 Pade (N=D=0): 'PadeSeriesParametrized1DIntegral is not implemented', ERROR STOP kills the interpreter. 7 Fourier: same ('Fourier1DIntegral'). 11 exp2 (lambda=0): same. 9 and 10: CAMBError (see spline-integral-convention). 8 steplog with v1=v2: identical to the reference (control).
- **Expected:** Docs ('Setting and Specfying.md': the wDE parametrizations of designer f(R) are the PureEFT ones, EFTwDE 6..11) promise these options. They should run, or be rejected at read time with eft_error and a CAMBError.
- **Root cause:** Designer f(R) needs DesfRwDE%integral(a) (007p3:362, 471, 609, 1082, 1100; ShiftSym 007p7:613, 631). Pade (04p010:438), Fourier (04p011:511) and exponential_2 (04p012:254) implement integral() as MpiStop.
- **Proposed fix (unknown):** Give parametrized_function_1D a default numerical integral (the Gauss-Legendre-in-ln-a routine used in the spline fix) and use it in the types without a closed form. Alternatively, reject EFTwDE=6, 7, 11 in the designer models' read_model_selection with eft_error=1 instead of stopping at run time.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I ran the repro unchanged on A0 and every case matched the claim. I then ran my own check through a different path: camb.read_ini and get_results in a child process, without harness.run_model. That check covered a second model, ShiftSym alpha_B (007p7), and a nonzero exp2 lambda. Again, wDE=6, 7 and 11 call MpiStop, which kills the Python interpreter with ERROR STOP instead of raising a CAMBError.

The source agrees with the stated root cause. Designer f(R) and ShiftSym send EFTwDE values >= 6 to allocate_parametrized_1D_function (04p1 allocator: 6=Pade, 7=Fourier, 11=exponential_2), and nothing at read time rejects them. Their integral() functions are MpiStop stubs (04p010:438, 04p011:511, 04p012:254). They are called at 007p3:362/471/609/1082/1100 and 007p7:613/631. The docs (find_your_model/'Setting and Specfying.md' l.19, 57, 69) say designer f(R) and ShiftSym take the same wDE options as PureEFT, which covers 6 to 11.

The defect is in EFTCAMB, not in upstream CAMB or the test. It is not a regression: the B1 tree (pre-rebase) has the same "not implemented" stubs. The 9 and 10 failures (CAMBError, NaN asymptote) I saw as well, but they belong to the separate spline-integral-convention finding.

I keep S2 rather than S1: this happens only for some optional wDE choices in two designer models, and the code stops loudly instead of giving wrong numbers. It still kills the host interpreter, for example a sampler, so rejecting these options at read time with eft_error is the minimum fix.

### F-048 [S2, confirmed] Spline parametrizations return null_value instead of 0 for all derivatives outside the node range
