- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/04f_parametrizations_1D/04p019_spline5_parametrizations_1D.f90:430` (also: fortran/eftcamb/04f_parametrizations_1D/04p019_spline5_parametrizations_1D.f90:481,536,592, fortran/eftcamb/04f_parametrizations_1D/04p016_spline_parametrizations_1D.f90:380,404,426,448)
- **Track:** t4a-param; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/spline-derivatives-outside-nodes.py`
- **Observed:** w=-1 on nodes 0.01..0.9 with null_value=-1 (an exactly constant function): d1=d2=d3=d4=-1 at 8 of 10 test points (a<0.01 and a>0.9). Pure EFT (2_PEFT_Omega_lin_1) with this w as EFTwDE=9 or 10 vs EFTwDE=0: worst Cl 1.01e-4, P(k) 1.98e-3 (EFTLambdadot 6.9e-6). With the fix: 0.0.
- **Expected:** Outside the nodes the function is the constant null_value, so all derivatives are 0; spectra identical to EFTwDE=0.
- **Root cause:** Every derivative function initialises its result to self%null_value and returns it when x is outside [x_initial, x_final]: 04p019:430, 481, 536, 592 and 04p016:380, 404, 426, 448.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** Initialise the derivative results to 0._dl instead of self%null_value (value keeps null_value). Patch 'spline5_outside' in checks/t4a-param/make_fix_tree.py, plus the 04p016 hunks in work/t4a-param/FIX.diff; tested as above.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; Reproduced the defect exactly with the given repro script. An independent check on A0 then pins it to the derivative functions. The spline has the same value everywhere and identical derivatives (0) inside the nodes, so the only thing the narrow and wide node sets change is which points fall outside the node range. Node set 1 (narrow): w=-1 on nodes 0.01..0.9 with null_value=-1. Node set 2 (wide): the same w=-1 spline on nodes from 1e-12 to 1.5, so no evaluated a is ever outside the range. Wide nodes give output bit-identical to EFTwDE=0 for both flag 9 and flag 10. Narrow nodes give the reported differences. EFTLambdadot agrees with the reference for a<=0.9 (diffs of 1e-17 relative or less). For a>0.9 it changes by a factor of about 2.6 (a=1: -4.26e-11 becomes -1.10e-10), consistent with w'=-1 being returned beyond x_final. The root-cause lines are correct: each derivative is initialised to self%null_value and returned when the point is outside the nodes, at 04p019:430,481,536,592 and 04p016:380,404,426,448. The same code is in branch eftcamb-pre-rebase (04p019:408,460,515,571; 04p016:363,387,409,431). So it is an older EFTCAMB defect, not something the rebase introduced, and it is not upstream CAMB. S2 is right: the default null_value is 0, which happens to give correct derivatives of 0. The bug only shows when the user sets null_value to a non-zero value and the nodes do not cover the whole a range, which makes it an edge case/parameter region. Side note, not part of this verdict: 04p016 uses <=/>= so it also returns null_value exactly at the end nodes (a=1 when x_final=1), while 04p019 uses </>.

### F-049 [S2, confirmed] Quintic spline value uses c6*dx^6 instead of c6*dx^5 (not interpolating, discontinuous at nodes)

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/02_bundled_algorithms/02p12_splines5.f90:391`
- **Track:** t4a-param; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/spline5-poly5-dx6.py`
- **Observed:** f=0.2 sin(4a)+0.1 on 9 nodes, Spline_Order=5. Logx=F: value jump at interior nodes up to 1.54e-3 (rel), |d1 - FD(value)|/|FD| 1.73e-3. Logx=T: value at node a=0.01 is 0.00753 vs node value 0.1080 (rel 0.93), d1 vs FD 6.6e-2. With the fix: all ~0 (FD/node checks <= 1.8e-12). A smooth example (Omega=0.1 a^1.5, Logx) changed Cl vs the power-law reference only from 1.255e-3 to 1.261e-3 (earlier attempt), so the impact depends on the data.
- **Expected:** Value interpolates the nodes, is C4-continuous and consistent with dpoly5..d4poly5 (which differentiate c6*dx^5).
- **Root cause:** poly5 = c(1)+c(2)dx+c(3)dx^2+c(4)dx^3+c(5)dx^4+c(6)*dx**6 (typo); the derivative helpers use dx^5.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** 02p12_splines5.f90:391: `c(6)*dx**6` -> `c(6)*dx**5` (in work/t4a-param/FIX.diff).
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; The source at fortran/eftcamb/02_bundled_algorithms/02p12_splines5.f90:391 reads `poly5 = c(1) + c(2)*dx + c(3)*dx**2 + c(4)*dx**3 + c(5)*dx**4 + c(6)*dx**6`. The function's own comment, the coefficient definition in spline5pars ("p_j = sum_{i=1}^6 c(i,j)(x-c(0,j))^(i-1)") and dpoly5..d4poly5 all treat the last term as c(6)*dx**5, so this is a typo. I ran the given repro unchanged and got its numbers. Logx=F gives value jumps at the nodes up to 1.54e-3 and d1 vs FD 1.73e-3. Logx=T gives 0.00753 against 0.1080 at a=0.01 (0.93 relative) and d1 vs FD 6.57e-2.

My own check took a different route, without CAMB or Python ctypes. I compiled the repo module verbatim into a standalone Fortran test with -fcheck=all, with a patched copy alongside. The test fits a known global quintic on 9 non-uniform nodes with bctype=[2,2], which a correct quintic spline must reproduce exactly.
- **As is:** max error 5.6e-4, and the end of each segment misses the next node by 5.6e-4. dpoly5 disagrees with the FD of poly5 by 8.4e-2 relative.
- **With the dx**5 fix:** 3.7e-14, 8.5e-16 and 7.4e-9 respectively.

So the coefficients from spline5pars are correct, and only the value evaluator is wrong. The bug is in EFTCAMB code, not upstream CAMB. It was inherited unchanged from pre-rebase EFTCAMB (eftcamb-pre-rebase line 392 has the same dx**6), so it is not a rebase regression.

Reach: spline5_parametrization_1D (parametrization flag 10 in 04p1_parametrizations_1D_allocator.f90:106-107) defaults to Spline_Order=5. It calls poly5 for y in value and all derivative routines (04p019 lines 399, 442, 494, 547, 603). Every user who picks this parametrization at default order therefore gets a function value that does not interpolate its pixels and does not match its derivatives. No test ini in fortran/eftcamb_test/parameters uses this parametrization (grep for Spline_Order/_Logx_Flag finds none). The size of the error depends on the data, around 1e-3 relative for smooth inputs and up to O(1) near small a with Logx. That fits S2 (wrong only in a parameter region, or silently poor accuracy, in a non-default parametrization). The one-line fix is correct; fix_class is physics-changing.

### F-050 [S2, confirmed] Quintic-spline type with accepted Spline_Order=3 hits ERROR STOP in third/fourth derivative

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/04f_parametrizations_1D/04p019_spline5_parametrizations_1D.f90:541` (also: fortran/eftcamb/04f_parametrizations_1D/04p019_spline5_parametrizations_1D.f90:597-600)
- **Track:** t4a-param; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/spline5-order3-errorstop.py`
- **Observed:** 2_PEFT_Omega_lin_1 with Omega=a as PureEFTmodelOmega=10 on linear nodes. Spline_Order=5: Cl diff 2.0e-12 vs the analytic run. Spline_Order=3: error:rc=1, ERROR STOP 'Third derivative invalid for EFTOmega_Spline_Order = \x03\x00\x00\x00' kills the Python interpreter. With the fix (cubic d3=6 c4, d4=0): 8.1e-13 vs reference.
- **Expected:** init_func_from_file accepts order 3 or 5, so order 3 should run; Pure EFT always evaluates EFTOmegaPPP/PPPP.
- **Root cause:** case(3) branches of SplineQ1DThirdDerivative / SplineQ1DFourthDerivative call MpiStop (lines 541-544, 597-600) instead of returning the cubic derivatives. The message also uses format '(a,a,a,I3)' for (string, string, integer), which prints garbage.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** case(3): compute y, yx, yxx with poly3/dpoly3/d2poly3 when logx or logy; yxxx = 6*bcoef(5,ip); yxxxx = 0 (patch 'spline5_o3' in checks/t4a-param/make_fix_tree.py, tested). Fix the format to '(a,a,I3)'.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; Reproduced as reported. SplineQ1DInitFromFile (04p019 lines 226-230) accepts Spline_Order 3 or 5, but the case(3) branches of SplineQ1DThirdDerivative and SplineQ1DFourthDerivative (lines 541-544 and 597-600) call MpiStop, which is ERROR STOP. Pure EFT always evaluates these derivatives: PureEFTwDE%third_derivative in the background solver (007p1 line 1318), and EFTOmegaPPP/PPPP and Gamma*PPP at lines 1392-1400 and 1556+. So any Pure EFT function given as an order-3 quintic-spline (type 10) kills the Python interpreter. The setting is accepted at init and nothing warns before the crash.

I checked this independently on a different function (w_DE instead of Omega) and got the same failure. The same failure happens on B1, so the defect predates the rebase and is not upstream CAMB. The test itself is fine: with order 5 the identical overrides run cleanly.

The format bug is also confirmed. '(a,a,a,I3)' is given (string, string, integer), so the third 'a' consumes the integer and prints raw bytes '\x03\x00\x00\x00'. Lines 297-298 pass four items, so they are correct.

The stop looks deliberate: a cubic spline's third derivative is piecewise constant and its fourth derivative is zero. But since the model needs these derivatives anyway, a crash after the input was accepted is a defect. It stays S2: it needs a non-default option and gives an explicit fatal stop, with no memory corruption.

About the proposed fix: d3 = 6*bcoef(5,ip) indexes correctly, because poly3 uses c(0:) with c(4) as the cubic coefficient, which is bcoef(5). However, it feeds a discontinuous EFTOmegaPPP into the perturbation equations, so it is rightly classed physics-changing. A safe-local alternative is to reject order 3 at init (eft_error=1) for models that need third and fourth derivatives, or to return a non-fatal error.

### F-051 [S2, confirmed] Ini keys truncated to 20 characters: documented parameters with long names silently read as 0

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/04_abstract_parametrizations_1D.f90:343` (also: fortran/eftcamb/01_EFT_def.f90:34, fortran/eftcamb/04f_parametrizations_1D/04p016_spline_parametrizations_1D.f90:172,257, fortran/eftcamb/04f_parametrizations_1D/04p019_spline5_parametrizations_1D.f90:189,285, affected names: 007p8 Designer3p1sigma1_ODE, Designer3p1rho1N_ODE, Designer3p1rho2_ODE; 007p2 RPH*_ODE with steplog/Fourier suffixes)
- **Track:** t4a-param; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/param-name-truncation.py --model`
- **Observed:** Designer3p1sigma1_ODE0=0.7 and Designer3p1sigma1_ODEExp=2.0 are both read through key 'Designer3p1sigma1_OD', so both become 0.000. Designer3p1rho2_ODEExp is read via 'Designer3p1rho2_ODEE' as 0.000. RPHbraiding_ODE_delta and RPHkineticity_ODE_delta are read via 'RPHbraiding_ODE_delt' / 'RPHkineticity_ODE_de' as 0.000. Model: 3_RPH_KT_OmegaDE_1 with RPHkineticitymodel_ODE=8: the documented key RPHkineticity_ODE_delta=0.5 gives error:rc=1 (NaN in the cache, theory unstable); the truncated key RPHkineticity_ODE_de=0.5 gives ok.
- **Expected:** The documented (full) parameter names are the ini keys.
- **Root cause:** ParametrizedFunction1DInitFromFile stores the name in character(len=EFT_names_max_length) with EFT_names_max_length=20 (01_EFT_def.f90:34) and reads Ini%Read_Double(trim(param_name), 0._dl) (lines 353-354). Longer names are cut silently and the default 0 is used. The same buffer is used in feedback and spline InitFromFile.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** Raise EFT_names_max_length (e.g. 64), or use deferred-length character in InitFromFile/parameter_names. Also make set_name/set_param_names fail when a generated name exceeds the limit.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I ran the repro exactly as given and it reproduced every number. I then checked the claim independently through full CAMB runs on a different function (RPH braiding instead of kineticity). The root cause is correct. parameter_names stores and returns full-length names: with a len=200 buffer the repro prints the untruncated name. The only thing that cuts them is the local buffer `character(len=EFT_names_max_length) :: param_name` in ParametrizedFunction1DInitFromFile (04_abstract_parametrizations_1D.f90:343, read at 353-354), together with EFT_names_max_length=20 (01_EFT_def.f90:34). The same 20-character buffer appears in the 2D InitFromFile (05_abstract_parametrizations_2D.f90:342) and in the spline/spline5 InitFromFile. About 25 Feedback routines also use it, so long names are printed truncated there too (cosmetic only). This is an EFTCAMB defect, not upstream CAMB, and the rebase did not introduce it: B1 (pre-rebase) has the same constant (=20) and the same buffer at 04_abstract_parametrizations_1D.f90:320. Severity stays S2. No shipped test ini uses a key longer than 20 characters (I grepped all of parameters/*.ini). The defect only shows up with non-default parametrization choices (RPH *_ODE with steplog/Fourier suffixes) or in the 3+1 designer ODE functions, which have no test ini. There the failure is silent: the parameter becomes 0, and the run either gives NaN or silently uses the wrong physics. Note that Designer3p1sigma1_ODE (21 characters) can never be set from an ini under any parametrization, and Designer3p1rho1N_ODE (20 characters, plus at least one suffix character) is likewise always truncated. I could not complete a full 3+1 model run. The audit ini 007p8-3p1.ini has a broken DEFAULT path (/Users/marco/Desktop/IW/..., which does not exist). With a path-corrected copy, every variant fails with 'EFTCAMB 3+1 designer background solver failed', whatever the sigma1_ODE value or key. That is a separate issue the 3+1 track should look at.

### F-052 [S2, confirmed] Pade parametrization (flag 6) fourth derivative drops the u' factor of the 4u'g''' term

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/04f_parametrizations_1D/04p010_pade_series_1D.f90:419`
- **Track:** t4a-param; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/pade-fourth-derivative.py`
- **Observed:** Omega=(0.1+0.2(a-1)-0.3(a-1)^2)/(1+0.4(a-1)): d4 at a=0.1, 0.3, 0.5, 0.8, 1.0 = -15.40, -9.40, -6.14, -3.58, -2.63 vs exact -13.02, -7.22, -4.27, -2.12, -1.40 (rel 0.18-0.88). d3 exact. Pure EFT with a rational Pade Omega, A0 vs fixed: EFTOmegaPPPP differs by a factor of 22, Cls bit-identical. The wrong value enters EFTcdotdot/EFTLambdadotdot, which feed mass stability and positivity bounds (verdict unchanged for the tested set).
- **Expected:** Exact fourth derivative of the rational function (sympy).
- **Root cause:** Leibniz expansion of (u/g)'''' writes `4._dl*(-ddddown/down**2 ...)` instead of `4._dl*dup*(...)`.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** Line 419: `4._dl*(-ddddown/down**2 - ...)` -> `4._dl*dup*(-ddddown/down**2 - ...)` (tested, exact error 1.6e-15).
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I ran the repro script unchanged against the A0 build and it gives exactly the reported numbers: d3 matches sympy to every printed digit, and d4 is off by a relative 0.18 to 0.88. As an independent check I worked the Leibniz expansion of (u*h)'''' with h=1/g by hand. The term should be 4u'h''', with h''' = -g'''/g^2 + 6g'g''/g^3 - 6g'^3/g^4. At 04p010_pade_series_1D.f90:419 the code writes 4*h''' and leaves out the factor dup. The other four Leibniz terms, including the h'''' term, are transcribed correctly. If this were the only error, observed minus exact would equal 4(1-u')h''' exactly. For this function (g''=g'''=0, so h'''=-6g'^3/g^4), that predicts -2.38037, -1.87500, -1.45797, -1.22880 at a=0.1, 0.5, 0.8, 1.0. The measured differences are -2.38037, -1.87500, -1.45797, -1.22880, an exact match, so the stated root cause and the one-token fix (insert dup*) are right. The bug is in EFTCAMB code, not upstream CAMB. It was already present before the rebase (eftcamb-pre-rebase, same expression at line 400), so the rebase did not introduce it. The wrong value reaches EFTOmegaPPPP for designer models that use a Pade Omega (Pure EFT at 007p1:1395; Omega_Lambda_gamma at 007p5:559 if Pade is selected). From there it feeds EFTcdotdot and EFTLambdadotdot. I did not re-run the finder's Cls and stability comparison: the claim that Cls are bit-identical and stability verdicts unchanged is the finder's and is untested by me. Taking it as given, S2 fits (a silently wrong quantity limited to stability and positivity use), not S1.

### F-053 [S2, confirmed] Scaling Cubic Galileon third derivatives wrong: hpprime sign and y2 coefficient, fpprime missing x1pp term, malformed EFTLambdadotdot, typo in EFTGamma2PPP

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/08f_full_models/008p6_Scaling_Cubic.f90:931` (also: fortran/eftcamb/08f_full_models/008p6_Scaling_Cubic.f90:926 (fpprime), fortran/eftcamb/08f_full_models/008p6_Scaling_Cubic.f90:973 (EFTLambdadotdot), fortran/eftcamb/08f_full_models/008p6_Scaling_Cubic.f90:1060,1065 (second-order copies), fortran/eftcamb/08f_full_models/008p6_Scaling_Cubic.f90:1092 (EFTGamma2PPP), fortran/eftcamb/08f_full_models/008p6_Scaling_Cubic.f90:1203 (ComputeHubbleDer copy))
- **Track:** t4b-derivs; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/scg-higher-derivatives-wrong.py [TREE]`
- **Observed:** repro (A0, 5_scg.ini), stored vs numerical derivative of the stored lower derivative, relative error at a = 1e-3, 1e-2, 0.05, 0.2, 0.5, 0.8:
- Hdotdotdot: 5.5e-2, 2.8e-2, 6.8e-3, 2.3e-3, 1.5e-2, 2.1e-3.
- EFTLambdadotdot: 1.7e4, 1.9e5, 9.2e6, 3.9e5, 3.4e4, 1.6e4. At a=0.01 the stored value is 1.204e-9 against a numerical value of 6.27e-15.
- EFTGamma2PPP: 6.7e-2, 1.5e-2, 1.2e-3, 2.3e-3, 1.8e-2, 3.3e-2.
- Controls (Hdot, Hdotdot, EFTLambdadot, Gamma2P, Gamma2PP): <= 8e-5, against a numerical-derivative floor of 4e-5.
Symbolic check (checks/t4b-derivs/scg_formula_sympy.py, code vs exact derivative along the ODE flow): fpprime 51, hpprime 209, x1ppp 10, EFTLambdadotdot 1.6e4, Hdotdotdot 2.3, G2PPP 0.91, while every first and second derivative is <= 1e-13. At y2=0 hpprime has exactly the wrong sign (ratio -1.000000). Patched tree P_scg3: Hdotdotdot 1.2e-8..4.5e-5, EFTLambdadotdot 5.5e-7..1.1e-4, Gamma2PPP 1.3e-7..9.7e-6 (FD floor); spectra unchanged (worst_cl 3e-15). Consumers: compute_positivity_bounds (posbound2 via N4/N5, tachpos via Q1-Q4, i.e. EFT_positivity_bounds / EFT_minkowski_limit) and get_eft_functions output. Same code in B1.
- **Expected:** Hdotdotdot = d Hdotdot/dtau, EFTLambdadotdot = adotoa (d EFTLambdadot/dN - 2 EFTLambdadot), EFTGamma2PPP = d EFTGamma2PP/da, to ~1e-6.
- **Root cause:** Hand derivation errors, all three copies of hpprime (background, second-order and ComputeHubbleDer):
- hpprime has prefactor -1/D instead of +1/D, and (1 + 2A lambda + 2A beta2) instead of (1 - 2A lambda + 2A beta2) in the y2 term.
- fpprime omits (2A lambda - 1)(sqrt6 - 6A x1) x1pp.
- EFTLambdadotdot contains dimensionally inconsistent terms: a bare 3(2A lambda - 1), a stray factor a in 3 sqrt6 a (x1p + 2 x1pp), and hpprime multiplied by x1p instead of x1.
- EFTGamma2PPP has 3*h*2 where 3*h**2 is needed.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** Diff in scratchpad work/t4b-derivs/fix_diffs/scg-higher-derivs.diff (applied by checks/t4b-derivs/patch_scg_derivs.py):
- hpprime = +1/D*(0.5*OmegaRadpprime*(2A lambda - 1) + 3*(-(1-2A lambda)^2 x1p^2 + (2A lambda - 1)(sqrt6 A + x1 - 2A lambda x1) x1pp + (1-2A lambda+2A beta1)(y1p^2+y1 y1pp) + (1-2A lambda+2A beta2)(y2p^2+y2 y2pp))), in all three copies.
- Add -(2A lambda - 1)(sqrt6 - 6A x1) x1pp inside the -3/D bracket of fpprime (both copies).
- EFTLambdadotdot = -2 adotoa^4 ((1+3h) K + K'), where EFTLambdadot = -2 adotoa^3 K and K' = dK/dN, written explicitly in the diff.
- In EFTGamma2PPP use x1p*(2 - 6h + 3h**2 + 3 hprime).
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I ran the repro on A0 and got the reported numbers exactly. Separately, I wrote my own sympy check (fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/checks/verify/scg_derivs_indep.py). It builds the exact SCG flow in N = ln a, with x1, y1, y2, OmegaRad, ln adotoa and ln a as the state. It compares hand transcriptions of the Fortran formulas at 200 random state and parameter points against exact derivatives taken along that flow, so it does not use CAMB or finite differences. Every first and second derivative matches to 1e-13. hpprime, fpprime, x1ppp, EFTLambdadotdot, EFTGamma2PPP and Hdotdotdot are wrong by O(1) to O(100) in relative terms.

All four root causes in the finding are visible in the source and match my own derivation by hand:
- hpprime has prefactor -1/D, and its y2 coefficient is (1+2A lambda+2A beta2) where it should be (1-2A lambda+2A beta2). This appears in all three copies: lines 931 and 1065, and ComputeHubbleDer at about line 1203.
- fpprime is missing the (2A lambda-1)(-sqrt6+6A x1) x1pp term.
- EFTLambdadotdot has a bare 3(2A lambda-1) term and a stray scale factor `a`.
- EFTGamma2PPP has `3*h*2` where `3*h**2` is needed.

The bug is in the EFTCAMB code itself, not in upstream CAMB or the test. B1 (eftcamb-pre-rebase) contains the same code.

Severity: S2. These fields are not used in the perturbation equations, so spectra are unaffected. They are used only by compute_positivity_bounds (N4, N5, Q1, Q4, Q5, Q6, active when EFT_positivity_bounds or EFT_minkowski_limit is set; both default .false.), by the RGR printout and by get_eft_functions output. So the damage is limited to an optional prior and to diagnostic output for one supported model.

I applied the proposed fixes for hpprime, fpprime and EFTGamma2PPP to my transcription, and they now match the exact derivatives to 1e-13. I did not test the EFTLambdadotdot rewrite separately, but its form, -2 adotoa^4((1+3h)K+K') with EFTLambdadot = -2 adotoa^3 K, is the correct derivative under the a^2-weighted convention.

### F-054 [S2, confirmed] Higher-derivative cache fields unset or wrong for all full-mapping models and designer f(R) (Hdotdotdot, EFTLambdadotdot, EFTcdotdot, EFTGamma1PP, EFTOmegaPPPP), used by the positivity/Minkowski priors

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/06p1_abstract_EFTCAMB_full_map.f90:145` (also: fortran/eftcamb/06_abstract_EFTCAMB_model.f90:1124-1150 (consumers N4, N5, Q1-Q6), fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:570-575 (call order), fortran/eftcamb/07f_designer_models/007p3_Designer_fR.f90 (Hdotdotdot, EFTLambdadotdot never set))
- **Track:** t4b-derivs; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/fullmap-hdotdotdot-wrong.py ; /Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/minkowski-tachpos-lambdadotdot-zero.py`
- **Observed:** Derivative scan (A0):
- EFTcdotdot, EFTLambdadotdot, EFTGamma1PP and EFTOmegaPPPP are identically 0 in the cache for every full-mapping test model (quintessence x5, K-mouflage x3, K-mimic x3, ADE, Horndeski TG x3, Horava x7; SCG except EFTLambdadotdot) and for designer f(R) x4. Their numerical derivatives are nonzero (rel err ~1).
- Hdotdotdot = 0 for f(R) and Horava.
- The generic full-mapping Hdotdotdot vs d Hdotdot/dtau (max over a>1e-3, relative to max(|FD|,H^4)): Kmouflage_1 2.5e2, Kmimic_1 4.8e2, quint_cubic 6.3, quint_exponential 0.40, ADE 2.1e-2. A corrected formula agrees to 2.5e-3..6.9e-3, limited by the finite differences (hdddot_formula_check.py). The t0-layout repro fullmap-hdotdotdot-wrong.py, re-run: Kmouflage 2.79e2, quint_exponential 1.91, ADE 3.3e-2, f(R) 1.0.
- Impact: compute_positivity_bounds runs before compute_additional_derivs in EFTStabilityComputation (09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:570-575). The track-6 repro minkowski-tachpos-lambdadotdot-zero.py, re-run: for 5_quint_exponential the Minkowski condition fails at 0/2991 points with the code's values and at 2887/2991 with the finite-difference EFTLambdadotdot, so the verdict flips.
- Not used in perturbation equations or initial conditions.
- **Expected:** Every stored derivative equals the derivative of its base field, or the consumers do not read unset fields.
- **Root cause:** Full-mapping models compute only up to EFTcdot/EFTLambdadot/EFTOmegaPPP. Second derivatives are produced only by compute_additional_derivs, which is called only for the mass-stability check (and is itself broken; filed by another track). The generic EFTCAMBFullModelComputeHubbleDer Hdotdotdot formula reads EFTLambdadotdot = 0 and EFTOmegaPPPP = 0, and contains typos: '3.5 a OmegaP*1.5 a2 OmegaPP' where a + is needed, a dimensionally wrong '(adotoa2 + 3 adotoa)', and a wrong N1 prefactor. Designer f(R) and Horava never assign Hdotdotdot.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** In 06p1 compute_H_derivs, set Hdotdotdot to the exact derivative of the Hdotdot expression: Hdotdotdot = (N2 - 2 Hdotdot D1 - Hdot D2)/D, with
- D = 1+O+aO'/2
- D1 = aH(1.5O'+0.5aO'')
- D2 = a(H^2+Hdot)(1.5O'+0.5aO'') + a^2H^2(2O''+0.5aO''')
- B = 1+O+2aO'+a^2O''
- B1 = aH(3O'+4aO''+a^2O''')
- B2 = a(H^2+Hdot)(3O'+4aO''+a^2O''') + a^2H^2(7O''+6aO'''+a^2O'''')
- N2 = -B2H^2/2 - 2B1 H Hdot - B(Hdot^2 + H Hdotdot) - [X2(gpresm_t) + X2(EFTLambda)]/2
- X2(x) = xdotdot + 4H xdot + (2Hdot+4H^2) x
This formula is coded in checks/t4b-derivs/hdddot_formula_check.py. Provide EFTLambdadotdot and EFTOmegaPPPP (and EFTcdotdot, EFTGamma1PP) from each full-mapping model analytically, or call a corrected compute_additional_derivs before compute_positivity_bounds. Assign Hdotdotdot in designer f(R) and Horava (Horava: see horava-hdot-inconsistent). Overlaps t0-layout (fullmap-hdotdotdot-wrong) and track 6 (minkowski-tachpos-lambdadotdot-zero); this finding adds the full per-model catalogue.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; Both repros reproduce exactly, and an independent check on the A0 reference npz grids (np.gradient in ln a, no CAMB runs) gives the same per-model catalogue. Reading the code confirms the root cause. Outside the stability module 06p2 and the models themselves, EFTLambdadotdot, EFTcdotdot, EFTGamma1PP and EFTOmegaPPPP are assigned only in EFTCAMBFullModelComputeAdditionalDer (06p1:170-196). EFTStabilityComputation (09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:572-577) calls compute_positivity_bounds before compute_additional_derivs, and only calls compute_additional_derivs when EFT_mass_stability is on. So the Minkowski/positivity coefficients N4, Q1, Q5 and Q6 (06_abstract_EFTCAMB_model.f90:1124-1150) always read the zeros that cache initialize() wrote, whatever the flags are. The Hdotdotdot formula at 06p1:145 contains the typos the finding describes. One is '3.5_dl*a*EFTOmegaP*1.5_dl*a2*EFTOmegaPP', which appears twice and where a + is needed. Another is the dimensionally inconsistent '(adotoa2 + 3.0_dl*adotoa)'. The third is N1 = -aH(3O' + 0.5aO'')/2, where the derivative of the denominator is aH(1.5O' + 0.5aO''). On top of these, the formula reads EFTLambdadotdot = 0 and EFTOmegaPPPP = 0. Designer f(R) and Horava never assign Hdotdotdot. The defect is in EFTCAMB code, not upstream CAMB. It does not come from the test: the code's own EFTLambdadotdot is unchanged in the tachpos value used, which is identical with mass stability off and on. Severity S2 stands. The affected consumers are the opt-in priors EFT_positivity_bounds and EFT_minkowski_limit, plus mass stability, all off by default (09_EFTCAMB_main.f90:89-100). They are not used in the perturbation equations, so with these priors switched on the stability verdicts are silently wrong. Small overstatements: for designer f(R), EFTcdotdot and EFTGamma1PP are 0 because their base fields are 0 too, so those two are correct. Horava's EFTOmegaPPPP is likewise correctly 0. For quintessence the Hdotdotdot error comes only from the missing EFTLambdadotdot, because Omega = 0 and the typo terms drop out. A side observation (not part of this finding): with the finite-difference EFTLambdadotdot, exponential quintessence fails the Minkowski condition at 2887/2991 points. That model should be healthy, so after the fix the Minkowski/tachyon formula itself needs its own review.

### F-055 [S2, confirmed] EFT_IC_type 2/3 evaluate a radiation-era super-horizon series for pi at the turn-on time: garbage spectra (up to 1e20) for most models

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:307` (also: fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:314-345 (case 3), fortran/equations.f90:2236-2361 (IC 2/3 adiabatic block))
- **Track:** t5-robust; reported severity S1, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/ic23-pi-radiation-series-at-turn-on.py`
- **Observed:** Repro: 2_PEFT_Omega_const_2 (turn-on 0.01), at a=0.0103: pi(IC1) = -5.2e-5, -4.0e-6, -3.1e-4 vs pi(IC2) = 2.98, -1.9e2, -1.1e4 for k = 0.01, 0.1, 1; IC2 vs IC1: max|dTT/TT| = 7.2e5, max|dP/P| = 9.5e8, sigma8 0.547 -> 2.763. 2_PEFT_gamma3_exp_1 with IC3 aborts (RungeKuttaDP45 error -3). Over 17 models: IC2/IC3 give 1e1-1e20 errors for 13, NaN for 5, RK45 errors for 4. They agree with IC1 only when the turn-on is in the radiation era (5_hdsk_TG_2 at 1e-8: 5.6e-7 vs the delta_phi run). Even at turn-on 1e-6 (Omega_const_2) P(k>1/Mpc) is off by up to x626. No warning or error is issued.
- **Expected:** IC types 2/3 (documented as 'MG-Omega', 'MG-Omega+Gamma') give pi close to the attractor and spectra close to IC1, or are refused where the series is not valid
- **Root cause:** case(2)/(3) set y(w_ix) = h0 (1/4)(1+Omega)/(5+3Omega) k^2 tau^3 (and the Gamma3 version) at the perturbation turn-on tau. These are radiation-era, k tau << 1 expansions, but they are applied at a = 0.01 (matter era, k tau ~ 10-1e4) and grow like k^2 tau^3.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** Apply the series only when the turn-on is in the radiation era and k*tau << 1 (e.g. k*tau < 0.1); otherwise fall back to the type-1 attractor IC, or stop with a clear error if EFT_IC_type /= 1 is used with a late turn-on.
- **Verifier (physics):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; The effect is real. With the default EFTCAMB_turn_on_time = 0.01, EFT_IC_type 2 sets pi from the radiation-era, super-horizon k^2 tau^3 series (09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:305-309) at a time where k*tau >> 1. pi starts 1e5-1e9 times too large, the spectra are garbage, and no warning is printed. IC3 aborts for 2_PEFT_gamma3_exp_1 (a CAMBError raised from calc_transfer, not a hard crash).

Physics lens: the series itself is not wrong. Inside its regime it is correct. When EFT is switched on near the CAMB initial time (turn-on 1e-8), IC2 matches IC1 at the same turn-on to 1e-8 in the Cls. IC types 2/3 are documented as 'assume Omega=const at IC time' and change the adiabatic modes of every species at the CAMB start (equations.f90:2236ff, with Omega taken at the turn-on). So they are only self-consistent when EFT is active from the start. With a late turn-on, the species ICs are also inconsistent, because GR equations evolve the MG modes up to a = 0.01.

So the defect is missing validation (or documentation) of IC type 2/3 combined with a late turn-on, not a wrong formula. Two parts of the finding are overstated:
(a) The 'expected' reference (IC1 at the same 0.01 turn-on) is not the physical truth for these models. For Omega_const_2, IC1 at 0.01 vs IC1 at 1e-8 already differs by 45% in Cl and sigma8 goes 0.547 vs 0.419, because the turn-on time is itself a physics choice.
(b) The claim that P(k>1) is off by up to x626 at turn-on 1e-6 is mostly a generic turn-on artifact. IC1 at 1e-6 is off from the 1e-8 reference by a comparable amount (worst_pk 646). At k=1, IC2 at 1e-6 is off by only 1.9e-3.

Severity: S2 rather than S1. The garbage only appears for a non-default, opt-in IC option used outside its intended regime (late turn-on). It is still serious, because the default turn-on is that regime and the code stays silent. Proposed fix: refuse, or warn and fall back to IC1, when EFT_IC_type /= 1 and the turn-on is not deep in the radiation era (or k*tau_turn_on is not << 1). Also document that IC 2/3 need an early EFTCAMB_turn_on_time.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; The repro behaves as reported. I had to change one path in it: PARAMS points to /Users/marco/Desktop/IW, and the repo now lives at /Users/marco/IW. The independent check used harness.run_model with overrides, on A0 and on the pre-rebase B1. In 09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:306-312 (case 2) and 314-345 (case 3), pi and pi' come from the radiation-era series h0*(1/4)(1+Omega)/(5+3Omega)*k^2*tau^3. The series is evaluated at the perturbation turn-on tau with no check that the turn-on is in the radiation era or that k*tau << 1. At the default turn-on, a=0.01, k*tau is 14-1400, so pi starts 1e5-1e8 times larger than the attractor value and the spectra are garbage. No warning is printed. With an early turn-on (1e-8) the same IC2 run agrees with IC1, so the series formula is correct and only its use outside its range is wrong, as the finding says. The defect is in EFTCAMB code, not upstream CAMB and not the test: B1 gives the same pi values at turn-on (2.974, -209.5, 1.645e4) and the same sigma8 blow-up (0.5465 -> 2.763), so it predates the rebase. I lowered severity to S2: EFT_IC_type 2/3 is a non-default option, and the default IC1 works. The failure is a parameter region (IC2/3 with a turn-on outside the radiation era) where the output is silently far off; this is not wrong physics for a supported model at default settings. Separate failure the stated root cause does not explain: 2_PEFT_gamma3_const_2 with IC3 gives NaN Cls (13396 NaN entries, sigma8=NaN) even at turn-on 1e-8, where the series should be valid. The IC3 code (case 3 in 09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90 or the IC3 adiabatic block in equations.f90) probably has its own problem, worth a separate look.

### F-056 [S2, confirmed] EFT_IC_type 2/3 read Gamma1-3 and background quantities from an uninitialised or stale EV%eft_cache

- **Location:** `fortran/equations.f90:2243` (also: fortran/equations.f90:2245-2248)
- **Track:** t5-robust; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/ic23-stale-cache-gammas.py`
- **Observed:** Repro (A0_icdbg = A0 plus a print; EFT_IC_type=3, 2_PEFT_gamma3_exp_1, 1 thread). Release path: first k mode Gamma1=4.9e-324, Gamma2=-7.6e176 (a previous run printed -1.9e263), Gamma3=4.9e-324; second mode Gamma3=0.6487 (left over from the previous mode at a~1). Expected Gamma3(a_eval=0.01) = 0.3709, as printed by the RGR feedback. With the candidate fix (full cache fill at a_eval): Gamma3=0.3709 for every mode.
- **Expected:** Omega0, Gamma10..Gamma30 evaluated at a_eval for every k mode, independent of thread and mode order
- **Root cause:** subroutine initial calls only compute_background_EFT_functions(a_eval, ...) and then reads EV%eft_cache%EFTGamma1V/2V/3V. Those are set only by compute_secondorder_EFT_functions, which is never called here. EV is OpenMP-private and reused between modes and is not initialised, so the first mode on a thread reads uninitialised memory and later modes read the previous mode's values. Pure-EFT background functions also read stale adotoa and densities from the cache.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** Before line 2245: call EV%eft_cache%initialize(); fill a, densities and adotoa (compute_adotoa, compute_H_derivs), then call compute_background_EFT_functions and compute_secondorder_EFT_functions at a_eval (the same sequence as EFTCAMBReturnToGR_functions). Tested in work/t5-robust/A0_icdbg with EFT_T5_ICFIX=1.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; Reproduced and the attribution holds. The defect is in EFTCAMB's own code: the IC-type 2/3 branch of `subroutine initial` in fortran/equations.f90. It is not upstream CAMB and not a test artefact.

- Line 2243 calls only `compute_background_EFT_functions(a_eval, ...)`. Lines 2245-2248 then read `EFTGamma1V/2V/3V`, which only `compute_secondorder_EFT_functions` sets.
- `EV%eft_cache` is never initialised at that point. The only `initialize()` in equations.f90 near that path (line 1956) is in a different routine and only runs when `DebugEFTCAMB` is on.
- The same code is in the pre-rebase branch (eftcamb-pre-rebase, equations.f90:2083-2088). So this is a long-standing EFTCAMB bug, not something the rebase introduced.

**The repro.** Its hardcoded paths still point to /Users/marco/Desktop/IW, but the repo now lives in /Users/marco/IW, so it does not run unmodified. I ran it through a wrapper that only fixes that path and holds a CPU slot. It showed uninitialised Gammas on the first k mode and the previous mode's value (0.6487) on the second. With the fix, Gamma3 = 0.3709.

**My independent check (unmodified frozen A0, end-to-end outputs).** With `EFT_IC_type=3` and the default turn-on a=0.01, every run aborts in `calc_transfer` with RungeKuttaDP45 error -3. It aborts with the candidate fix too, so that abort is a separate IC3 problem. With an early turn-on (`EFTCAMB_turn_on_time=1e-8`), IC3 runs finish with status ok but every `cl_total` entry is NaN, and so is sigma8, on 1 or 2 threads. The same models with IC type 1 are finite.

**With the candidate fix** (t5-robust/A0_icdbg, `EFT_T5_ICFIX=1`), the NaNs disappear and results match between 1 and 2 threads. sigma8 matches the IC1 runs to all printed digits. With `ICFIX=0` that tree gives the same all-NaN result as frozen A0.

**Two caveats.**
- I did not see thread-dependent finite outputs, because the unfixed outputs are all NaN regardless.
- The fix's change is far above 1e-5 (NaN to finite), so "safe-local" is right in spirit (an uninitialised read) but the output change is large.

**Severity.** S2 is right, with a case for S1. It only affects the non-default IC options; type 3 uses the Gammas, while type 2 only uses Omega0. But every IC3 run with a valid ini silently returns NaN spectra.

### F-057 [S2, confirmed] EFT_IC_type=3 returns all-NaN spectra without error when Omega = Gamma3 = 0 at the turn-on

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:325` (also: fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:336, fortran/equations.f90:2279-2354 (case 3 adiabatic block))
- **Track:** t5-robust; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/ic3-nan-when-omega-gamma3-zero.py`
- **Observed:** Repro: 2_PEFT_gamma1_const_2 IC1 gives sigma8=0.64456 and finite C_l; IC3 gives nan_frac=1.0, sigma8=NaN, no CAMBError. The variation runs show the same NaN with IC3 for 2_PEFT_wDE_wCDM_1, 4_Des_mc5e_CPL_1 and 5_quint_exponential, i.e. every minimally coupled model.
- **Expected:** Finite spectra (the IC reduces to the GR case) or an explicit error
- **Root cause:** The case-3 expressions divide by (3 Gamma30 + 5 Omega0 + 3 Gamma30 Omega0 + 3 Omega0^2) and by 8(3 Gamma30 (1+Omega0) + Omega0(5+3 Omega0)), which vanish for Omega0 = Gamma30 = 0. The adiabatic IC of every mode becomes 0/0, and nothing checks for NaN.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** If the denominator is 0 (or |.| < 1e-12), use the case-2 or GR expressions. Also check IsNaN(y) after the EFT IC and set global_error_flag.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; The defect is real and is in EFTCAMB code. EFT_IC_type=3 on a model with Omega = Gamma3 = 0 at the turn-on gives all-NaN C_l and sigma8, and no CAMBError or error flag is raised. Both case-3 blocks divide by (3G3 + 5Om + 3G3*Om + 3Om^2) and by (3G3 + 8Om + 6G3*Om + 6Om^2), which are 0 there, so the result is 0/0:
- fortran/equations.f90:2279ff, in initv for clxg, qr, pir, aj3r and eta. Through clxg this poisons every species, so it is the main source.
- fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:318-337, the pi field and its velocity.
The singularity is removable: as Om, G3 -> 0 the clxg ratio goes to 1/3, the GR value, so a fallback to the case-2 or GR expressions is the right fix. The proposed fix is sound and safe-local. It does not change any run that is finite today.
Severity stays S2. EFT_IC_type is a non-default option (default 1), and only this combination is affected. The option already exists in the pre-rebase branch, so the defect is not new to the rebase.
The repro script as given cannot run on this machine. Its PARAMS path is hard-coded to /Users/marco/Desktop/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB, which does not exist (the repo is at /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB), so both runs fail with 'ERROR STOP' in read_ini. The script's path should be fixed. My independent harness check (A0 tree, overrides EFT_IC_type) reproduced the claim. Separately, 2_PEFT_Omega_const_1 with IC3 (Omega=1) fails with an explicit RungeKuttaDP45 error -3. That is not this finding: it raises an error and is not silent.

### F-058 [S2, confirmed] Return-to-GR test compares dimensionful c, Lambda (Mpc^-2) with a dimensionless threshold; threshold 1e-7 silently drops all DE perturbations

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_RGR.f90:316` (also: fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_RGR.f90:153-170 (RGR_time = 1.1), fortran/results.f90:602-604)
- **Track:** t5-robust; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/rgr-threshold-dimensionful-drops-de-perturbations.py`
- **Observed:** Repro (A0). 4_Des_mc5e_CPL_1: max|EFTc/a^2| = 5.8e-9 Mpc^-2 (H0^2 = 5.4e-8). Threshold 1e-8: RGR time 0.01, sigma8 0.63241. Threshold 1e-7: RGR time 1.1, so perturbations are never switched on; sigma8 0.63642 (+6.3e-3), TT ell=2 -6.4e-2, TT ell>=30 up to 2.2e-3, C_L^pp 4.3e-3. 5_quint_exponential: RGR 1e-8 -> 1.1, sigma8 +7.0e-3, TT ell=2 -7.7e-2, C_L^pp 3.8e-3. Noise floor: sigma8 7e-5, TT30 7e-4. The only feedback is the RGR line, followed by the misleading 'coincide' warning.
- **Expected:** A 10x change of the GR threshold has << 1e-3 effect on a model with w != -1 today; perturbations are not dropped while the background is modified
- **Root cause:** eft_functions(5..8) = |EFTc/a^2|, |EFTLambda/a^2 + grhov|, |EFTcdot/a^2|, |EFTLambdadot/a^2| carry units of H^2 and H^3 (Mpc^-2, Mpc^-3), about H0^2 ~ 5e-8 today, but are compared with the dimensionless EFTCAMB_GR_threshold (default 1e-8). If nothing exceeds it, RGR_time = 1.1 and results.f90:604 sets EFTCAMB_pert_turn_on = 1.1, so DE perturbations are absent at all times while the w(a) background is kept.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** Normalise the dimensionful entries, e.g. eft_functions(5) = |EFTc/a^2|/(3 adotoa^2/a^2), (6) = |EFTLambda/a^2 + grhov|/(3 adotoa^2/a^2), (7,8) divided by (adotoa/a)^3. When RGR_time > 1 but the background is not LCDM, turn perturbations on at the requested turn-on and print a warning.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I ran the repro unchanged and got the reported numbers exactly. A separate check with different settings shows the whole effect comes from the perturbations being switched off. Setting threshold 1e-7 with EFTCAMB_skip_RGR=T gives results bit-identical to threshold 1e-8. Setting threshold 1e-8 with EFTCAMB_turn_on_time=0.9999 reproduces the 1e-7 result (sigma8 diff 1.7e-10). At 09_EFTCAMB_RGR.f90:316-319, eft_functions(5..8) are raw |EFTc/a^2|, |EFTLambda/a^2+grhov|, |EFTcdot/a^2| and |EFTLambdadot/a^2|. Those carry units of Mpc^-2 and Mpc^-3, about H0^2 = 5.45e-8, and are compared with the dimensionless EFTCAMB_GR_threshold. Measured in dimensionless terms the model is far from GR: max |c/a^2|/(3H^2) = 3.5% and |Lambda/a^2|/(3H^2) up to 0.63. Yet the raw maximum, 5.8e-9, is below 1e-7, so RGR_time comes out as 1.1. results.f90:602-604 then sets the turn-on to 1.1, and no DE perturbations are evolved at any time. The defect is in EFTCAMB code, not upstream CAMB and not the test. The same logic is in pre-rebase EFTCAMB (eftcamb-pre-rebase 09_EFTCAMB_RGR.f90:157), so the bug is long-standing, not introduced by the rebase. Severity stays S2. The default threshold 1e-8 did not trigger the bug in my tests, including w0=-0.97, wa=0: there the default run is bit-identical to skip_RGR because another entry (f6) still passes the threshold. The failure needs a larger threshold, or models whose deviations are small in raw Mpc units.

### F-059 [S2, confirmed] Designer f(R) matter power at k >= 1/Mpc changes by 2-11% with EFTCAMB_GR_threshold (RGR-delayed turn-on)

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_RGR.f90:164` (also: fortran/results.f90:602-604)
- **Track:** t5-robust; reported severity S2, confidence medium
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/fr-rgr-threshold-high-k-pk.py`
- **Observed:** Repro (A0, 4_Des_F_R_LCDM_2, B0=1e-4). Default threshold 1e-8: turn-on a=0.0931, sigma8 0.683869. Threshold 1e-10: turn-on 0.0239, sigma8 0.683710; P/P_default-1 = -2.0e-4 (k=0.1), -1.74e-3 (0.3), -1.82e-2 (1), -9.06e-2 (3.1), -1.08e-1 (10); C_L^pp 2.5e-3; TT 5.6e-6. Threshold 1e-12 (turn-on 0.01): -1.13e-1 at k=10. Threshold x10 (1e-7): turn-on 0.183, P(k) 0.21, sigma8 3.6e-3. AB2 noise for P(k): 1.8e-3.
- **Expected:** Results converged with respect to the GR threshold (changes << 1e-3)
- **Root cause:** The RGR criterion uses the size of the EFT functions (Omega ~ 1e-8 here), but for f(R) the modification below the Compton scale is O(1) (mu -> 4/3) however small Omega is. Delaying the perturbation turn-on to the threshold crossing removes real growth at k >= 1/Mpc. Same mechanism as t3's t3-fr-rgr-switch-gr-approach (B0 -> 0), shown here for the default test model.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** Do not delay the turn-on through RGR for models with scale-dependent modifications (designer f(R)), or base the criterion on a dimensionless measure of the perturbation modification (e.g. |mu-1| at kmax, or the pi mass term m^2/k^2), and document the remaining sensitivity.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; The repro gives exactly the reported numbers on A0. The repro script could not run as committed: the repo moved from ~/Desktop/IW to ~/IW, so its hardcoded PARAMS path fails with ERROR STOP. I ran it unchanged except for PARAMS, through the wrapper fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/checks/verify/frthr_repro_pathfix.py, which also holds a harness slot.

Accuracy noise does not explain the effect. At accuracy_boost=2 the 1e-8 vs 1e-10 difference is the same: -0.110 at k=10 and -0.088 at k=3. The pre-rebase build B1 gives the same numbers, -0.1084 at k=10, so this is old EFTCAMB behaviour, not a rebase regression.

The defect is in EFTCAMB code. In 09_EFTCAMB_RGR.f90:164 (EFTCAMBReturnToGR) the turn-on is chosen when any |EFT function| exceeds 1e-8, and results.f90:602-604 applies that time as EFTCAMB_pert_turn_on. At the chosen a=0.093 the quasi-static (QS) f(R) mu-1 is already 0.29 at k=10/Mpc and 0.125 at k=3. Lowering the threshold or skipping RGR converges smoothly: 1e-10, 1e-12 and skip_RGR agree to under 1e-3, and skip_RGR is bit-equal to 1e-12. So the default 1e-8 result is the unconverged one.

The stated mechanism has the wrong sign. The finding says the delay "removes real growth". The default run actually has more power than the converged run, by about +11% at k=10. An independent QS growth integration (mu=1 before a_on, f(R) QS mu after; frthr_qs.py) agrees with converged EFTCAMB to 0.5%. That integration predicts that delaying the turn-on to a=0.093 should lower P at k=10 (+0.856 vs +1.196). EFTCAMB at the delayed turn-on gives +1.48 instead.

So the error comes from switching on discontinuously when mu-1 is already O(1) at high k. In the default run the growth rate f=-kz/(aH delta_c) jumps from 0.948 to 1.336 at k=10 at a=0.0948, while the converged run moves smoothly through 1.05. It is not lost growth.

The file:line and the core diagnosis (criterion based on Omega size, with mu-1 at kmax already O(1)) are right. The proposed fix (criterion on |mu-1| at kmax or on m^2/k^2) addresses this either way. S2 fits: silently poor accuracy of linear P(k) at k>=1/Mpc at default settings. Errors are about 1.8% at k=1, 9% at k=3 and 11% at k=10, with C_L^phiphi 2.5e-3. CMB TT is only 5.6e-6 and sigma8 2.3e-4.

### F-060 [S2, confirmed] EFTCAMB_evolve_metric_h=T starts from the GR h' = 2kz, violating the Horndeski 00 constraint: P(k) -13% at k=10/Mpc

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:290` (also: fortran/equations.f90:3144-3163 (constraint vs evolved h'))
- **Track:** t5-robust; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/metric-h-ic-violates-constraint.py`
- **Observed:** Repro (A0, 5_hdsk_TG_1). P(k; metric_h=T)/P(k; F)-1 at k=0.1,1,5,10,20/Mpc: turn-on 1e-8 (test ini): -9.4e-5, -2.3e-3, -3.9e-2, -1.32e-1, -4.2e-1; turn-on 1e-7: -4.6e-4, -2.3e-2, -3.4e-1, -8.8e-1, +5.6e-1. TT ell=4 -9.1e-3, TT ell>=30 1.2e-3. The default (constraint) path is stable: turn-on 1e-7 vs 1e-8 differs by 3.8e-4 at k=5. Same numbers in B1. With h'_ini from the constraint (A0_icsw, EFT_T5_HIC=1): +8.8e-6, -2.8e-8, -9.2e-5, -3.7e-4, -1.4e-3, but the TT ell=4 -9.1e-3 difference remains.
- **Expected:** Evolving h with its second-order (ii) equation reproduces the constraint solution (< 1e-4). The Horndeski module recommends this flag when alphaB crosses 2.
- **Root cause:** The IC sets y(w_ix+2) = 2 k z with the GR z = (0.5 dgrho/k + etak)/adotoa, while the evolution uses the Horndeski constraint h' = (4 k etak + 2 dgrho/Meff2)/((2-alphaB) adotoa) + (delta_phi terms). These differ whenever Meff2 != 1 or alphaB != 0 at the turn-on (Meff2 = 0.9 for TG at 1e-8). The constraint violation then propagates to late times. A separate late-time discrepancy (low-ell ISW, -0.9%) remains and was not isolated.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** -        if (CP%EFTCAMB%EFTCAMB_evolve_metric_h) y(EV%w_ix + 2) = 2._dl*k*z
+        if (CP%EFTCAMB%EFTCAMB_evolve_metric_h) y(EV%w_ix + 2) = (4._dl*k*etak + 2._dl*dgrho/EV%eft_cache%Meff2)/ &
+            (2._dl + 2._dl*EV%eft_cache%alphaB)/adotoa   ! delta_phi = 0 at the IC; alphaB_BS = -2 alphaB
(tested: high-k error 13% -> 4e-4); then investigate the remaining low-ell TT difference.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I ran the repro as given (frozen A0, 2.5 min) and got the reported numbers exactly. I then checked the claim a second way, through the per-k evolution cache (get_scale_evolution). On a grid from a = 1e-8 to 1, I compared the h' the code actually integrates (2 k z) with the Horndeski 00 constraint rebuilt from cached etak, dgrho, Meff2, alphaB, adotoa, s00/s00k/s00p and delta_phi.
- With evolve_metric_h = F the residual is 0 to 1e-16 at all k and a, so my constraint formula matches equations.f90:3147.
- With evolve_metric_h = T the residual just after turn-on is -0.10 to -0.14. This equals the GR-vs-constraint mismatch (2kz_GR/h'_c - 1 = -0.09998, since Meff2 = 0.9000 and alphaB = 6.8e-8 at turn-on). It grows to about -1.5 by a ~ 1e-7 to 1e-6, then decays: k=10 gives 7.9e-4 at a = 1e-4; k=0.01 still gives -3.3e-3 at a = 1.
- With the constraint-based IC (t5 tree A0_icsw, EFT_T5_HIC=1) the turn-on residual drops to -1.4e-4 and the a = 1 residual to 1e-7 to 5e-7.
So the defect is the GR initial value y(w_ix+2) = 2kz at 09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:290, in EFTCAMB code; it is neither upstream CAMB nor a test artefact. The proposed fix's denominator (2 + 2 alphaB_EFT) equals (2 - alphaB_BS) as used in equations.f90:2960 and 3147.

One caveat for the report: the leftover low-ell TT difference does not come from this IC. With the fixed IC it is identical to three digits (-9.06e-3 at ell=4) across all IC and turn-on variants, and it is in cl_unlensed_scalar (-1.1% to -1.2% at ell=2-7). Meanwhile cl_lens_potential differs by only 2e-5 and the late-time constraint residual is 1e-7. That points to a separate late-time source/ISW-path discrepancy under evolve_metric_h = T, which deserves its own finding.

S2 stands: the flag is non-default (off in all test inis) but is the remedy the Horndeski module recommends. I did not check the claim that B1 behaves the same.

### F-061 [S2, confirmed] Third stability sampling pass never updates y (duplicate of F-012): a ghost window just below a=1 is accepted

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:127` (also: F-012 (t2d-static, repro/stability-third-pass-stale-y.py))
- **Track:** t6-stability; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/stability-sampler-stale-y.py [tree]`
- **Observed:** Pure EFT, LCDM background, gamma1 = -1e-3 + 2.5e4 (a-0.9995)^2. Per-point EFTTestStability is unstable at 400/1001 points in [0.999301, 0.9997], with EFT_kinetic(0.9995) = -3.92e-9. Sampler points inside the window: loop 1: 0, loop 2: 0, loop 3 as coded: 0 (it samples only a = 1e-10), loop 3 intended: 36. Verdict: stable (EFTCAMB_Stability_Check and get_background).
- **Expected:** unstable
- **Root cause:** Loop 3 computes Atest = aend + (astart-aend)*10**y with y left at 0 from loop 2, so Atest = astart 1000 times.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** Add y = LogSamplingScale + real(ind-1)*(0-LogSamplingScale)/real(indMax-1) before line 127 (work/t6-stability/fix_fixy.diff). Fixed tree (fixy): verdict unstable.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; The defect is real and sits in the code under audit, at fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:127. The third loop ("log sampling close to aend") never assigns y, so it reuses y from the last iteration of loop 2. That value is exactly 0: y = -10 + 999*10/999 = 0.0. So Atest = aend + (astart-aend)*1 = astart, and loop 3 tests a = astart (1e-10) 1000 times instead of points clustered near a=1. The same bug is already in the pre-rebase B1 source (lines 124-125), so it was inherited from the original EFTCAMB, not introduced by the rebase. It duplicates F-012. I kept S2 because the miss needs an instability window narrower than the linear grid spacing (about 1e-3) that also avoids a=1, which is an edge case. The fix (compute y inside loop 3) is physics-changing: some models that pass today would be flagged unstable. One problem with the repro itself: it hard-codes /Users/marco/Desktop/IW/... for base_params.ini, but the repo is now at /Users/marco/IW/..., so it fails as written. It needs a path update before it can be filed.

### F-062 [S2, confirmed] EFT_minkowski_limit (and positivity bounds) are evaluated before compute_additional_derivs: EFTLambdadotdot, Gamma2PP/PPP, Gamma3PP.. are 0 for models that do not set them analytically

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:570` (also: fortran/eftcamb/06_abstract_EFTCAMB_model.f90:1086-1180 (EFTCAMBModelPositivityBounds), camb/camb.py:274)
- **Track:** t6-stability; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/minkowski-tachpos-lambdadotdot-zero.py [tree]`
- **Observed:** A0, EFT_minkowski_limit=T, check interval [1e-3,1]. 5_quint_exponential: EFTLambdadotdot used = 0.000; the Minkowski condition fails at 0/2991 points with the code coefficients vs 2887/2991 with the finite-difference EFTLambdadotdot. At a=0.5, tachpos = -5.777e-8 (code) vs +5.137e-8 (FD); verdict stable. 5_quint_inverse_power: 0/2991 vs 2661/2991. With EFT_mass_stability=T the derivatives are filled only after tachpos is built, so they do not help. On the candidate fix tree (fixaddl): 2885/2991 vs 2887/2991, tachpos 4.890e-8 vs 5.137e-8, verdict unstable. camb.set_params guards only EFT_positivity_bounds, not EFT_minkowski_limit.
- **Expected:** The Minkowski/positivity coefficients use the model's actual higher derivatives, i.e. the fail counts agree with the FD reconstruction.
- **Root cause:** EFTStabilityComputation calls compute_positivity_bounds (l.570) before compute_additional_derivs (l.573, and only when mass stability is on). EFTTestStability initialises the cache first, so EFTLambdadotdot etc. are exactly 0 for quintessence 5e, K-mouflage, ADE, Horava, Horndeski, ExtGal, OL alpha, designer f(R) (where_set.py). compute_additional_derivs itself is broken (F-009). Whether the corrected verdict for quintessence is physically right depends on the tachpos formula (physics workflow).
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** Call compute_additional_derivs before compute_positivity_bounds whenever EFT_mass_stability (a > turn-on), EFT_positivity_bounds or EFT_minkowski_limit is on, with the F-009 helper fix (work/t6-stability/fix_fixaddl.diff, tested above). Extend the camb.py guard to EFT_minkowski_limit.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; The finding holds. The repro gives the claimed numbers, and an independent check from the cached EFT functions agrees. In fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90, line 570 calls compute_positivity_bounds before line 573 calls compute_additional_derivs, and line 573 only runs when EFT_mass_stability is on. EFTCAMBModelPositivityBounds (06_abstract_EFTCAMB_model.f90, ~l.1144: Q1 = (-H*Lambdadot + Lambdadotdot)*a^2, tachpos = -tach_pos_num/D1) therefore always uses EFTLambdadotdot = 0 for models that do not set it analytically. Quintessence is one of them.

The dropped term is not small. The finite-difference EFTLambdadotdot has median |EFTLambdadotdot|/|H*EFTLambdadot| of about 2.5, so the sign of tachpos and the EFT_minkowski_limit verdict flip.

This is a defect of the EFTCAMB code, not of upstream CAMB. It is also not a test artefact: the finite-difference convention used to rebuild the second derivative (Xdot = dX/dtau - 2HX) reproduces the cached EFTLambdadot from EFTLambda to 7e-4 to 3e-3. The same ordering is already in the pre-rebase tree B1 (09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:536/539), so the rebase did not introduce it.

The docs say EFT_minkowski_limit "works up to Horndeski", and quintessence is within that range. camb.py:274 guards only EFT_positivity_bounds, so nothing stops the bad verdict. It stays S2 because it needs an opt-in flag that is off by default and does not touch spectra.

One caveat about the repro script: its paths still point to /Users/marco/Desktop/IW/..., which no longer exists, so as given it stops with "include file not found". I ran a copy with only the path fixed. Whether the corrected Minkowski verdict is physically right also depends on the tachpos formula, which I did not check.

### F-063 [S2, confirmed] EFT_mass_stability verdict is unusable: rejects canonical quintessence (|mu|/H^2 ~ 1e9); shipped inis that enable it pass only because the second derivatives are zeroed

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/06_abstract_EFTCAMB_model.f90:849` (also: fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:372-388,572-575, F-009 (t2d-static), work/t6-stability/verdict_A0_mass.json, verdict_fixaddl_mass.json, verdict_fixaddl_default.json)
- **Track:** t6-stability; reported severity S2, confidence medium
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/mass-stability-verdict-unreliable.py [tree]`
- **Observed:** A0 with EFT_mass_stability=T forced on all 73 test models: 51/73 rejected, including all 5 canonical 5_quint_*, the 8 designer mc5e and the 4 designer f(R). On the F-009-fixed tree (fixaddl): 62/73. 5_quint_exponential: min mu/H^2 = -1.02e9 (A0), -5.86e8 (fixaddl), against a decoupling-limit m_pi^2/H^2 in [-0.055, 0.74]. 15 shipped inis enable the check. For example 3_RPH_MB_const_1 at a=0.5 has EFTGamma1PP = -8.508 (model), -8.507 (FD) but 0 is used, giving min mu/H^2 = -1.13 (accepted). With correct derivatives (fixaddl) 8 shipped inis are rejected at default settings: 2_PEFT_Omega_const_1/2, 2_PEFT_Omega_lin_1/2, 3_RPH_KT_OmegaDE_1, 3_RPH_MB_const_1 (-1.45e4), 3_RPH_MK_const_1 (-8.8e3), 3_RPH_MK_power_1. 2_PEFT_gamma5_const_1 has NaN mu at 85/201 points, so it is rejected through the NaN check.
- **Expected:** A canonical scalar (the Goldstone pi has an O(H^2) mass) is accepted with rate 10, and the verdict does not hinge on zeroed derivatives.
- **Root cause:** (a) F-009: compute_additional_derivs overwrites the models' analytic second derivatives with 0 or wrong values. (b) The mu1/mu2 expression divides by (3F2^2+F1F3)^2 (3F2^2+2F1F3) F2^4 F3^2. For Omega = gamma_i = 0, 3F2^2+F1F3 = 4 EFTc/a^2 exactly, so mu scales as EFTc^-2 as c -> 0 (near-LCDM quintessence) unless the numerator cancels to the same order, which with the supplied inputs it does not. There is no guard against vanishing F2 or F3.
- **Proposed fix (unknown):** Keep the models' analytic second derivatives (do not overwrite them). Re-derive and verify the EFT_mu1/2 expression and its normalisation against the reference (with a canonical-quintessence unit test: |mu|/H^2 = O(1)). Guard the 0/0 cases. Until then, document the check as experimental and do not enable it in shipped inis. Do not apply the F-009 fix alone: it turns 8 shipped inis unstable.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; The effect is real and comes from the code under audit. The stated root cause is only partly right.

Part (a), the F-009 overwrite, is confirmed. For shipped inis that enable the check, the mass check sees EFTGamma1PP/EFTGamma2PP = 0 instead of the model's analytic values, which match a finite difference. That is why they pass.

Part (b), the claim that mu scales as c^-2 because the numerator does not cancel, is wrong as stated. I evaluated the code's own mu1/mu2 expression, auto-translated from the source at 06_abstract_EFTCAMB_model.f90:768-899, at 60 digits. It matches the Fortran to all printed digits, so round-off is ruled out. Scaling EFTc, EFTcdot and EFTcdotdot by s = 10 down to 0.01 at a=0.5 leaves mu1/H^2 at about -5e3: the numerator does cancel to O(c^2).

The quintessence verdict actually has two separate causes:
(1) The 1e8-1e11 values at early times (the -1.02e9 minimum is at a=0.0105, where c/H^2 = 2.6e-12) come from rho_d = (grhob_t+grhoc_t)/a^2 at line 768. It counts only baryons and CDM, while the background also holds massive neutrinos (omnuh2 = 0.01 in base_params, so (H^2-Hdot-c)/(a^2 rho_d/2) = 1.075) and radiation. That background mismatch is then divided by powers of the tiny c. With Hdot and Hdotdot replaced by values consistent with dust only, the early-time mu1/H^2 drops to about -1e3.
(2) Even with fully consistent inputs and c not small (a=1, c/H^2 = 0.17), the formula gives mu1/H^2 = -3.09e3, against a decoupling-limit pi mass of O(0.1-1). So the mu expression itself, or its normalisation, rejects canonical quintessence at rate 10 (threshold -100) for any background.

The verdict is robust to how mu/H^2 is normalised (a^2 or not). Severity S2: the check is optional and off by default, but 14 shipped inis enable it (the finding says 15).

The proposed fix direction is right (keep the analytic derivatives, re-derive and unit-test mu, and do not apply the F-009 fix alone). It should also say that rho_d must include all matter species, or the formula must be re-derived for a general background.

### F-064 [S2, confirmed] Catastrophic cancellation in EFT_kinetic at a < 7e-6 makes a healthy RPH model a spurious ghost at the default EFTCAMB_stability_time

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/06_abstract_EFTCAMB_model.f90:690` (also: fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:319)
- **Track:** t6-stability; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/rph-kinetic-early-roundoff-ghost.py [tree]`
- **Observed:** 3_RPH_MB_OmegaDE_1 (analytic D = alpha_K + 6 alpha_B^2 = 6 Omega_DE^2 > 0): verdict is stable with the ini's EFTCAMB_stability_time = 0.1 and unstable with the code default 1e-10. EFT_kinetic < 0 at 456/2001 log points in [1.0e-10, 6.7e-6]. At a = 3.2e-7: code 2.66e-34 vs analytic 3.42e-41, while the cancelling term 36 M^2 EFTc = 6.6e-18. At a = 1e-3: code 1.96e-20 vs 7.85e-21. The EFT_kinetic bracket fix does not remove it (fixkin, a = 3.2e-8: code 1.3e-35 vs analytic 3.4e-47).
- **Expected:** stable for any stability_time
- **Root cause:** RPH builds gamma1 from alpha_K and c (4 gamma1 H0^2 a^2 = alpha_K M H^2 - 2c), so EFT_kinetic = 9M(4cM + 8 gamma1 M a^2 H0^2 + ...) cancels 4cM against -4cM. At early times |c| exceeds the true kinetic term by more than 1e16, so the sign is roundoff, and EFTTestStability tests EFT_kinetic < 0 with no tolerance. The test ini hides this with stability_time = 0.1.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** Evaluate the no-ghost condition in cancellation-free form. For alpha-basis designers (RPH, OL alpha, ShiftSym) use EFT_kinetic = 18 Meff2^3 adotoa^2 (alphaK + 6 alphaB^2). Generally, treat |EFT_kinetic| <= eps*(sum of |terms|) as zero (a relative tolerance; this is a natural meaning for the unused EFTCAMB_stability_threshold).
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I reproduced the effect, and found the same false ghost in two independent ways. The cause is as stated. RPH sets gamma1 = (alphaK*M*H^2 - 2c)/(4 h0^2 a^2) (007p2_RPH.f90:1749), so the 4cM and 8*gamma1*M*a^2*h0^2 terms of EFT_kinetic (06_abstract_EFTCAMB_model.f90:690) cancel exactly. Here alphaK = 0, so in exact arithmetic the leftover is 0. In floating point it is +-eps*|4cM| (about 1e-37 at a = 1e-9). That is far bigger than the true kinetic term, 18 M^3 H^2 * 6 alphaB^2 (about 3e-48). EFTTestStability (09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:319) then checks EFT_kinetic < 0 with no tolerance. The defect is in EFTCAMB, not upstream CAMB, and was not introduced by the rebase: B1 (the pre-rebase EFTCAMB) behaves the same way. S2 fits. A healthy model is wrongly rejected only when the stability scan starts below a ~ 7e-6. The default EFTCAMB_stability_time of 1e-10 does start there, but the test ini hides it. Every rejection falls before EFTCAMB_turn_on_time, i.e. before EFT perturbations are switched on.

One caveat, about the finding's wording rather than the defect. At late times the code value is exactly 2.5 times the analytic one (at a = 1e-3, a = 0.1 and a = 1). Cancellation does not cause that. It comes from a separate parenthesis bug on the same line, which the finding calls the 'bracket fix' (fixkin). The 6*adotoa*Gamma2*OmegaP term sits inside the h0_Mpc*(...) group, so it picks up an extra factor h0 and is effectively dropped. For this model that turns 12 alphaB^2 into 30 alphaB^2. So the finding's 'a = 1e-3: code 1.96e-20 vs 7.85e-21' example reflects the bracket bug, not roundoff. The roundoff claim holds without it: with the bracket corrected (my Python evaluation from the cache fields), the cancelling pair still comes out as roundoff, either a non-zero residue or an exact 0 that hides the true positive part. One example is a = 1e-9: -1.9e-37 against a true value of 0, while the real remaining positive part is 3.8e-49.

Path note: the repro script as given fails, because it hard-codes /Users/marco/Desktop/IW/... and the repo is at /Users/marco/IW/.... I ran a copy with that one path changed.

### F-065 [S2, confirmed] Stability verdict depends on uninitialised memory: TimestepCacheInit does not reset EFTcdotdot/EFTGamma1PP but is_nan checks them

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/03_abstract_EFTCAMB_cache.f90:402` (also: fortran/eftcamb/03_abstract_EFTCAMB_cache.f90:631,637, fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:71,207)
- **Track:** t6-stability; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/stability-cache-cdotdot-uninitialised.py`
- **Observed:** A0, 4_Des_F_R_LCDM_1, public EFTTestStability(a=0.5): cache pre-filled with zeros gives stable; pre-filled with NaN gives UNSTABLE ('EFTcdotdot is NaN'). Build with -finit-real=nan -finit-derived (work/t6-stability/nanini), stability forced on all 73 models: 27/73 rejected (every full-mapping model, designer f(R), Horava) vs 3/73 on A0. 5_quint_exponential, 4_Des_F_R_LCDM_1 and 5_Kmouflage_1 are stable on A0 and unstable on nanini. With the init fix and the same flags (nanfix): 3/73, identical to A0.
- **Expected:** The verdict does not depend on prior memory contents.
- **Root cause:** EFTCAMBTimestepCacheInit (l.402-586) resets every field except EFTcdotdot and EFTGamma1PP. These are only set by compute_additional_derivs (mass stability, a > turn-on), yet EFTCAMBTimestepCacheIsNan checks them (l.631, l.637) inside EFTTestStability on a local, non-initialised cache (09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:71).
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** Add self%EFTcdotdot = 0._dl and self%EFTGamma1PP = 0._dl to EFTCAMBTimestepCacheInit (work/t6-stability/fix_nanfix.diff, tested: 3/73 like A0). Overlaps another track's repro/uninit-cache-fields-garbage.py.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; The defect is real and sits in EFTCAMB code, not upstream CAMB. EFTCAMBTimestepCacheInit (fortran/eftcamb/03_abstract_EFTCAMB_cache.f90:402-586) assigns every real(dl) field of TEFTCAMB_timestep_cache except EFTcdotdot and EFTGamma1PP. EFTCAMBTimestepCacheIsNan checks both of them (l.631, l.637). EFTCAMB_Stability_Check declares a local eft_cache with no default initialisation (09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:71). EFTTestStability calls is_nan on that cache (step 2, around l.207). These two fields are written only by compute_additional_derivs (06p1:183,192; 06p2:74,80) and by a few designer models (007p1, 007p2, 007p8). So for the other models the stability verdict depends on whatever was in memory before.

I checked this two ways. First, I ran the repro, which showed the verdict flipping. Second, I compared the list of fields the type declares with the fields the init routine assigns, using a separate script; exactly EFTcdotdot and EFTGamma1PP are missing.

The bug is not new from the rebase. B1 (the pre-rebase tree) also checks both fields in is_nan (l.540, l.546) and never assigns either one in its cache file.

Why S2 and not S1: the release A0 build gives the correct verdict in practice. It goes wrong when memory starts out as NaN: -finit-real=nan builds, ifort -init=snan builds, or unlucky stack contents. In those cases a stable model is silently rejected.

One problem with the test itself: the repro script hard-codes the old repo path /Users/marco/Desktop/IW/..., which no longer exists, so it stops with "include file not found". I fixed only the paths in a copy (fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/checks/verify/repro_cdotdot_pathfix.py) and the copy reproduces the finding. The repro in the repo should get the same path fix.

The proposed fix is right and safe-local: add self%EFTcdotdot = 0._dl and self%EFTGamma1PP = 0._dl to EFTCAMBTimestepCacheInit.

### F-066 [S2, confirmed] EFTStabilityComputation does not reset gpinu_tot in its 2nd/3rd massive-neutrino loops: the stability cache carries 3x the massive-neutrino pressure (Hdotdot/Hdotdotdot wrong)

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:493` (also: fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:522, fortran/eftcamb/07f_designer_models/007p1_Pure_EFT_std.f90:1677,1693)
- **Track:** t6-stability; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/stability-gpinu-tot-tripled.py [tree [fixed_tree]]`
- **Observed:** A0, 2_PEFT_gamma3_const_1 (omnuh2 = 0.01, 1 massive nu): stability-cache gpinu_tot / python-bridge gpinu_tot = 3.0000 at every a. Hdotdot relative error is -6.81e-2 (a=1e-6), -2.09e-2 (1e-3), -5.3e-4 (1e-2); Hdotdotdot -2.3e-2 at 1e-6. Same for 2_PEFT_wDE_wCDM_1 and 2_PEFT_gamma1_const_1. Fixed tree (fixnu): ratio 1.0000 and errors 0. Fixing it changes posbound2 by -1.1e-2 (a=1e-6) and -7.0e-3 (1e-4), and tachpos by 5e-4. EFT_kinetic and EFT_gradient are unchanged.
- **Expected:** gpinu_tot equal to the bridge value (ratio 1); Hdotdot equal to the bridge.
- **Root cause:** Lines 493-496 and 522-525 reset gpinudot_tot twice and never reset gpinu_tot before accumulating gpinu_tot = gpinu_tot + grhormass_t*gpinu. compute_H_derivs of Pure EFT (and mc5e), Horava and 3p1 uses gpinu_tot in Hdotdot/Hdotdotdot, which feed the pi coefficients (mass_math), EFT_mu (mass) and the positivity/Minkowski coefficients. Same in B1.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** Replace the first eft_cache%gpinudot_tot = 0._dl at l.493 and at l.522 with eft_cache%gpinu_tot = 0._dl (work/t6-stability/fix_fixnu.diff, tested). Spectra are unaffected; only the inputs to the decisions change.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; The source confirms the bug. In EFTStabilityComputation (fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90), the first neutrino loop resets gpinu_tot (l.461). The second loop (l.493-496) and the third loop (l.522-526) reset gpinudot_tot twice but never reset gpinu_tot, and each still does gpinu_tot = gpinu_tot + grhormass_t*gpinu. So the first compute_H_derivs call sees 2x the massive-neutrino pressure, and the second call and the stored cache see 3x. Pure EFT compute_H_derivs (007p1_Pure_EFT_std.f90:1677,1693) uses gpinu_tot in Hdotdot and Hdotdotdot. The pre-rebase branch (eftcamb-pre-rebase, l.465/468 and 491/494) has the same pattern, so the bug is inherited and not caused by the rebase. It is EFTCAMB code, not upstream CAMB. An independent finite-difference check shows the python-bridge Hdotdot and Hdotdotdot match numerical derivatives of Hdot and Hdotdot to about 1e-6, while the stability-cache values are off by up to 7%. The stability cache is also inconsistent with itself: its gpresm_t, built in loop 1, implies 1x the massive-neutrino pressure, while its gpinu_tot holds 3x. The fixnu patch makes the stability values equal to the finite-difference ones. S2 is right: the C(l) and P(k) spectra are untouched. Only the inputs to the stability and Minkowski decisions change, at the 1e-2 level and only early (a<1e-3), so a model's accept/reject verdict can change only near a stability boundary. Practical note: the repro script as filed fails at the moment because it hardcodes the old repo path /Users/marco/Desktop/IW/... (the repo is now at /Users/marco/IW/...), so the include file is not found. I ran a copy with only the parameter path (PAR) and the harness import path fixed: fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/checks/verify/gpinu_repro_pathfix.py. The repro script's PAR line should be updated.

### F-067 [S2, confirmed] EFTCAMB_evolve_metric_h=T gives results inconsistent with the constraint and pi-field formulations (1% low-ell TT, x13 P(k) at k=100)

- **Location:** `fortran/equations.f90:3144` (also: fortran/equations.f90:3019-3020 (RSA branch), fortran/equations.f90:3159-3163, fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_IC.f90:290 (y(w_ix+2)=2kz))
- **Track:** t8a-equations; reported severity S2, confidence medium
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/evolve-metric-h-inconsistent.py`
- **Observed:** 5_hdsk_TG_1 with evolve_metric_h=T vs the default h' from the 00 constraint: dTT/TT = -1.12e-2 (l=2), -1.22e-2 (l=5), -8.4e-3 (l=10), +7.4e-4 (l=30); dP/P = -2.3e-3 (k=1), -0.13 (k=10), -0.92 (k=50), +12.3 (k=100 /Mpc). The same model with pi-field equations (5_hdsk_TG_2) agrees with the constraint run to <=5e-5. Unchanged at accuracy_boost=l_accuracy_boost=2 (-1.10e-2 at l=2); B1 (pre-rebase) gives the same (-1.06e-2, k=100: +12.3).
- **Expected:** All three formulations agree to code accuracy (~1e-4).
- **Root cause:** Not fully established. With evolve_metric_h, z=h'/2k comes from the integrated y(w_ix+2) driven by metric_h_ddot (ii equation, 3159-3160, plus 3163), not from the 00 constraint. Starting from the GR value 2kz at turn-on, it drifts off the constraint. On large scales sigma=(h'+6eta')/2k is a near-cancellation, which amplifies the drift into the ISW source; at high k the evolution is unstable. CAMB itself takes z from the constraint 'for numerical stability' (3123). The error does not converge with accuracy, so a missing or inconsistent term in the ii-equation source (e.g. the dp_matter content) is also possible. Pre-existing, not a rebase regression.
- **Proposed fix (unknown):** Until the ii-equation path is validated, always take h' from the 00 constraint (treat EFTCAMB_evolve_metric_h as F, or stop with an error when it is T). Alternatively add constraint damping, or re-derive and check metric_h_ddot against the constraint (d/dtau of the 00 equation).
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I reran the repro script exactly as given and got the reported numbers. The defect is real, it is in EFTCAMB code (not upstream CAMB, not the test), and B1 has it too, so it is not a rebase regression. Severity stays S2: the flag is off by default and in every test ini.

The stated root cause is only partly right. Two separate problems add up to the reported effect, and I separated them with variant runs (setting the cubic coupling Horndeskic30=0, or using only massless neutrinos):

(1) The ~1% low-ell TT error comes from massive neutrinos, not from constraint drift. In MassiveNuVars (fortran/equations.f90:1540-1542, EFTCAMB MOD), the neutrino pressure perturbation is computed as dgp = gpnu_t*clxnu, i.e. delta_p = w*delta_rho. The true delta_p is available from Nu_Integrate_L012, or from y(nu_ix+1) in the fluid approximation. The h'' (ii-equation) source uses this delta_p through dp_matter, while the 00 constraint never uses it, so only evolve_metric_h=T picks up the error. Evidence:
- With massless neutrinos only, T vs F agrees to 2e-6 in TT.
- With the cubic coupling off, the 1% TT error remains.
- In a tree copy patched to use the true delta_p, the TT error drops from 1.1e-2 to 8e-5.
- The same patch also moves the default F path by 2e-5 at l=2, through spim*dp_matter in the delta_phi equation.

(2) The high-k P(k) error (x13 at k=100) is a separate problem. It needs the cubic coupling (with it off, the difference is 3e-4), does not depend on neutrinos (massless-nu runs show the same numbers), and does not change at accuracy_boost=2. So it is a genuine inconsistency or instability of the evolved-h' path with alphaB != 0, i.e. the drift mechanism at equations.f90:3144/3159-3163 that the finding proposes. Its exact term is still not pinned down; candidates are the missing baryon pressure in dp_matter, or an ii-equation coefficient that is inconsistent with the time derivative of the 00 constraint.

Side observations, not verified:
- The RSA branch sets dp_matter = dgpnu without the /a2 that the non-RSA branch applies (equations.f90:3027 vs 3153), yet both multiply by a2 in metric_h_ddot.
- get_scale_evolution outputs for T vs F differ by a k-dependent but a-independent factor (x6.7 at k=1, 37% at k=0.05) while P(k) at k=1 differs by only 2e-3. The evolution-output path with evolve_metric_h=T looks separately broken.

Proposed fix, two parts:
- In MassiveNuVars, compute dgp from the momentum-integrated pressure (Nu_Integrate_L012, or y(nu_ix+1) in the fluid approximation). This is physics-changing for the F path at the 2e-5 level.
- Separately, validate the ii-equation path at high k, or refuse evolve_metric_h=T until it is validated.

### F-068 [S2, confirmed] EFT polterddot replacement drops curvature factor Kf(2): EFTflag=0 non-flat CMB differs from CAMB (also filed by t3)

- **Location:** `fortran/equations.f90:3858` (also: fortran/equations.f90:3860 (E2ddot), fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/t3-polterddot-curvature-gr.py (t3 duplicate))
- **Track:** t8a-equations; reported severity S2, confidence high
