- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/polterddot-nonflat-gr.py`
- **Observed:** EFTflag=0, A0 vs B0 (plain CAMB v2), Reion.max_redshift=50 on both: omk=-0.05 dTT/TT(l=2)=+5.4e-3, dTE/sqrt(TTEE)=+1.7e-3, l=3 -1.9e-4; omk=+0.05 dTT/TT(l=2)=+1.8e-4, l=10 +1.2e-5; EE identical; flat 7.8e-16.
- **Expected:** EFTflag=0 identical to CAMB (~1e-13) for any curvature.
- **Root cause:** pigddot = 2/5 k qgdot - 3/5 k octgdot - ... is applied to every model. It omits the Kf(2) in denlk2(2) that the photon hierarchy (3399) and CAMB's original polterddot (commented at 3863-3868) carry, so it is not the derivative of the evolved pigdot when K != 0. Present in the pre-rebase code too.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** Use the hierarchy coefficients: pigddot = EV%denlk(2)*qgdot - EV%denlk2(2)*octgdot - dopacity*(pig - polter) - opacity*(pigdot - polterdot) + 8/15*k*sigmadot (and the matching E2 term), or restore CAMB's expression when EFTflag==0 .or. .not. EV%EFTCAMBactive.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; Confirmed. I ran the repro exactly as given and got the reported numbers to all digits. The defect is in EFTCAMB's MOD block in fortran/equations.f90 at lines 3858-3861. That block replaces CAMB's polterddot for every model, EFTflag=0 included. Upstream CAMB (branch camb, equations.f90:2883) uses the full Lewis expression, which puts Kf(2) on both the octgdot term and the Edot(3) term. The replacement has Kf(2) on neither. Flat models agree to round-off, so the effect needs K != 0. EE is bit-identical; only TT and TE change, mostly at low ell. The same pigddot line exists in eftcamb-pre-rebase at equations.f90:3596.

Attribution is right but the fix needs a caveat. Both missing Kf(2) factors are needed to match CAMB. Adding Kf(2) only to the octgdot term, the "hierarchy coefficient" in the proposed fix, makes omk=-0.05 slightly worse. The E2 equation CAMB actually evolves (equations.f90:3417, upstream 2565: -k/3*E3) has no Kf(2), so a "matching E2 term" taken from the hierarchy would not restore CAMB. The E2 term has to follow CAMB's original polterddot, with Kf(2)*Edot(3). That mismatch between the evolved E2 equation and polterddot is CAMB's own, so it is not a defect of the code under audit.

Recommended fix: restore CAMB's original polterddot when EFTflag==0 .or. .not. EV%EFTCAMBactive, or put Kf(2) on both terms. An earlier verifier tested the restore-CAMB variant in a tree copy and got round-off agreement with B0 for omk in {0, +-0.05, +-0.1}. For EFTflag=0 that is safe-local. For EFT models with curvature, adding Kf(2) changes physics. Severity S2 stands: wrong only in curved models, at the 1e-4 to 1e-2 level, at low ell.

### F-069 [S2, confirmed] camb/results.py get_matter_power_interpolator(extrap_kmax) EFTCAMB MOD extrapolates P(k>kmax) about 10x less accurately than upstream, for all models including GR

- **Location:** `camb/results.py:1188` (also: camb/results.py:1201 (non-log branch))
- **Track:** t8b-integration; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/pk-extrap-slope-bias.py`
- **Observed:** LCDM z=0, transfer kmax=2/Mpc (kh_max=3.11), fractional error of the extrapolated P vs a direct kmax=100 run at k/kh_max = 1.5, 2, 5, 10: A0 +0.084, +0.224, +1.336, +3.406; upstream B0 +0.015, +0.037, +0.176, +0.350. NonLinear_both (kh_max=7.78): A0 +0.081, +0.220, +1.378, +3.758; B0 +0.033, +0.088, +0.480, +1.103. Non-log (zero-crossing) branch, delta_tot x Weyl at 10 kmax: +3.85.
- **Expected:** At least the upstream accuracy (B0 numbers) for positive spectra; a comparable power-law extrapolation for sign-changing spectra.
- **Root cause:** Both branches use mean(np.diff(logP[:, -10:])) / (logkh[-1]-logkh[-2]). The mean of diffs telescopes to (logP[-1]-logP[-10])/9, i.e. the secant slope over the last 9 intervals. Since the log-slope of P(k) steepens with k, this slope is too shallow (nonlinear case: -1.535 vs end slope -1.918), so P is overestimated beyond kmax. The result is also only valid for a uniform log-k grid. Upstream uses the end slope from the last two nodes.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** Tested in a copy of A0 (diff in work/t8b-integration/out/candidate_fix_pk_extrap.diff). Log branch: dlog = (log_p_new[:, -3] - log_p_new[:, -4]) / (logkh[-1] - logkh[-2]) (upstream). Non-log branch: slope = log(P[:, -1]/P[:, -2]) / dlogk, P_ext = P[:, -1]*exp(slope*(logk - logk[-1])), and raise CAMBValueError if P[:, -1]*P[:, -2] <= 0. After the fix: log branch +0.015, +0.037, +0.176, +0.350 (identical to B0); non-log +0.351 at 10 kmax.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I ran the repro as given and got exactly the reported numbers. I then wrote an independent check that uses a single tree (A0) and one set of P(k) data, then applies both slope formulas to it. That rules out any Fortran-side difference between A0 and B0: the whole bias comes from the Python slope formula in the EFTCAMB MOD block at camb/results.py:1188 (log branch) and 1201 (non-log branch), compared with upstream's end slope from the last two nodes. The defect belongs to the code under audit (an EFTCAMB MOD), not to upstream CAMB or the test.

The stated root cause is right, and in the linear case the effect is worse than described. The last 10 k nodes are not evenly spaced in log k: the spacing is 0.039 at first and 0.223 at the end. Dividing the mean of the diffs by the last spacing therefore gives neither the end slope nor the secant slope: -1.92, against a secant of -2.35, an end slope of -2.47 and a true local slope of -2.49.

Upstream's end slope is itself still shallower than the true slope, which steepens with k. So upstream is not exact either, but EFTCAMB's version is about 10x worse. S2 fits: the error only appears when the user opts into extrap_kmax beyond kmax, and it is silently poor accuracy, not a crash. I did not re-run the non-log zero-crossing number (+3.85); it uses the same mean-of-diffs formula on log|P|.

### F-070 [S2, confirmed] get_background_densities / get_Omega('de') / get_dark_energy_rho_w ignore the EFT background (use the uninitialised w=-1 DarkEnergy placeholder)

- **Location:** `fortran/results.f90:1157` (also: fortran/results.f90:1357 (CAMBdata_DarkEnergyStressEnergy), fortran/results.f90:634 (DarkEnergy%Init skipped for EFT))
- **Track:** t8b-integration; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/eft-background-densities-bypass.py`
- **Observed:** H rebuilt from get_background_densities vs results.hubble_parameter: max|H_dens/H-1| = 5.45e-2 (2_PEFT_wDE_wCDM_1, z=1: Omega_de 0.2298 returned vs 0.3114 implied by H), 2.11e-2 (4_Des_mc5e_CPL_1), 1.28e-2 (5_Kmouflage_1); w_de = -1.000 at every z for every model; GR control 1.1e-16.
- **Expected:** Densities and w consistent with the expansion history actually used (dtauda -> EFT), agreement to round-off as for EFTflag=0.
- **Root cause:** CAMBdata_GetBackgroundDensities (results.f90:1157) and CAMBdata_DarkEnergyStressEnergy (results.f90:1357) call CP%DarkEnergy%BackgroundDensityAndPressure. With EFTflag/=0, SetParams skips CP%DarkEnergy%Init (results.f90 EFT block ~549-636), so DarkEnergy is a default w=-1 fluid unrelated to the EFT model. These routines are the v2 Python API for get_background_densities, get_Omega and get_dark_energy_rho_w.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** In both routines, when CP%EFTCAMB%EFTflag /= 0, use the EFT background: 8piG rho_DE a^4 = 3/dtauda(this,a)**2 - this%grho_no_de(a) (consistent with H by construction), and w from the EFT effective fluid (compute_background_EFT_functions + compute_rhoQPQ: w = gpresq/grhoq, as 11_EFTCAMB_python.f90 get_eft_functions does) or from -1 - dln(rho_DE a^3)/(3 dln a). Alternatively raise an error for EFT models.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I ran the repro script unchanged on A0 and it reproduced the reported numbers exactly. I then checked the same claim a second way, using the EFT timestep cache (get_eft_functions) from the A0 reference runs.

The code under audit has this defect. results.f90:1157 (CAMBdata_GetBackgroundDensities) and results.f90:1357 (CAMBdata_DarkEnergyStressEnergy) call CP%DarkEnergy%BackgroundDensityAndPressure. With EFTflag/=0, the EFT branch of SetParams (results.f90:549-636) never calls DarkEnergy%Init; the only call is at line 634, in the else branch. Their only callers are the Python wrappers in camb/results.py (lines 723 and 751, which serve get_background_densities, get_Omega and get_dark_energy_rho_w). Neither routine has an EFTCAMB MOD guard.

It is not a regression from the rebase. The pre-rebase branch eftcamb-pre-rebase has the same two routines unchanged (results.f90:1068 and 1190 there), so EFTCAMB never routed these API calls to the EFT background.

Severity stays S2, not S1. CMB spectra, P(k), H(z) and distances are unaffected because they go through dtauda and the EFT background. Only these Python diagnostic outputs are wrong, but they are wrong for every EFT model.

The proposed fix is sensible, with one detail: for designer/PEFT models the EFT cache already provides grhov_t and gpiv_t, which agree with grhoq and gpresq.

### F-071 [S2, confirmed] theta->H0 solve (set_cosmology(cosmomc_theta)) aborts for EFT models unstable/unsolvable at the H0 bracket ends; for 5_scg a trial H0 kills Python

- **Location:** `camb/model.py:786` (also: camb/model.py:626-639 (set_H0_for_theta f/brentq), fortran/results.f90:589, fortran/results.f90:617, fortran/eftcamb/08f_full_models/008p6_Scaling_Cubic.f90:274)
- **Track:** t8b-integration; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/theta-solve-aborts-unstable-bracket-end.py`
- **Observed:** Test models at their own parameters, theta from the ini H0=70. 2_PEFT_Omega_power_2 and 3_RPH_KT_OmegaDE_1: f(100) raises 'EFTCAMB: theory unstable' and set_cosmology(cosmomc_theta) raises CAMBError. 5_quint_inverse_power: same with 'EFTCAMB: background solver failed'. With the failing ends moved inwards, brentq recovers H0 = 69.9998 / 70.0007 / 70.0000 (rel <= 9.7e-6). 5_scg: f(41.83) raises CAMBError, and f(H0=95 or 100) hits MpiStop (008p6_Scaling_Cubic.f90:274, 'No positive values of y2_i') -> ERROR STOP, which kills the interpreter.
- **Expected:** set_cosmology(cosmomc_theta=theta(H0_ini)) returns H0_ini to brentq rtol 5e-5 whenever the model is valid there, and never terminates the process.
- **Root cause:** set_H0_for_theta evaluates f(H0) at both bracket ends (EFTCAMB MOD lower end 1.1*100*sqrt(omega_m), upper end 100). For EFT models each trial runs the full EFT background solve and stability check in CAMBdata_SetParams (results.f90:589/617), which raises for parameter points far from the target H0. brentq does not catch the error. Root cause shared with t8c theta-h0-range-eft (which showed the lower end H0=10 with set_H0_for_theta); this shows set_cosmology itself failing at the upper end for 4/73 test models, plus the SCG crash.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** In CAMBparams.set_H0_for_theta, evaluate f at the bracket ends inside try/except CAMBError and move a failing end towards est_H0 by bisection until it evaluates (as in the repro; at most ~40 background calls), then call brentq; re-raise only if no valid bracket with a sign change exists. Replace the MpiStop in 008p6_Scaling_Cubic.f90:274 (and similar background-solver stops) with a global error, so that bad trial points become CAMBError.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I ran the repro exactly as given on A0 and it matches the report. Set_cosmology(cosmomc_theta=theta(H0=70)) raises CAMBError for 2_PEFT_Omega_power_2 and 3_RPH_KT_OmegaDE_1 ('theory unstable') and for 5_quint_inverse_power ('background solver failed'), because f(100) fails at the upper end of the bracket. For 5_scg, H0=100 hits MpiStop and the process dies with ERROR STOP. My independent check did not use the repro's hand-written brentq. It used the public API's own theta_H0_range argument, with theta from a full get_background including thermo. With the upper end at 100 the solve aborts; with theta_H0_range=(10,74) it returns H0=70.0007 / 70.0007 / 70.0005, about 1e-5 relative, which is within brentq rtol 5e-5. So the only cause is trial points at the bracket end that fail validation, not a theta or normalisation mismatch. Scanning H0 shows where each model stops evaluating: PEFT fails from 80, RPH_KT from 95, quint from 80. All three are valid at 70, so a supported model at its own test parameters cannot be inverted from theta. The defect sits in the EFTCAMB-modified path: model.py set_H0_for_theta / set_cosmology (EFTCAMB MOD at line 786 changes only the lower end), the EFT validation run on every trial H0 in SetParams, and MpiStop in 008p6_Scaling_Cubic.f90:274 instead of a global error. Plain upstream CAMB never fails at these trial points. S2 is right: this is a failure in one parameter region (the theta-to-H0 inversion) with a clear workaround (narrow theta_H0_range). The SCG process kill also happens on a direct H0=95 or 100 call without any theta, which makes it a hard crash reachable with plain input. That could arguably be its own S1/S2 finding. I could not use B1 to tell whether this is a regression: B1's read_ini ignores EFTCAMB keys, so my B1 runs of 5_scg were GR.

### F-072 [S2, confirmed] With EFTCAMB_use_background=T, cosmomc_theta uses the exact-R EFT sound horizon instead of CAMB's cosmomc definition (theta shifted by 1e-3)

- **Location:** `fortran/results.f90:1135` (also: fortran/eftcamb/08f_full_models/008p0_Horndeski.f90:2819)
- **Track:** t8b-integration; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/cosmomc-theta-exact-rs-use-background.py`
- **Observed:** 5_hdsk_TG_1: cosmomc_theta = 0.010854335 (use_background=F) vs 0.010843225 (use_background=T), with identical H(z) (H(z=1) 138.7091842971 in both). Relative shift -1.024e-3, 100*Delta theta = -0.00111 (Planck sigma ~0.0003). A quadrature with CAMB's definition gives 0.010854335.
- **Expected:** theta(T)/theta(F) - 1 below 1e-5: the cosmomc_theta definition (dsound_da_approx, R = 3e4 a ombh2) should not depend on which table computes the same background.
- **Root cause:** The CosmomcTheta EFTCAMB MOD replaces Integrate_Romberg(dsound_da_approx) with model%compute_SoundHorizon(astar). Horndeski's r_sound table integrates the exact baryon loading R = 3 rho_b/(4 rho_g) (008p0_Horndeski.f90:2819), the sound_horizon() definition, not the approximate one. The same code was in the pre-rebase version. MCMC runs that sample cosmomc_theta with use_background=T get a different H0 mapping.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** Drop the use_background branch in CAMBdata_CosmomcTheta (results.f90:1135-1139). Integrate_Romberg(this, dsound_da_approx, 1d-8, astar, atol) already uses the EFT expansion through dtauda. Alternatively give the Horndeski model an approximate-R sound-horizon table and use it only here.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I ran the repro exactly as given and got the reported numbers. I then wrote an independent check (fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/checks/verify/cosmomc_theta_exact_rs.py). It does not use a Python quadrature. It uses CAMB's own exact-R sound horizon, results.sound_horizon(z*) = CAMBdata_sound_horizon with dsound_da_exact, computed with use_background=F. Dividing that by D_A gives theta(use_background=T) to 1.05e-6. So the T branch returns the exact-R r_s(z*)/D_A, not the cosmomc dsound_da_approx definition.

The two backgrounds agree. D_A(T)/D_A(F)-1 = 2.5e-8, the exact r_s(T)/r_s(F)-1 = 1.1e-6, and H(z=1) is identical. The 1.02e-3 shift therefore comes entirely from the definition of R, not from the background solver.

The root cause is as stated. results.f90:1134-1140 is an EFTCAMB MOD that calls model%compute_SoundHorizon(astar). Horndeski's table integrates ydot(3) = ydot(1)/sqrt(3(1+0.75 rho_b/rho_g)) at 008p0_Horndeski.f90:2819, which is the exact R. Only Horndeski overrides compute_SoundHorizon; the abstract version (06:1214) only prints that it is unsupported. The else branch, Integrate_Romberg(dsound_da_approx), already follows the EFT H(z), because use_background=F reproduces CAMB's definition exactly (0.010854335 matches the quadrature). The defect is in EFTCAMB's modification of CAMB, not in upstream CAMB or in the test.

The pre-rebase source has the same branch (git show eftcamb-pre-rebase:fortran/results.f90, lines 1044-1050). A B1 runtime comparison is not meaningful, because B1's read_ini ignores EFTCAMB keys (a known fact); B1 gave identical T and F output. I could not refute the claim.

Severity stays S2: use_background is a non-default option that only Horndeski supports (no test ini sets it to T), and the effect is a silent 0.1% bias in theta_MC, about 3.7 Planck sigma in 100*theta. The proposed fix is sound and changes physics.

### F-073 [S2, confirmed] Halofit/HMcode always receive w0=-1, wa=0 for EFT models (only quintessence with EFTCAMB_effective_w0wa=T is different)

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_main.f90:331` (also: fortran/halofit.f90:320-328, fortran/halofit.f90:1132-1140, fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_main.f90:700-704, fortran/eftcamb/08f_full_models/008p4_quintessence.f90:240)
- **Track:** t8c-features; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/nonlinear-halofit-w0wa-lcdm-for-eft.py [TREE]`
- **Observed:** Pure EFT wCDM, w=-0.8, every EFT function zero (2_PEFT_wDE_wCDM_1), against CAMB's fluid with w=-0.8, cs2=1:
- Linear P(k) agree to 1.06e-5.
- P_nl/P_lin differs by -3.29e-2 (takahashi), -7.48e-2 (mead2020) and -5.9e-2 (mead) at z=0, k=4.5/Mpc; -2.5e-2 / -4.3e-2 at z=1.
- get_effective_w0wa() returns (-1, 0) for every test model.
- **Expected:** With no modified gravity, the non-linear ratio should match the CAMB fluid run to ~1e-5.
- **Root cause:** halofit.f90:320-328 and 1132-1140 (EFTCAMB MODs) call EFTCAMB%Effective_w_wa. EFTCAMB_effective_w0wa_values returns (-1, 0) unless EFTCAMB_effective_w0wa (ini default .false.) and model%effective_w0wa are both true (09_EFTCAMB_main.f90:700-704). Only 008p4_quintessence.f90:240 implements it. Designer models hold their w_DE(a) parametrization but never expose it. The same logic exists in B1.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** In EFTCAMB_effective_w0wa_values, return the background's CPL expansion at a=1 when the model does not provide one, instead of (-1, 0). For designer models use the wDE parametrization (PureEFTwDE, DesfRwDE, RPH_wDE, ShiftSymwDE, Designer3p1wDE):
- w0 = wDE%value(1)
- wa = -wDE%first_derivative(1)
For full-mapping models, use a w0/wa fit of grhov_t and gpiv_t at a=1.

Tested for pure EFT only (work/t8c-features/fixNL, select type EFTCAMB_std_pure_EFT): P_nl/P_lin difference falls to 5.5e-6 (takahashi) and 4.5e-6 (mead2020) at z=0.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I ran the repro unchanged on A0 and got exactly the reported numbers. Three independent checks then tied the effect to the hard-coded (-1, 0) that EFTCAMB_effective_w0wa_values (09_EFTCAMB_main.f90:331-337) passes to halofit.f90:320-328 and 1132-1140.
(1) Control at w=-1 for the same pure-EFT designer model against the CAMB fluid: the non-linear ratios agree to 3-4e-6. The pipeline is therefore consistent when the true w matches the (-1, 0) that is passed in.
(2) The size of the mismatch grows with |1+w|: about -1.7e-2 at w=-0.9 and -3.3e-2 at w=-0.8 (takahashi, z=0).
(3) HMcode 2016 ('mead') shows the same effect. get_effective_w0wa() returns (-1.0, 0.0) for the EFT run while the fluid run uses w=-0.8.
In the source, only 008p4_quintessence sets model%effective_w0wa=.true., and the flag is also ANDed with the ini option EFTCAMB_effective_w0wa (default F). Designer wDE models therefore never pass their w(a) on, even when that option is set.
B1 (pre-rebase) has the identical function (B1 09_EFTCAMB_main.f90:258-274) and halofit hooks (B1 halofit.f90:301-309, 1059-1067). The defect is in EFTCAMB code that predates the rebase; it is not upstream CAMB and not a rebase regression.
Severity S2 is appropriate. Linear results and CMB are unaffected. Halofit/HMcode for genuinely modified-gravity models is approximate anyway. Even so, the non-linear output is silently wrong by several per cent in the region where it should be exact (wDE backgrounds without modified gravity). For lensing, the impact is limited to runs with non-linear lensing on.
Two caveats:
- My 'mead' number at z=0 is -4.57e-2, not the reported -5.9e-2. I did not track down the difference; the qualitative claim stands.
- Running the B1 tree through my own script crashed with a segfault (rc=139, likely a wrapper call mismatch in B1). B1's behaviour is confirmed from source only, not from a run.

### F-074 [S2, confirmed] CAMB v2 default set_for_lmax(lmax) silently switches on GR-calibrated non-linear lensing for MG models

- **Location:** `camb/model.py:1091` (also: fortran/results.f90:551-554)
- **Track:** t8c-features; reported severity S2, confidence medium
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/auto-lens-accuracy-enables-nonlinear-for-eft.py`
- **Observed:** 4_Des_F_R_wCDM_1:
- cp.set_for_lmax(2500) gives NonLinear=NonLinear_lens and max_eta_k=72000.
- Lensed BB changes by 34.8%, TT by 1.75% (l=2500) and EE by 0.37% relative to linear lensing.
- Halofit receives w0wa=(-1,0) although the model has w0=-0.9.
- The CAMB 1.x / pre-rebase default call (lens_potential_accuracy=0) keeps NonLinear_none.
- No warning unless EFTCAMB_feedback_level>0.
- **Expected:** For EFTflag/=0, non-linear lensing should stay opt-in, as in the pre-rebase default, or at least raise a visible warning. EFTCAMB states that non-linearity is not worked out for general models.
- **Root cause:** set_for_lmax (camb/model.py:1091-1095): lens_potential_accuracy=None -> max(4,(lmax-1500)/500), and then set_nonlinear_lensing(nonlinear is not False). EFTCAMB has no MOD here. The only safeguard is the feedback-level-gated print at results.f90:551-554. Combined with the w0wa defect above, every default Python run of an MG model gets GR halofit with an LCDM expansion history.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** Add an EFTCAMB MOD in set_for_lmax: if self.EFTCAMB.EFTflag != 0 and nonlinear is None, keep the kmax boost from the automatic lens_potential_accuracy but do not call set_nonlinear_lensing(True), and emit a warnings.warn explaining how to opt in (nonlinear=True). Alternatively, make the results.f90 warning unconditional.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I ran the repro as given and wrote my own check, and both show the effect. Python set_for_lmax(2500) with the default lens_potential_accuracy=None turns on Halofit lensing (NonLinear_lens) for EFT models. The f(R) Halofit correction is 1.5-3.7x larger than in GR. Nothing tells the user unless EFTCAMB_feedback_level > 0.

Attribution: the code at camb/model.py:1063-1068 is upstream CAMB v2, the same as `git show camb:camb/model.py`. EFTCAMB's own defect is a missing adaptation: there is no EFTCAMB MOD in set_for_lmax, and the only guard is the feedback-gated print at results.f90:549-555. Pre-rebase EFTCAMB has `lens_potential_accuracy=0` as the default in set_for_lmax, so the same call stayed linear there. So this is a change in default behaviour that came in with the rebase, not a bug in EFTCAMB code that already existed. Pre-rebase EFTCAMB already let users turn on Halofit for MG models with only a warning, so this is a policy/default issue, not a calculation error.

camb.set_params(lmax=...) is also affected, because it calls cp.set_for_lmax. Ini-driven runs are not affected: base_params.ini has do_nonlinear=0.

The size of the change is mostly the ordinary non-linear lensing correction. The finding's "34.8% BB" should not be read as an EFT error. It is the gap between GR-calibrated Halofit and linear lensing for an MG model. The extra problem for MG is that Halofit gets a w0wa of (-1,0), but that is filed as a separate finding.

S2 is appropriate: the results are silently questionable in a supported default Python workflow. This could also be treated as a design decision. The proposed fix (a warning, and keeping non-linear lensing opt-in when EFTflag!=0) is reasonable.

### F-075 [S2, confirmed] 21cm sources with EFTflag!=0 are refused only by the command-line driver; the Python paths compute them silently with GR source substitutions

- **Location:** `fortran/camb.f90:916` (also: fortran/equations.f90:1801-1830)
- **Track:** t8c-features; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/21cm-not-refused-in-python-with-eft.py`
- **Observed:** camb.read_ini (2_PEFT_Omega_const_1 with a 21cm window, line_basic and line_distortions) plus get_results returns the 21cm C_l without error or warning. W1xW1 at l=10: 3.71e-12, vs 3.22e-11 for the GR control (ratio 0.115). The same ini through CAMB_RunFromIni stops with 'EFTCAMB is not compatible with 21 cm sources so far'.
- **Expected:** The same explicit refusal in every path (a CAMBError in Python).
- **Root cause:** The compatibility check exists only in CAMB_RunFromIni (camb.f90:913-919, and it uses error stop). The 21cm line-of-sight sources (equations.f90:1801-1830, window_21cm branch: line_distortions and line_extra terms) use the GR Einstein-equation substitutions (etak, sigma, dgrho) and have no EFT branch.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** Move the check into the common initialisation, the EFTCAMB MOD block of CAMBdata_SetParams in results.f90 (~line 549):

    if (this%CP%EFTCAMB%EFTflag /= 0 .and. (this%CP%Do21cm .or. any(window_21cm))) call GlobalError('EFTCAMB is not compatible with 21 cm sources so far', error_unsupported_params)

Replace the error stop in camb.f90 with ErrMsg plus return.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; Reproduced as stated. The only EFT-vs-21cm refusal is in CAMB_RunFromIni at fortran/camb.f90:913-919 (`if (P%EFTCAMB%EFTflag /= 0 .and. P%Do21cm)`, then error stop). camb.read_ini plus get_results, and a pure-Python CAMBparams with a 21cm GaussianSourceWindow plus EFTCAMB.initialize_parameters, both skip this check. They return 21cm C_l without any error or warning.

The independent check shows the EFT 21cm path matches GR in the GR limit: Omega0=1e-8 gives a ratio of 0.9995. It moves strongly once EFT is switched on. So the result is computed silently by sources that were never validated for EFT. equations.f90:1801-1830 has no EFT branch for window_21cm. Its line_distortions/line_extra terms are built from GR Einstein-equation combinations of etak, sigma and dgrho, while the counts branch just above it does have an EFTCAMB MOD. The authors' own driver message calls this combination unsupported.

I cannot measure the size of the physics error, because there is no EFT 21cm reference. The defect is a missing refusal on supported API paths for an unsupported combination, so S2 (not S1) is appropriate.

Caveats on the proposed fix:
- `any(window_21cm)` is pseudo-code. CP%Do21cm is only set from the SourceWindows at results.f90:686, which comes after the proposed insertion point at ~549. The check must loop over this%CP%SourceWindows(:)%Window%source_type, or be placed after line 686.
- Through Python, camb.run_ini also hits the error stop, which kills the whole interpreter (subprocess rc=1). That supports replacing error stop with ErrMsg plus return / GlobalError.

The defect is in the EFTCAMB modification (the refusal is in an EFTCAMB MOD block), not in upstream CAMB.

### F-076 [S2, confirmed] omk != 0 is silently accepted with EFTflag != 0, but the EFT perturbation background is flat

- **Location:** `fortran/equations.f90:2786` (also: fortran/equations.f90:2907, fortran/eftcamb/07f_designer_models/007p1_Pure_EFT_std.f90:1632, duplicate root cause: fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/t3-eft-curvature-ignored.py)
- **Track:** t8c-features; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/curvature-silently-wrong-with-eft.py`
- **Observed:** 2_PEFT_wDE_LCDM_1 (exactly LCDM, EFT perturbations never on) vs EFTflag=0:
- omk=0.02: TT(EFT)/TT(GR)-1 = +5.15e1 at l=2, -1.79e-1 at l=10, -7.7e-3 at l=30; sigma8 +5.1e-3.
- omk=0: <=1e-5.
- c6, omk=-0.02: near-GR PEFT l=2 +5.65; f(R) l=2 +5.38.
- B1 (pre-rebase) is identical: l=2 +5.13e1.
- **Expected:** Either refuse omk/=0 for EFT models, or agree with GR to ~1e-4 in the LCDM limit.
- **Root cause:** derivs takes adotoa from model%compute_adotoa (equations.f90 ~2786-2810), e.g. EFTCAMBPureEFTstdComputeAdotoa, which uses sqrt((grhom_t+grhov_t)/3) with no grhok term. cothxor is set to the flat value 1/tau (~2907). No check rejects curvature. This is the same root cause as the t3-gr finding t3-eft-curvature-ignored.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** In the EFTCAMB MOD block of CAMBdata_SetParams (results.f90 ~549), add

    if (this%CP%EFTCAMB%EFTflag /= 0 .and. abs(this%CP%omk) > 0) call GlobalError('EFTCAMB does not support spatial curvature', error_unsupported_params)

Alternatively, add grhok*a^2 to every model's compute_adotoa/Hdot and use the curved cothxor.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; The repro script, run exactly as given on A0, reproduces the stated numbers. An independent harness-based check (fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/checks/verify/curv_verify.py, on trees A0 and B1, overrides omk and num_redshiftwindows=0) gives the same numbers on both trees. It also confirms the root cause directly. With omk=0.02 the EFT timestep cache's adotoa at a=1 is 69.2965 km/s/Mpc, which equals 70*sqrt(1-omk) to all printed digits. So the grhok term is missing from the model's compute_adotoa, while CAMB's own background H(z) is still 70.0. In derivs, the EFTflag/=0 branch of equations.f90 sets cothxor = 1/tau (the flat value) and takes adotoa from the model. compute_dtauda in equations.f90:48/53 also has no grhok. A grep over fortran/eftcamb/ and camb/eftcamb.py finds no omk, flat or curvature check anywhere, so the input is accepted silently. The defect is in EFTCAMB's modifications, not upstream CAMB: upstream CAMB handles curvature in the EFTflag==0 branch. It is not a test artifact either: at omk=0 the same LCDM-limit model matches GR to about 1e-5. B1 (pre-rebase) shows the same defect, so it is long-standing. S2 is appropriate because curvature with EFT is an edge region that EFTCAMB never claimed to support, but the result is silently wrong by O(50x) at l=2. Refusing omk/=0 is a reasonable safe-local fix. Supporting curvature would be physics-changing and would also need the EFT dtauda/Hdot paths updated, not just cothxor.

### F-077 [S2, confirmed] A value Fortran cannot parse in initialize_parameters / set_params hits ERROR STOP and kills the interpreter

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:36` (also: forutils/IniObjects.f90:298-316, camb/eftcamb.py:113-121)
- **Track:** t9-wrapper; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/initialize-parameters-bad-value-kills-python.py [--tree PATH]`
- **Observed:** Each of these exits with rc=1, 'Error for key ...', ERROR STOP and no Python exception: EFT_ghost_stability=np.True_, EFTflag=1.0, EFTOmega0=None, EFTOmega0='0.0l', EFTOmega0=b'0.01', and set_params(EFTwDE=np.float64(0)). Samplers that pass floats or numpy scalars for integer keys kill the whole process.
- **Expected:** A Python exception (ValueError) with the interpreter still alive; numpy bool/int/float and bytes values converted sensibly.
- **Root cause:** The module Ini is a plain type(TIniFile). Its read errors go through TNameValueList_Error -> FailStop -> 'error stop' (forutils/IniObjects.f90:298-316). On the Python side, initialize_parameters (camb/eftcamb.py:113-121) maps only Python bool to T/F and str() everything else: np.True_ becomes 'True', bytes becomes "b'...'", and 1.0 is passed to an integer key.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** See work/t9-wrapper/fix_fortran.diff and fix_python.diff. Add a TPythonIniFile extending TIniFile whose FailStop override sets a 'failed' flag. Use it as the module Ini (this needs type(TIniFile) -> class(TIniFile) in the 26 EFTCAMB files that take an Ini). Set eft_error=2 when it fails. On the Python side, map np.bool_ to T/F and decode bytes, then raise ValueError when temp==2. Tested: 4/6 cases now raise ValueError, 2/6 are converted and accepted, and the interpreter stays alive in all 6.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I ran the repro as given on A0. All 6 cases exit with rc=1, print 'Error for key ...' and then 'ERROR STOP', and no RESULT line ever appears, so the interpreter dies before any Python exception can be raised. My independent check skips initialize_parameters and its str() conversion: it calls EFTCAMB_initialize_read_params and EFTCAMB_initialize directly through ctypes with raw strings. Raw strings 'EFTflag=1.0', 'EFTwDE=0.0', 'EFTOmega0=None' and 'EFT_ghost_stability=True' each give rc=1 and ERROR STOP. The controls 'EFTflag=1' and 'EFT_ghost_stability=T' return err=0. This places the abort in the Fortran side: the module Ini is a plain type(TIniFile) at 11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:36, and its read errors go through TNameValueList_Error -> FailStop -> 'error stop' (forutils/IniObjects.f90:298-316). The Python side only makes it more likely: str() turns np.True_ into 'True' and np.float64(0) into '0.0'. A Python control with np.int64(1), np.float64(0.01) and bool True works (rc=0), because str() gives parseable text for those. The defect is in EFTCAMB's wrapper, not upstream CAMB: upstream read_ini checks ini files in a separate subprocess (validate_ini_file), and this dictionary path has no such check. B1 (pre-rebase) has the same code: line 36 'type(TIniFile), private :: Ini' and the str() conversion at camb/eftcamb.py:124, so the problem predates the rebase. I could not run B1 end to end because its set_cosmology raises a numpy TypeError in this environment. S2 is the right severity: the crash needs input that is malformed or loosely typed, but samplers often pass numpy or float values, and the result is the whole process dying rather than a recoverable error. I did not test the proposed fix.

### F-078 [S2, confirmed] read_parameters returns bytes for strings and for Fortran D-exponent numbers; feeding the dict back garbles strings or kills Python

- **Location:** `camb/eftcamb.py:184`
- **Track:** t9-wrapper; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/read-parameters-roundtrip-bytes.py [--tree PATH]`
- **Observed:** On 2_PEFT_Omega_power_1, read_parameters()['output_root'] is b'eftcamb_test/...' (bytes). After initialize_parameters(read_parameters()), output_root becomes "b'eftcamb_test/...'". On 4_Des_F_R_LCDM_2 (test ini has EFTB0 = 1.d-4), EFTB0 stays b'1.d-4'; initialize_parameters(read_parameters()) gives 'Error for key "EFTB0" : error reading double', ERROR STOP, rc=1. Across all 73 test inis, output_root is bytes in 72 and EFTB0 in 1.
- **Expected:** read_parameters returns str for strings and float 1e-4 for '1.d-4'; initialize_parameters(read_parameters()) reproduces the object (as it does once bytes are decoded: 73/73 bit identical).
- **Root cause:** read_parameters (camb/eftcamb.py:159-205) keeps the raw bytes buffers. boolify/autoconvert try only bool(b'T'/b'F'), int and float. Python float() rejects Fortran 'd' exponents, so those values and every string stay bytes.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** Decode the buffers to str first. autoconvert(s): 'T'/'F' -> bool, then int, then float, then (regex [dD] exponent) float(s.translate({'d':'e','D':'E'})), else str. Optionally add read_parameters(raw=True) returning the strings as read (used by the write_ini and set_params fixes). See fix_python.diff. Tested: both models round-trip, EFTB0 -> 0.0001 (float).
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I ran the repro script unchanged on A0 and it shows the claimed effect. On 2_PEFT_Omega_power_1, read_parameters()['output_root'] is bytes, and after a round trip it becomes the string "b'...'". On 4_Des_F_R_LCDM_2, EFTB0 comes back as b'1.d-4', and feeding it back makes the Fortran ini reader fail with 'error reading double', then ERROR STOP, rc=1. The interpreter dies with no Python exception.

My independent check (v_read_params_bytes.py) confirms the attribution in three ways:
1. Plain Python semantics: float(b'1.d-4') and int(b'1.d-4') both raise ValueError, while float(b'1e-4') = 1e-4 and int(b'5') = 5. So autoconvert leaves every string and every D-exponent number as bytes.
2. Passing str values ('1.d-4', 'abc') straight to initialize_parameters works (rc=0). The Fortran side reads '1.d-4' correctly, so the failure comes from str(bytes) producing "b'...'" in initialize_parameters, which is fed by the bytes that read_parameters returns.
3. Decoding read_parameters' bytes to str before re-feeding gives a faithful round trip with 0 differing keys (31/31 keys on 4_Des_F_R_LCDM_2, 47/47 on 2_PEFT_Omega_power_1).

The defect is in EFTCAMB's Python wrapper camb/eftcamb.py (read_parameters, boolify/autoconvert around lines 184-196). It is not upstream CAMB and not a test artefact. The same logic already existed in the pre-rebase wrapper, so it is long-standing but still part of the code under audit.

Severity S2 stands: the crash needs a specific use of the API (feeding the read_parameters output back), and EFTB0 is written with a D exponent in only 1 of the 73 test inis, but when it happens it silently corrupts strings or hard-kills the interpreter. The proposed fix (decode to str, then bool/int/float, then float after d->e, else str) is consistent with my results.

### F-079 [S2, confirmed] camb.set_params(cp=pars, ...) silently resets the EFT model of pars to GR; an EFT-only update raises after resetting

- **Location:** `camb/camb.py:216` (also: fortran/eftcamb/11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:115-146)
- **Track:** t9-wrapper; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/set-params-cp-drops-eft-model.py [--tree PATH]`
- **Observed:** 2_PEFT_Omega_const_1. Case A: set_params(cp=p, H0=p.H0) -> EFTflag=0, model='' and max|TT/TT_control-1| = 0.733, where the control is read_ini + set_cosmology(H0), i.e. what set_params does to the CAMB part. Case B: set_params(cp=p, EFTOmega0=0.02) raises CAMBUnknownArgumentError('Unrecognized parameter: EFTOmega0') and leaves p with EFTflag=0.
- **Expected:** Case A: EFT model kept, TT identical to the control. Case B: the same model with EFTOmega0 = 0.02.
- **Root cause:** EFT_do_set (camb/camb.py:216-224) always calls cp.EFTCAMB.initialize_parameters(cp, params) with only this call's keywords, unlike do_set, which calls a setter only for its own keys. initialize_EFTCAMB_from_py first resets TEFTCAMB to its defaults, so every EFT setting not repeated is lost. Case B: once EFTflag=0 is read, EFTOmega0 is never read, so it is reported as unknown. Overlaps the t3-gr repro t3-set-params-cp-resets-eft-model.py; the case-B behaviour is new.
- **Proposed fix (physics-changing):** In EFT_do_set: '_eft = cp.EFTCAMB.read_parameters(raw=True) if cp.EFTCAMB.EFTflag != 0 else {}; _eft.update(params); setter(cp, _eft, ...)'. See fix_python.diff. Tested: case A gives EFTflag=1 and maxrel 0.0 vs control; case B gives no exception and values [0.02]. The roundtrip set_params path is unchanged.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I ran the repro as given on A0 and reproduced both cases. I then wrote an independent check that goes through a different path: the EFT model is built only with set_params(**kwargs), with no ini and no read_ini, then set_params(cp=p, H0, ombh2, omch2) is called again with the same values. Afterwards p has EFTflag=0 and its TT is bit-identical to a plain GR run. So the EFT model really is reset to GR; this is not a mismatch in how the control was built.

The root cause is as stated. EFT_do_set (camb/camb.py:216-224) always calls cp.EFTCAMB.initialize_parameters(cp, params) with only this call's keywords. initialize_EFTCAMB_from_py (11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:115-146) first resets TEFTCAMB to its defaults and then reads only those keywords. do_set, by contrast, skips any setter that gets no keywords.

The defect is in EFTCAMB's own changes to camb.py, not in upstream CAMB. It is not new with the rebase: the EFT_do_set code on branch eftcamb-pre-rebase is identical.

Case B is partly in line with upstream style, because upstream setters such as DarkEnergy.set_params also reset the other parameters they own to defaults. Still, raising CAMBUnknownArgumentError after the model has already been changed is a defect.

S2 is the right severity: the API is used validly and the result is silently GR physics, but only for the cp= re-use path.

### F-080 [S2, confirmed] CAMBparams.write_ini writes no EFTCAMB parameters: validation fails for every EFT model and the written ini is GR

- **Location:** `camb/_ini.py:106`
- **Track:** t9-wrapper; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/write-ini-drops-eftcamb.py [--tree PATH]`
- **Observed:** 2_PEFT_Omega_const_1: write_ini(validate=True) raises CAMBValueError 'params.EFTCAMB.EFTflag differs: 0 != 1'. With validate=False the file has 0 EFT keys, re-reads as EFTflag=0, and max|TT_reread/TT_original-1| = 0.665. All 72 EFT test inis: 0/72 validate, 0/72 round trip, 0 of 2994 EFT keys written.
- **Expected:** Every EFT key written; ini -> read -> write -> read reproduces the model (project rule in AGENTS.md: new class parameters must be handled in write_ini).
- **Root cause:** _update_ini_state_from_params (camb/_ini.py:106-190) was not extended for params.EFTCAMB. The model parameters live only in Fortran (read_param_keys/values), not in the ctypes fields.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** At the end of _update_ini_state_from_params: 'if params.EFTCAMB.EFTflag != 0: for k, v in params.EFTCAMB.read_parameters(raw=True).items(): if k != "output_root": state.set(k, v)'. Needs read_parameters(raw=True) from the bytes fix. Tested: 72/72 models validate and round-trip at the read_parameters level, and the repro TT difference is 0.0.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I reproduced the defect and it is in the code under audit. Upstream CAMB v2 added write_ini and _update_ini_state_from_params (camb/_ini.py:106, same line on branch camb). The EFTCAMB patch extended CAMBparams with the EFTCAMB objects (camb/model.py:426-429) but never touched _ini.py: grep finds no EFTCAMB references in it. So no EFT key is serialised. With validate=True, the default, the call fails loudly. With validate=False it silently writes a GR ini that reloads as EFTflag=0. My independent check took a different route: the module-level camb.write_ini over all test inis, then reading the raw written file. It also shows the written ini says dark_energy_model = fluid, not EFTCAMB. So the DarkEnergy write path also loses the EFT selection. The proposed fix must make sure read_ini turns EFT back on from the EFTflag key alone. The author says it does (72/72), but I did not test the fix. S2 is right: this is a wrong-output utility path, not the main computation, and the default validate=True path raises instead of silently corrupting.

### F-081 [S2, confirmed] get_evolution / get_scale_evolution sort the requested times and return rows in sorted order (silently misaligned)

- **Location:** `camb/eftcamb.py:316`
- **Track:** t9-wrapper; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/get-evolution-sort-misalign.py [--tree PATH]`
- **Observed:** get_scale_evolution(results, [0.1], a=1/(1+z)) for z=[0,0.5,1,2,5] returns a=[0.167,...,1.0]: max|a_ret-a_req|/a = 5.0, and adotoa is reversed. get_evolution with eta=[7609.9,13995.2,10682.0] returns tau=[7609.9,10682.0,13995.2], an error of 3313 Mpc. The same was seen in all 3 dbg models (5_hdsk_TG_1 a_req [1e-4,5e-9,...] came back sorted).
- **Expected:** Output rows in the order requested (or documented sorted output).
- **Root cause:** get_evolution does 'times = np.sort(times)' and never maps the results back to the input order.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** order = np.argsort(times, kind='stable'); pass times[order] to Fortran; after unpacking, results = results[:, np.argsort(order, kind='stable')]. Tested: errors 1.3e-9 and 0.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; The defect is in code under audit: camb/eftcamb.py:316 runs `times = np.sort(times)` and never maps the output back to the input order. Pre-rebase EFTCAMB has the same line (eftcamb-pre-rebase:camb/eftcamb.py:316), so it is EFTCAMB's own code, not inherited from upstream CAMB. The sort itself is needed: GetEFTOutputEvolutionFork (11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:363-376) integrates forward through eta(j), so unsorted times would break the integration. The missing step is the inverse permutation afterwards. Neighbouring APIs keep the caller's order: CAMB's results.get_time_evolution does, and EFTCAMB's own get_eft_functions does (it does not sort). get_scale_evolution with the natural a=1/(1+z), z increasing, therefore comes back reversed with no warning. Every returned row is a correct value at some requested time, but rows are assigned to the wrong times. The k order is preserved, so the error is only along the time axis. I kept S2 rather than S1: this is a plotting/diagnostic helper ("should be used only for pretty plots"), sorted ascending input is correct, and power spectra and Cls are unaffected. The proposed argsort plus inverse-permutation fix restores the caller's order. Values match the sorted-input call bit for bit on 2 models. On the other 2, the largest difference is 4.8e-317, which is denormal noise in uninitialised cache fields and unrelated to the fix. Class: safe-local.

### F-082 [S2, confirmed] EFTCAMB_turn_on_time = 0, negative, NaN or >1 silently disables EFT perturbations (GR perturbations on the EFT background)

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_RGR.f90:143` (also: fortran/results.f90:599-604, fortran/equations.f90:2186-2192, fortran/eftcamb/09_EFTCAMB_main.f90:240)
- **Track:** t9-wrapper; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/pert-turn-on-nonpositive-silently-gr.py [TREE]`
- **Observed:** Pure EFT Omega=0.01 const, lmax 1000. a_on = 0, -0.1, NaN and 2.0 all run without error and give max|TT/TT_GR-1| = 4.2e-4 and max|TT/TT_default-1| = 3.04e-2, the same as GR vs default. a_on = 1e-30 raises 'EFTCAMB starts before thermo tauminn'. With EFTCAMB_skip_RGR=T, a_on = 0 and -0.1 also raise that error. The docstring of the sister flag EFTCAMB_back_turn_on presents 0 as 'at all times'.
- **Expected:** An error for a_on <= 0, NaN or > 1 (or 0 meaning 'from the start'), never a silent GR-perturbation result.
- **Root cause:** CAMBdata_SetParams replaces the turn-on with the return-to-GR time (results.f90:599-604). EFTCAMBReturnToGR scans log10(a_on)..0: for a_on <= 0 or NaN the grid is -inf/NaN, so no function exceeds the threshold and RGR_time = 1.1 (after today); for a_on > 1, RGR_time = a_on. The only validation (equations.f90:2186-2192) runs afterwards, on the replaced value.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** Validate in CAMBdata_SetParams before the RGR call, or at read time (09_EFTCAMB_main.f90:240): 'if (.not. (EFTCAMB_pert_turn_on > 0 .and. EFTCAMB_pert_turn_on <= 1)) call GlobalError("EFTCAMB: EFTCAMB_turn_on_time must be in (0,1]", error_unsupported_params)'. In EFTCAMBReturnToGR, return an error when initial_time <= 0 or is not finite. Not built; valid inputs are unaffected.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; The defect is real and sits in EFTCAMB code: an invalid EFTCAMB_turn_on_time (0, negative, NaN or >1) is accepted with no error. EFTCAMBReturnToGR (09_EFTCAMB_RGR.f90:143-171) then overwrites it in results.f90:604, and the only check is the tauminn test in equations.f90:2187. That test runs after the overwrite and cannot catch NaN, because NaN < tauminn is false. The stated root cause is only partly right. For a<=0 or NaN, log10(a_on) is -inf or NaN, so even the first grid point gives log_a_used = -inf + 0*inf = NaN. What happens next depends on the model. Designer f(R) interpolates its functions, so nothing passes the threshold and RGR_time = 1.1, as the finding says. Pure EFT uses analytic Omega(a); const Omega exceeds the threshold at a=NaN, so RGR_time = NaN and pert_turn_on becomes NaN. With a NaN turn-on, the branches at equations.f90:4411/4417 (a < turn_on, a >= turn_on) are both false. The stability checks gated by a > turn_on (09_EFTCAMB_stability.f90:376,384,572) are skipped too. So the result is undefined, not always GR. In the repro configuration (Omega0=0.01) it happens to equal GR perturbations on the EFT background. With 2_PEFT_Omega_const_1.ini (Omega0=1) it matches neither GR nor the default EFT run. That makes the claim 'silently GR perturbations' too narrow: the real outcome is a silently wrong, undefined result. The fix is valid as proposed (validate 0 < a_on <= 1 and finiteness before the RGR call or at read time). It should also check that RGR_time is finite and in range. S2 stands: invalid or edge input gives silently wrong output, and no valid input is affected.

### F-083 [S2, confirmed] get_evolution / get_scale_evolution pi, Psi, mu at k ~ 1/Mpc are dominated by integration error at default accuracy

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:362`
- **Track:** t9-wrapper; reported severity S2, confidence medium
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/get-evolution-pi-highk-tolerance.py [--tree PATH]`
- **Observed:** 2_PEFT_Omega_power_1, k=1.317/Mpc, 400 a values in [0.02,1]. Default vs IntTolBoost=8: max|diff|/max|tight| is 31.9 for pi, 1101 for pidot, 14.1 for Psi and 22.0 for mu. Requesting every 4th time changes the default result by 3.8 (pi) and 2.0 (Psi); the tight result changes by 3.6e-3 and 1.7e-3. Example at a=0.379: Psi default +7.2e-4, tight -5.9e-4. C_L^phiphi (L<=1500) changes by only 7.9e-6. The same O(1) noise appears for 4_Des_F_R_LCDM_1 (pidot 3.1) and 5_hdsk_TG_1 (pidot 1.9) against the debug source dump.
- **Expected:** get_evolution output that is converged and does not depend on the requested sampling at the documented accuracy.
- **Root cause:** GetEFTOutputEvolutionFork integrates with CAMB's source tolerance tol1 = base_tol/exp(AccuracyBoost*IntTolBoost-1) and restarts step control at each output stop. The step-error norm is set by the large components of y, so the small, fast-oscillating pi (sound speed ~c, k*tau ~1e4) is under-resolved. CMB/lensing observables are insensitive to it.
- **Proposed fix (unknown):** In GetEFTOutputEvolutionFork, use a tighter tolerance than the source integration (IntTolBoost=8 converged to 4e-3 here), e.g. tol1/1e3, or a user 'tol'/'accuracy' argument. At minimum, document in get_evolution's docstring that pi-sector outputs at k >~ 0.5/Mpc need IntTolBoost >~ 8. This only affects get_evolution outputs.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; I ran the repro as given on frozen A0 under harness slots. It reproduces every quoted number exactly. I then wrote an independent check, fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/checks/verify/evol_highk_indep.py. It steps through IntTolBoost 1, 2, 4, 6 and 8, and separately sets lAccuracyBoost=4. It also compares against CAMB's own results.get_time_evolution path, which goes through upstream GetOutputEvolutionFork rather than the EFTCAMB fork.

- **Convergence:** the EFTCAMB pi/Psi/Phi error falls steadily as the tolerance tightens (31.9, 11.7, 1.59, 0.23 relative to IntTolBoost=8). That is integration error, not physics or a script bug.
- **Hierarchy truncation ruled out:** raising lAccuracyBoost to 4 does not help (32.0), so the hierarchy cut-off is not the cause.
- **Sampling dependence:** the default result changes with the choice of output stops, by 3.8 for pi. At IntTolBoost=8 the change is 3.6e-3.
- **GR comparison:** the upstream-path Weyl potential is only mildly affected (3.7e-3 at default). With EFTflag=0 it is 3.7e-6. So the stiff EFT pi sector is what degrades accuracy, and the large-component-dominated error norm explains why Weyl and Cls are barely touched.

**Root cause:** the tolerance at 11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:362 is correct and copied verbatim from upstream (cmbmain.f90:1138 and GetTransfer). Upstream CAMB has no fast-oscillating pi field, so the defect is EFT-specific and in the code under audit. One minor point: "restarts step control at each output stop" is overstated. ind and rk_settings carry over between stops, and the sampling dependence is the ordinary effect of hitting exact endpoints with a loose tolerance.

**Proposed fix:** IntTolBoost=16 aborts with RungeKuttaDP45 error -3 (tol too small). A blanket tol1/1e3 could therefore break the run, so the fix needs a bounded tightening or a user-facing accuracy argument. Documenting the limitation is also an option.

**Severity:** S2 stands. The get_evolution docstring says it is "only for pretty plots", but O(10-30) relative errors and dependence on sampling are worse than plot quality.

**Side observation:** the GR (EFTflag=0) run of my check script segfaulted (rc=139) after printing all its results. This is unrelated to this finding and was not investigated.

### F-084 [S2, confirmed] `import camb` fails for ifort/flang libraries: wrong symbol name for the bind(c) init routine

- **Location:** `camb/eftcamb.py:23` (also: fortran/eftcamb/11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:66)
- **Track:** t9-wrapper; reported severity S2, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/eftcamb-import-fails-non-gfortran.py [TREE]`
- **Observed:** Exported symbol 'initialize_EFTCAMB_from_py_read_params_': True; without the underscore: False. Re-executing camb/eftcamb.py with baseconfig.gfortran=False (the branch baseconfig takes for an ifort/flang library) raises AttributeError: dlsym(..., initialize_EFTCAMB_from_py_read_params): symbol not found. camb/model.py:29 imports camb.eftcamb, so `import camb` aborts.
- **Expected:** camb imports with every compiler that CAMB v2 detects (gfortran, ifort, flang; AGENTS.md lists ifort as supported).
- **Root cause:** 11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:66-67 fixes bind(c, name='initialize_EFTCAMB_from_py_read_params_') for all compilers, but camb/eftcamb.py:23-27 looks up the name without the trailing underscore when not gfortran. The v2 name mangler remaps only '__module_MOD_name' names.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** Always use camblib.initialize_EFTCAMB_from_py_read_params_ (drop the if/else), or rename the bind(c) symbol to one name and use it unconditionally.
- **Verifier (reproduce):** CONFIRMED, severity S2; The finding holds and the stated root cause is correct. fortran/eftcamb/11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:66-67 gives the routine the fixed binding label 'initialize_EFTCAMB_from_py_read_params_' for every compiler. Under the Fortran 2003 rules a bind(c, name=...) label is exported exactly as written. camb/eftcamb.py:23-27 looks up the name without the trailing underscore whenever baseconfig.gfortran is False, and CAMB v2's baseconfig sets it False for ifort and flang libraries. MultiCompilerLoader.__getitem__ (baseconfig.py:72-76) only remaps names that contain '_MOD_', so this bind(c) name is looked up as written and is not found. camb/model.py:29 imports camb.eftcamb, so `import camb` itself fails. The defect is in EFTCAMB's own code, not upstream CAMB: plain CAMB v2 (tree B0) imports fine under the same simulation. The bug also predates the rebase: B1 has the same bind(c) label and the same if/else in camb/eftcamb.py. S2 is the right severity because it only bites non-gfortran builds, which CAMB supports but which are not the default toolchain. The proposed fix is fine and safe-local: always call camblib.initialize_EFTCAMB_from_py_read_params_ and drop the if/else.

### F-085 [S3, confirmed by orchestrator (reproduced)] Python calls character(len=200) getters without gfortran's hidden length argument

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:248`
- **Track:** orchestrator; reported severity S3, confidence high
- **Observed:** -fcheck build: 'Actual string length is shorter than the declared one for dummy argument model_name (127/200)' on every EFTCAMB.model_name() call; same for get_read_parameters, param names/labels, set_model_name.
- **Expected:** Explicit-length interface (bind(c) with a length argument, or the length passed from ctypes).
- **Root cause:** ctypes passes only the buffer pointer; gfortran reads the hidden length from a garbage register. Release builds assume len=200 and work only because Python allocates exactly 200 bytes.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** bind(c) wrappers taking (buffer, length) and copying with explicit length; or pass c_long(200) as the trailing hidden argument from camb/eftcamb.py.

### F-086 [S3, confirmed by orchestrator (reproduced)] upstream: size() of an unallocated array in ThermoData init (non-short-circuit .and.)

- **Location:** `fortran/results.f90:2168`
- **Track:** orchestrator; reported severity S3, confidence high
- **Observed:** -fcheck build aborts every run: 'Allocatable argument this is not allocated' at `if (allocated(this%tb) .and. this%nthermo /= size(this%tb))`.
- **Expected:** Nested if; Fortran does not guarantee short-circuit evaluation of .and.
- **Root cause:** Upstream CAMB code (same line in branch camb, line 2035).
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** if (allocated(this%tb)) then; if (this%nthermo /= size(this%tb)) then ... (report upstream).

### F-087 [S3, unverified] Python-Fortran call hygiene in camb/eftcamb.py: c_bool passed for logical(4), immutable bytes used as output buffers, bytes values from read_parameters, type punning in initialize_EFTCAMB_from_py, unchecked global errors, dead binding

- **Location:** `camb/eftcamb.py:211` (also: camb/eftcamb.py:165-178 (read_parameters buffers), camb/eftcamb.py:184-199 (autoconvert on bytes), camb/eftcamb.py:228,243,267,277 (name buffers), camb/eftcamb.py:44 (unused EFTCAMB_check_stability), camb/eftcamb.py:320,351 (no global error check), fortran/eftcamb/11_EFTCAMB_python.f90:102-107 (State declared CAMBdata))
- **Track:** t0-layout; reported severity S3, confidence high
- **Observed:** (1) feedback passes byref(c_bool(...)), 1 byte, to a default logical(4) dummy. Fortran reads 4 bytes: a c_bool(False) followed by non-zero bytes was read as .true., and the model parameters were printed (EFTOmega0 line present). Normal calls work only because a fresh ctypes object is zero padded. (2) read_parameters, param_names, param_labels and model_name pass Python bytes objects (immutable) that Fortran overwrites. (3) read_parameters returns bytes for string values (output_root -> b'f'). Feeding them back to initialize_parameters gives str(b'f') = "b'f'", and eftcamb_tree.check_params comparisons of string params always fail. (4) initialize_EFTCAMB_from_py declares type(CAMBdata) :: State but receives a CAMBparams. It works only because CAMBdata%CP is at offset 0 (verified). (5) get_evolution and get_eft_functions never call config.check_global_error, so failed modes come back as zero-filled rows. (6) EFTCAMB_check_stability is bound but never used.
- **Expected:** Logical arguments passed as c_int (CAMB's convention, like BoolField). Writable buffers from create_string_buffer. str values from read_parameters. Correctly typed dummies. Global errors raised as CAMBError.
- **Root cause:** The wrapper does not follow CAMB baseconfig conventions for function arguments; it only follows them for structure fields.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** (1) Use byref(c_int(1 if print_params else 0)). (2) Use buf = create_string_buffer(200) and pass it with the hidden length (see F-001). (3) Decode bytes in read_parameters: _values[ind] = autoconvert(_values[ind].decode().strip()). (4) Declare 'type(CAMBparams) :: P' in initialize_EFTCAMB_from_py and use P%EFTCAMB / P%eft_par_cache. (5) Call config.check_global_error('get_evolution') after the Fortran call. (6) Remove EFTCAMB_check_stability or wrap it properly.

### F-088 [S3, unverified] TEFTCAMB_parameter_cache%h0 is documented as H0/100 but holds km/s/Mpc; Horndeski overwrites it using a hard-coded 1/c

- **Location:** `fortran/eftcamb/03_abstract_EFTCAMB_cache.f90:82` (also: fortran/results.f90:568, fortran/eftcamb/08f_full_models/008p0_Horndeski.f90:2036-2037)
- **Track:** t0-layout; reported severity S3, confidence high
- **Observed:** Python reads pc.h0 = 70.0 for H0=70 (results.f90:568 stores CP%H0). For 5_hdsk_TG_1, Horndeski sets h0_Mpc to its own background H(a=1) and h0 = h0_Mpc/3.33564d-6 = 85.53729890661, while hubble_parameter(0) = 85.53727449453 (2.85e-7 rel, from the hard-coded constant). h0 is not used in any physics.
- **Expected:** Documentation matching the value (km/s/Mpc), and h0 computed with CAMB's c from constants.
- **Root cause:** The comment does not match the assignment. 008p0_Horndeski.f90:2037 uses a literal instead of c.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** Change the doc to 'Hubble constant in km/s/Mpc'. In 008p0_Horndeski.f90:2037, use params_cache%h0 = params_cache%h0_Mpc*c/1000._dl (use constants, only: c).

### F-089 [S3, unverified] CAMBparams.write_ini does not write EFTCAMB parameters (validate=False silently writes a GR ini)

- **Location:** `camb/_ini.py:249`
- **Track:** t0-layout; reported severity S3, confidence high
- **Observed:** For designer f(R), cp.write_ini(path) raises CAMBValueError 'params.EFTCAMB.EFTflag differs: 0 != 3'. cp.write_ini(path, validate=False) writes no EFT keys, and read_ini of that file gives EFTflag=0.
- **Expected:** EFTCAMB flags and the stored model read parameters are written, so the ini round trips. AGENTS.md asks for the write_ini logic to be updated when parameters are added.
- **Root cause:** _update_ini_state_from_params has no EFTCAMB section.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** In _update_ini_state_from_params, when params.EFTCAMB.EFTflag != 0, write every key/value from params.EFTCAMB.read_parameters(), with bytes decoded and booleans as T/F. Then override with the current python flag fields, using the ini key names: feedback_level for EFTCAMB_feedback_level, EFTCAMB_turn_on_time for EFTCAMB_pert_turn_on.

### F-090 [S3, unverified] SetParams checks DarkEnergy allocation against the previous parameters' EFTflag (this%CP) instead of the new P

- **Location:** `fortran/results.f90:365`
- **Track:** t0-layout; reported severity S3, confidence medium
- **Observed:** Code reading: 'if (.not. allocated(P%DarkEnergy) .and. this%CP%EFTCAMB%EFTflag == 0)' is evaluated before 'this%CP = P' (line 390), so it uses the EFTflag of the previous call. If a CAMBdata last used with EFT gets GR parameters whose DarkEnergy is unallocated, the error is skipped and this%CP%DarkEnergy%Init is reached unallocated. In the opposite case the error is raised spuriously. It also dereferences this%CP%EFTCAMB without an allocated() check. Not executed.
- **Expected:** Test P%EFTCAMB%EFTflag, guarded by allocated(P%EFTCAMB).
- **Root cause:** Wrong object referenced in the EFTCAMB MOD block.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** Replace with: if (.not. allocated(P%DarkEnergy)) then; if (.not. allocated(P%EFTCAMB)) then; call GlobalError('DarkEnergy not set', error_darkenergy); else if (P%EFTCAMB%EFTflag == 0) then; call GlobalError(...); end if; end if.

### F-091 [S3, unverified] EV%EFTCAMB_printing / EV%EFTCAMB_QS are garbage in OpenMP PRIVATE copies, so random k modes run derivs in printing mode

- **Location:** `fortran/cmbmain.f90:250` (also: fortran/cmbmain.f90:1274, fortran/equations.f90:195-196, fortran/equations.f90:3628-3638 (printing branches))
- **Track:** t1-crash; reported severity S3, confidence high
- **Reproduce:** `/Users/marco/Software/miniforge3/bin/python /Users/marco/IW/eftcamb_update/EFTCAMB/fortran/eftcamb_test/audit/repro/omp-private-ev-printing-uninit.py`
- **Observed:** gfortran demo: PRIVATE copy of a component with default init .false. prints T in all 4 iterations; release probe (relP): 158 of 992 k modes of 2_PEFT_Omega_const_1 enter DoSourcek with EFTCAMB_printing = .true.; resetting the flags gives bit-identical Cls and no measurable CPU change (A0 43.3 s vs fixed 50.9 s, noise). In checked builds this printing mode produced 21 of 85 traps (compute_mass_factors 06_abstract_EFTCAMB_model.f90:872 x17 and equations.f90:3633 x4 on never-computed EFTcdotdot/EFTGamma*PP) and the QS mu/Sigma 0/0 at 06_abstract:954. EFTCAMB_QS is never read anywhere (dead flag).
- **Expected:** Flags .false. in every k mode outside the python evolution fork.
- **Root cause:** equations.f90:195-196 rely on default initialisation, but !$OMP PARALLEL DO ... PRIVATE(EV) (cmbmain.f90:250 sources, 1274 transfers) creates uninitialised private copies and nothing in DoSourcek/GetTransfer/initial() resets these flags.
- **Proposed fix (safe-local):** In equations.f90 subroutine initial(), next to nullify(EV%CustomSources) (line 2142):
+    EV%EFTCAMB_printing = .false.
+    EV%EFTCAMB_QS = .false.
(GetEFTOutputEvolutionFork sets printing after initial, so the python evolution path is unaffected); remove the unused EFTCAMB_QS component. Equivalent reset in DoSourcek was tested (relQ, relS): no output change on all 73 models.

### F-092 [S3, unverified] derivst (tensor equations) reads the locals grhog_t, grhor_t before they are assigned in the EFTCAMB block

Release effect of the fix on 8 full-mapping models: worst Cl 2.5e-15..5.3e-15 (noise floor), tensor BB 0. Also flagged by -Wmaybe-uninitialized.

+            EV%eft_cache%gpresdotdotm_t = -(4._dl/3._dl)*EV%eft_cache%Hdot*(EV%eft_cache%grhog_t + EV%eft_cache%grhor_t) + &
+                (16._dl/3._dl)*(EV%eft_cache%grhog_t + EV%eft_cache%grhor_t)*adotoa**2 + EV%eft_cache%gpinudotdot_tot
